# v0.1 review: the fix plan

- **Date:** 2026-11-04
- **Branch:** `v0.1-schema`, at `a0465b7`
- **Findings:** `design/2026-11-03-v0.1-review-findings.md` (163 findings, F001-F163)
- **Status:** every decision taken by Tom on 2026-11-04 (the entries and the decisions table carry them); 58 fixes implemented on 2026-11-04 and 2026-11-05, and X50 and X51 waiting for the when RFC (see Progress)
- **Method:** every finding's cause, fix and route were extracted; two independent groupings (by cause and by the edit that resolves them) were reconciled into fixes; each fix was written against its findings and the sources, then reviewed adversarially (does it resolve each finding it claims, is its route right, does it hide a decision) and revised where the review asked; findings left unresolved were swept into further fixes.

**Decisions already made** (Tom, 2026-11-04), which the plan applies:

1. `astra.yaml` needn't be Windows-compatible. Paths are written as git writes them in a commit (tidy-up change 27), and a tool on Windows maps them, as git does.
2. Paths are case-sensitive, which protects everyone: a path names the file git holds at exactly that path, case (and Unicode form) included, on every machine.
3. Git's limits apply: no ASTRA rule bounds the length of ids, formats or paths.

The tidy-up RFC is cited by the change numbers of its current draft, `NNNN-settling-the-schema-for-v0.1.md` (29 changes); the findings cite the earlier draft's numbers.

## Summary

The 163 findings are grouped into 59 fixes, and astra-tools' design adds a 60th (X60): 32 just do, 19 tidy-up RFC, 2 when RFC and 7 checker and runner phase. Tom took every decision on 2026-11-04, the later ones in conversation with astra-tools' design; they are written into the entries they change.

The big themes are:
- **Spec text has drifted from RFCs 0004, 0005 and 0007.** This covers driving, runner rules, records, bytes and stand-ins.
- **The test harness and tooling don't check everything they claim to.** This covers the invalid-fixture test, the generator, the error map, the §9 extractor and the YAML loader.
- **The parse layer and the reading of git are underspecified.** This covers YAML 1.2, JSON forms, paths, symlinks and submodules.
- **Some check-phase questions are left open.** These are planned results, staleness, bytes and versions.

Two findings are only partly resolved: F061, by X16 and X47 together, and F140, whose .inf and .nan cases go to X31.

**Progress.** 58 of the 60 fixes are implemented on `v0.1-schema`, one commit each, and `just test` passes (1723 passed, 1 skipped), with `just check-ajv` compiling the three JSON Schemas under strict mode. 20 just do fixes landed on 2026-11-04 (`c23c395` to `680ed8b`). The other 38 landed in one run on 2026-11-04 and 2026-11-05 (`4b5f8e6` to `7d29af5`), each entry marked **Done** with its commit. In that run, the commits that touch more than the specification and the design were reviewed as they landed. The text-only ones (X48, X43, X07, X53) and X56 were reviewed afterwards. From X55 on, every commit was reviewed for scope and for truth, and amended where a problem was real. Those reviews added three follow-up commits: X43 (`bf0c5dc`), X07 (`41a90e6`) and X53 (`6d26cc1`). For the seven checker and runner phase fixes, only the part the entry does now is done: design §12's notes, X55's and X56's wording, and X59's version rules. The rest waits for that phase. For the tidy-up RFC fixes, the specification's side is done; their RFC text is the tidy-up draft's (36 changes). X50 and X51 wait for the when RFC (#93). Next: a focused second review (the validator, check and runner layers, and how the fixes interact) before a 0.1.0 pre-release.

A finding resolved by more than one fix counts once per route and once in all. "Tidy-up RFC (spec only)" in the table below marks the fixes that, under the rule proposed in "For the tidy-up RFC", need no RFC text.

### Decisions for Tom

Tom decided every point on 2026-11-04 (last column). The entries carry the decisions. Later decisions the same day (planned results and names, one spelling per entry, versions, placeholders, `modified`, runners' worktrees, submodules, the raw blob, parse strictness) are written into the entries they change: X02, X04, X28, X30-X33, X38, X39, X45, X52, X54, X56-X60.

| Fix | Question | Recommendation | Tom (2026-11-04) |
|---|---|---|---|
| X02 | Should STALE_INPUT be limited to results of an analysis from a commit (or one reached from it), as RFC 0007 §4-5 says, or should the wider rule stay as a new tidy-up change? | (a) Limit it as the RFC says: runners stay simpler, and local staleness still shows through STALE_RESULT. | Agreed |
| X04 | How should aliased entries be handled, that is, one element named by two addresses in one list (Output.inputs, decisions or when; Decision.when; Option.requires or incompatible_with)? | (a) Record them as a design §13 open point beside 'Agreeing spellings' for now, rather than adding a rule through the tidy-up RFC. | Agreed |
| X07 | How should §8's sentence that editing a finding leaves a result unchanged cover an input that names an astra.yaml? | (a) Qualify it with 'unless it changes something listed above (an astra.yaml named as an input, or an output whose results it reads)'. | Agreed, (a): an `astra.yaml` read as an input is an ordinary data file; qualify the sentence and handle nothing differently |
| X16 | Should the record's JSON-LD context be post-processed so that no term is a prefix, and should Environment.iri's pattern check RFC 3987's characters or its full grammar? | Use context (a), post-processed so no term is a prefix (validity unchanged), and pattern (i): RFC 3987's characters with bidi controls excluded, and the structural gap recorded in design §13. | Agreed |
| X17 | Should the url pattern's one-letter-host guard, the §7 drive-path sentence, C:/repo in the design §4 and §7 non-url lists, and Analysis-180 be dropped (Tom's decision 4)? | (a) Drop all of them, so that C:/repo is an scp-like remote at host C and fails as REMOTE_UNREACHABLE, which is how change 25 already reads. | Agreed |
| X18 | What should Output.decisions list? | (a) Only the decisions the output is made with directly, so that no list silently consumes another analysis's decision or lifts NO_UNIVERSES. | Agreed |
| X30 | Should each JSON Schema's `$id` carry a version, in what form, and what does a build between releases carry? | (A) Versioned ids in the X.Y.Z form, carrying `dev` unless HEAD is at a tag; `just release` adds a `dev` regeneration commit after the tag. | Agreed |
| X31 | Which codes and limits apply to values with no JSON form: non-string keys, non-finite or overflowing numbers, and lone surrogates? | (1a) A new NON_STRING_KEY code that wins over every other code for that key, with INVALID_SYNTAX for non-finite or overflowing numbers and for lone surrogates; (2a) reject such numbers at parse and give integer fields a maximum of 2^53-1; (3a) reject lone surrogates at parse. | Agreed |
| X32 | Which encoding, characters and tabs may an astra.yaml's bytes hold? | UTF-8 only. One context-free pre-scan rejects raw controls (except LF and CRLF's CR), DEL, C1, NEL, LS, PS, U+FFFE and U+FFFF, and U+FEFF past the first character. Raw tabs are forbidden. | Agreed |
| X33 | How is git read: what counts as tracked, which commit COMMIT_NOT_ANCESTOR compares against in the working tree, and how gitlinks and symlinks are treated? | Tracked means in the index, or in that commit's tree. In the working tree, COMMIT_NOT_ANCESTOR uses HEAD or MERGE_HEAD. Gitlinks are followed for declarations everywhere. Only X46's stand-ins are accepted as symlinked inputs; any other is MISSING_INPUT_FILE. A symlink on a declaration's path is not followed and fails as MISSING_DECLARED_ANALYSIS. | Agreed |
| X34 | How are selections merged, on what is driving judged, which declaration decides pins and unpinned reads, and in which frame does a pin resolve? | Merge selections by decision; judge driving on the effective selection, per chain; let the reference's last declaration decide; resolve a pin in the frame of the file it is written in. | Agreed |
| X35 | Should decision 1 (astra.yaml need not be Windows-compatible) be stated in spec §3 as well as in tidy-up change 27? | Yes, in one or two sentences in §3, because readers of the specification don't see the RFC. | Agreed |
| X36 | Which tags may an astra.yaml use, may it use anchors and aliases, and how many documents may it hold? | Only core-schema tags, judged once resolved (with `!` read as YAML reads it); no anchors or aliases; at most one document, so an empty file is WRONG_TYPE. | Agreed |
| X37 | May a record start with a BOM, and what code does a record that breaks a parse or schema rule get? | Reject a BOM in a record, and choose this together with X32. A broken record leaves its result `missing` as INVALID_RECORD, with the underlying parse or schema code given as the reason. | Agreed |
| X38 | How should cascades be handled: rules whose subject is undetermined, a missing or malformed spec_version, an invalid analysis from a commit, and PARTIALLY_DRIVEN when a universe has no effective selection? | Don't check a rule whose subject is undetermined. Validate a file with a missing or malformed spec_version against the validator's own schema and report every error. Report DECLARED_ANALYSIS_INVALID plus the inner codes. Judge PARTIALLY_DRIVEN over the driver's other universes. | Agreed: report every error, but not the ones that follow from another |
| X39 | What does a location selector's value mean, what pattern checks it, and how do a quote and a location combine? | The value is a fragment in the cited media type's syntax. The pattern is the exact character check, reported as INVALID_VALUE. The quote is sought within the location (oa:refinedBy). | Changed: option 1, W3C's `conformsTo`, optional; absent means PDF (RFC 8118) |
| X40 | How are the places a runner owns kept apart from outputs, inputs and declarations? | Two new MUST rules: CLASHING_RESULT_NAME (an output's id may not match a declaration's) and RESERVED_PATH (input and declaration paths stay out of all of results/ and .astra/). Both apply to every declaration; a container file is not reserved, and an input may name an astra.yaml. | Agreed |
| X41 | Which character classes do excluded and url take, and may a url contain an ASCII space? | (A) Keep [^\x00-\x20\x7f] on excluded and url and record what it admits; record that a url holds no ASCII space, with advice to write it as %20. | Agreed |
| X42 | Which DOI language does the doi pattern accept? | (A) DOIs in the 10 directory with a dot-divided registrant code and a suffix with no controls and no space at either end. ShortDOIs are rejected as aliases, with the reason recorded in the RFC. | Agreed |
| X43 | Which universes of the stack does a validator check? | (C) The selection rules in every universe of the stack, and the rules about the universe being built only in the universes that are built. | Agreed |
| X44 | Which optional lists must have at least one item? | (B) Every optional list of strings in astra.yaml, as change 24 names them, so that leaving the field out is the only way to write 'none'. | Agreed |
| X45 | Does getting a remote's commit count toward an analysis from a commit's fixed validity, and does a kept copy count as fetched? | (1a) Leave it out: report one REMOTE_UNREACHABLE on the outermost editable declaration, and let the rest of the result be remembered. (2a) A kept copy counts as fetched. | Agreed |
| X46 | Which stand-in forms does a validator recognise, and how does a `<path>.dvc` file count? | (A) A git-annex symlink or pointer file, a Git LFS v1 pointer, and `<path>.dvc` for an input. A `.dvc` file counts by its name once it is tracked. | Changed: the specification says nothing about stand-ins. How bytes are stored is the runner's, which the record names; a validator may recognise a stand-in |
| X47 | Is the `iri` pattern the whole rule for an environment IRI, or must checkers also parse it as RFC 3987? | (a) Record the narrowing so that the pattern is the whole rule and a matching record is valid, together with X16's pattern (i) and context (a). | Agreed |
| X49 | What makes a comment look like a pin for PIN_LIKE_COMMENT? | (b) A `#` immediately followed by an id, with only spaces and tabs after it, checked only on entries that resolve to an output, Evidence.artifact included. | Changed: drop `PIN_LIKE_COMMENT` (RFC 0003 only recommends it), so validators need no YAML comments |
| X50 | Does a decision of an analysis not built in a universe exist where an option is selected, and where is UNDRIVEN_DECLARATION checked? | (b) It doesn't exist there, as the draft is written. (i) Check UNDRIVEN_DECLARATION only where a use counts, which makes F048's r1 and r2 valid. | Agreed |
| X51 | When a `when` condition switches decisions off, over which universes is PARTIALLY_DRIVEN counted, and what does change 30's no-decisions clause cover? | (A) Count only the universes where a decision reached through the chain is active; the others take the chain's status. (iii) The clause covers an analysis with no decisions and no universes of its own. | Agreed; the no-decisions clause, part 2, moves to X34 |
| X52 | For the check phase: what are a root's planned results, how does each result get one name, and what status does unpinned per-universe evidence get? | The planned results are every active output of the root and of each analysis it drives, plus the read or cited results of consumed analyses, transitively. Name ties are broken by byte order, and a record's address must be that name. Unpinned evidence gets the worst status of the output's planned results, or `missing` if there are none. | Agreed |
| X53 | For STALE_RESULT: which option counts, does a missing read result make the reader stale, and is a local result judged against the working tree or HEAD? | The selected option's id counts; a missing read result makes the reader stale; a local result is judged against the working tree. | Agreed |
| X54 | Which files make up a commit, which bytes do the hash and size cover, who reports a .gitattributes breach, and what does a checker without the bytes report? | All (a): the commit's tree, with submodules reached as in X33; the checkout under the commit's .gitattributes with core.autocrlf=false and core.eol=lf; in the working tree, the blob git would stage, with the runner refusing otherwise; a runner warning for a .gitattributes breach; and a new `unknown` status when the bytes are missing. | Agreed |
| X55 | In the check phase, what happens when a record's commit leaves history, and what does a runner on a git-annex adjusted branch record? | (a) A MUST rule, RECORD_COMMIT_NOT_ANCESTOR, with status `missing`. On an adjusted branch the runner records the adjusted branch's commit and refuses when commits are unsynced. | Agreed for the record-commit check rule; the git-annex part is dropped (outside the specification). Runners: as agreed, see X02 |
| X56 | What counts as a change for UNCOMMITTED_CHANGES, and which ignore files count? | (e) A change is what git status reports, submodules included, except every record and any result whose bytes match its record's SHA-256. Only the committed .gitignore files count. | Agreed |
| X57 | Is a result's bytes status judged only against a valid record? | (A) Yes. A result with no valid record is `missing`/`absent`, and ABSENT_RESULT and MODIFIED_RESULT apply only to results that have one. | Agreed |
| X58 | What does a level mean in the check layer? | (A) A broken MUST is an error and a broken SHOULD is a warning, and neither fails the check run. | Agreed |
| X59 | What happens to a record whose version is older than any record schema the checker has, and which earlier schemas must a checker keep? | (1a) A new code, UNSUPPORTED_RECORD_VERSION (MUST, status `missing`); (2i) a checker MUST keep every earlier release's record schema, so that upgrading it never turns old results `missing`. | Agreed, with the obligation starting at 0.1.0: there are no records before it |

### Order of work

1. Guard the contract and the tests first: X09 (the invalid-fixture test checks exactly what its header says), X27 (every `$` is `$(?!\n)`), X01 (the error map codes each keyword by what it checks), X14 (guarded class-level checks, Ajv strict in CI), then X05 (§10's error-to-code mapping, which depends on X14).
2. Harden the tooling and the parse layer: X15 (the §9 extractor), X24 (§9 cells as data), X22 (the wheel's exact file set), X10 (the loader follows YAML 1.2.2), X19 (§2, §3 and §10 state the parse layer exactly).
3. Make the contract fixes that need a decision: X17 (url, Tom's decision 4), X16 (Environment.iri and the JSON-LD context), X30 (versioned `$id`s).
4. Add the coverage tests, which build on 1 and 3: X21 (a negative case for every constraint, after X01 and X17) and X26 (the spec's examples and the §4 ABNF tied to the contract).
5. Restore the spec text to its sources: X23, X13, X06, X25, X02, X07, X03, X08 (with the MAY sentence that is X58's just do half), X18, X20, X12, X29, then X11 (after X13).
6. Do the design bookkeeping and the last piece: X28 (the design renumbered to the 29-change draft), then X04. Do X04 last because it depends on X23 and X17 and on the tidy-up fixes X35 and X33.

- **tidy-up RFC:** once the contract and test guards (steps 1-3) are in, Tom decides the 18 open points and the changes are written into the RFC draft in one joint pass, so that the new change numbers don't clash (X37, X34 and X45 each claim "change 30" now). Suggested order: X35 first, then the change-29 parse block (X32, X31, X36, then X37), then X34, X38 and X45, then X33 and X46, then X16, X41, X42 and X47, then X43, X40, X39, X48, X44 and X49. The spec and contract edits for each land with it.
- **when RFC:** X51 and X50 go into the conditional-elements draft (#93) once X34's chain wording is settled; their §5, §7 and §9 edits land when that RFC does.
- **checker and runner phase:** for now, only the design §12/§13 notes and three small pieces are done as just do: X55's wording on whose commit a record names, X56's restored UNCOMMITTED_CHANGES exemption, and X58's MAY sentence. The rest waits for the check and runner phase.

| X49 | Tidy-up RFC | S | F093 | PIN_LIKE_COMMENT is dropped; §4 keeps the stray-space caveat as advice **Done** (`0e882a0`) |
| X50 | When RFC (#93) | M | F048, F108 | When RFC: a use counts where it happens, across analyses too |
| X51 | When RFC (#93) | M | F002 | When RFC: driving counts only the universes where something reached through the chain is active |
| X52 | Just do | M | F007, F039, F055 | Planned results, a result's names, and unpinned evidence's status **Done** (`4495c81`) |
| X53 | Checker and runner phase | S | F024, F040, F041 | Check phase: STALE_RESULT settles the option compared, a missing read result, and the working tree **Done, now part** (`ab9310a`, `6d26cc1`) |
| X54 | Checker and runner phase | M | F044, F071, F073 | Check phase: a file's content is the bytes git stores, a commit's files are its tree, and a checker that can't get an input's content gives `unknown` **Done, now part** (`d83dac1`) |
| X55 | Checker and runner phase | M | F019, F043 | Which repository's commit a record names (now) and a rule that it stays in history (check phase) **Done, now part** (`6a48242`) |
| X56 | Checker and runner phase | S | F045 (partly F053) | UNCOMMITTED_CHANGES is judged in the checkout a recipe runs in (now); what a change is (runner phase) **Done, now part** (`91a808f`) |

## For astra-tools

What the decided plan means for astra-tools' v0.1 design
(`astra-tools/design/2026-11-04-v0.1-tooling-design.md`, branch `v0.1-design`).
Items marked *done* are on `v0.1-schema` (commit given). The others are decided
but not yet in the specification.

**The rulings its §14 asks for:**

- **Planned results and result names** (X52, landing now). A root's planned
  results are, in each of its universes, its active outputs, every result those
  read through `Output.inputs` (transitively, across declarations), and every
  result its evidence cites. An output of a declared analysis that nothing
  reaches isn't built. A result's name is its output's id after the driven
  declarations from the analysis it belongs to. Reached through two such chains,
  it is built once and placed and recorded under each. Undriven declarations,
  back-edges included, never add a name, and a consumed analysis's results stay
  in its own directory (RFC 0005 §1). Building a result on request
  (`materialise u1 sub.out`) is the tooling's to offer.
- **An analysis with no decisions reading a per-universe output** (X34): an
  analysis with no decisions and no universes of its own counts as driven by
  every universe of its driver. *Superseded on 2026-11-06 by `8782a5b`: an
  analysis with no decisions is driven wherever its chain is, universes of its
  own or not, and imported universes reach its own universes.*
- **Agreeing spellings** (X04, astra-tools' proposal): two spellings of one
  decision in one universe, and two addresses of one element in one list, are an
  error, a new validator code (`DUPLICATE_ELEMENT`, confirmed by Tom on
  2026-11-05). Design §13 has no open point left.
- **Versions** (X30, X59): a patch release never changes what is valid. A 0.1
  validator ships one analysis schema and one record schema and accepts every
  0.1.x. A record whose `spec_version` has no record schema (below 0.1.0) fails
  `INVALID_RECORD`, so its result is `missing`; a newer one is read through the
  open variant. There is no `UNSUPPORTED_RECORD_VERSION`.
- **Placeholders** (X60): a placeholder gives a path the recipe can open from
  the directory it runs in; whether it is relative or absolute is the runner's.
  Substitution is verbatim and the author quotes. An output without a `recipe`
  has no meaning in v0.1.
- **Evidence ids:** stay removed.

**Its other points:**

- **A tag to pin against:** a 0.1.0 pre-release once the contract fixes land.
  Files keep `spec_version: "0.1.0"`. Under X30 each `$id` names a minor version
  (`…/0.1/…`), and a pre-release keeps its full version in its ids.
- **`conformance/` in astra-spec:** agreed. It stays out of the wheel (D7).
  `spec/` cases are normative; the `tools/` shapes are agreements between the
  tools, marked non-normative.
- **Analysis-159's second code:** gone (X14, done in `53d605e`).
- **Parametric outputs, multi-file inputs, declared targets:** recorded for
  later in design §12 (X28).

**What changes in its design:**

- **`PIN_LIKE_COMMENT` is dropped** (X49). No loader needs YAML comments.
- **Stand-ins are the tooling's** (X46, done in `9b06dcb`): the specification
  names none. Its stand-in cases move from `spec/` to `tools/`.
- **No `modified` status** (done in `887946f`): a result's bytes are `present`
  or `absent`. Tools may report more.
- **Loading `astra.yaml`:**
  - a key that isn't a string is `INVALID_ID` at its map, naming the key; the
    entry is left out and the rest validated (X31; the error map already gives
    `propertyNames/type` `INVALID_ID`, done in `c23c395`);
  - a value with no JSON form (`.inf`, `.nan`, a number beyond double range, a
    string with a lone surrogate) fails at parse as `INVALID_SYNTAX` (X31);
  - the file is UTF-8 (X32);
  - only core-schema tags, no anchors or aliases, at most one document (X36);
  - at a commit, it is parsed from the raw blob; in the working tree, from the
    file on disk (X33).
- **Records** are strict JSON, with no BOM and no repeated name. A broken record
  leaves its result `missing`, reported as `INVALID_RECORD` with the underlying
  code as its reason (X37).
- **No cascades** (X38): §9's "What is checked" gives a closed list of the
  checks skipped where another broken rule leaves their subject undetermined.
  Skipped checks aren't reported at all; this is unrelated to the validation
  report's `unchecked[]`, which lists codes a partial build doesn't implement.
- **Driving** (X34, X06 done in `dd59f3e`): judged on the effective selection,
  per chain of declarations; a reference's last declaration decides; a pin
  resolves in the frame of the file it is written in; the root is never driven.
  Driving an analysis declared with a commit stays as RFC 0007 has it.
- **Paths** (X35): case- and Unicode-exact against git's tree, on every machine.
  The specification has no Windows rule.
- **Submodules in an analysis read from a remote** (X33, X45): a gitlink's
  commit is fetched from the URL `.gitmodules` gives at that commit, a relative
  URL resolved against the declaration's `url`. Failing to fetch it is
  `REMOTE_UNREACHABLE`.
- **Content** (X54): a result's content and `size` are the bytes git stores, or
  what a recognised stand-in names. A runner refuses when a checkout would give
  other bytes (code `CONVERTED_CHECKOUT`, confirmed by Tom on 2026-11-05).
  Checkers never imitate git's conversions.
- **Runners** (X02, X56): a short list of MUSTs that protect what a record says.
  `UNCOMMITTED_CHANGES` applies to the checkout a recipe runs in, so a runner may
  run in its own clean worktree at commit C while the main checkout is dirty, and
  commit the records (naming C) later, on top of C. `UNDECLARED_READ` becomes a
  SHOULD on recipes, which a runner may enforce.
- **Location selectors** (X39): an optional `conforms_to` (`slot_uri:
  dcterms:conformsTo`); without it, the value is a PDF fragment (RFC 8118).

## For the tidy-up RFC

What the tidy-up RFC draft (`astra-rfcs/tidy-up/astra-spec/rfcs/NNNN-settling-the-schema-for-v0.1.md`,
owned by the tidy-up chat) needs from this plan. The rule for what goes in it,
proposed and still to be confirmed by Tom: a change needs RFC text when it adds,
removes or renames a field, makes realistic valid files invalid or changes what
they mean, or picks between meanings people would reasonably argue about. Making
the specification precise where the RFCs are vague, settling edge cases nobody
writes, and saying how tools read and report, go in the specification with one
line in design §11.

**Needs RFC text:**

- X34: driving and pins defined once, with the no-decisions clause.
- X35: one sentence in change 27: a path names the entry git holds at exactly
  that path, case and Unicode form included.
- X36: in change 29: only core-schema tags, no anchors or aliases, at most one
  document.
- X39: in change 4: the optional `conforms_to`, PDF (RFC 8118) when absent.
- X40: `RESERVED_PATH` (and `CLASHING_RESULT_NAME`).
- X44: in change 24: no optional list of strings is empty.
- X48: changes 11 and 12 say what the schema does.
- X49: in Removing: `PIN_LIKE_COMMENT` is removed.
- X04: in Tightening: one spelling of each entry (`DUPLICATE_ELEMENT`).
- X45 with X33: a submodule's commit is fetched from `.gitmodules`' URL, and
  failing to fetch it is `REMOTE_UNREACHABLE`.
- To confirm (X02): `UNDECLARED_READ` moves from a runner MUST to a SHOULD on
  recipes. That changes the level of an RFC 0005 §3 rule, so it probably needs a
  line too.

**Spec only, with a design §11 line (if Tom confirms the rule above):** X31,
X32, X33 (apart from the submodule part), X37, X38, X41, X42, X43, X45 (the
remembered answer), X47, X52 and X54.

**Edits the implemented fixes left for the draft** (they couldn't edit another
chat's file):

- From X08 (`887946f`): record that `modified` leaves the statuses, so a breach
  of RFC 0005 §1's MUST on what a default place holds gives `absent`.
- From X19 (`6d7273d`): change 29 says "the core schema's scalar types are
  JSON's (its integers and floats are numbers)", and states the `%YAML`
  directive rule as a sentence of its own, as ASTRA's rule.
- From X20 (`ff7fc8b`): change 4 cites RFC 8118 §3 for a PDF `value`, not RFC
  3778.
- From X29 (`a68ad06`): the `excluded` migration step names `no` and `off`
  beside `excluded: false` (the same edit as X48).

## Just do

Implementation work and spec text that an accepted RFC or the tidy-up draft already decides. No RFC needed.

### X01. The error map codes each keyword by what it checks

- **Resolves:** F059, F094, F137 · **Partly:** F001, F031 · **Size:** S · **Decision:** no · **Done:** `c23c395`

**The problem.** The error map in `tools/rules.py` gives a default code by keyword name alone, wherever the keyword is and whatever its value. So the nine `propertyNames/type` keywords get `WRONG_TYPE`, where §9's `INVALID_ID` row says a map key that doesn't match its key's type is `INVALID_ID`, and `test_every_map_key_is_an_id` filters them out (F059). A `minItems`/`minProperties` above 1 would be coded `EMPTY_COLLECTION` without an error (F137). The generator leaves three `required: []` behind when it drops an entry's key, and the map gives each `MISSING_FIELD` though it can never fail (F094).

**The fix.**
- `tools/rules.py`, `_ErrorMap.value`, the `propertyNames` branch: walk the key's subschema in its own method, which gives both `type` and `pattern` the key's code (`pattern_code` of the key slot) and raises `unknown()` on any other keyword.
- `tools/rules.py`, the defaults: `minItems`/`minProperties` take `EMPTY_COLLECTION` only when the value is 1, and raise `unknown()` otherwise, as `maxItems` does. The `DEFAULTS` docstring says so.
- `tools/jsonschema_generator.py`, `after_generate_schema`: delete `required` from an entry when removing the key empties it.
- Run `just gen-schema`. In `rules.json` the nine `propertyNames/type` entries become `INVALID_ID`, and the three `…__identifier_optional/required` entries go.
- `tests/test_rules.py`: widen `test_every_map_key_is_an_id` to every location containing `/propertyNames`. Add a test that no generated schema has an empty `required`. Add a test, on a scratch schema in `tests/generator/`, that a `minimum_cardinality: 2` fails the build.
- New fixture `tests/data/invalid/Analysis-187-output-key-integer.yaml`, `# rule: INVALID_ID`, `# fails at: /outputs`, with `outputs: {1: {type: metric, format: json}}`. Ajv, which turns the key into `"1"`, gives the same code. The `true:` key stays with X31. If X31 makes a key that isn't a string fail at parse, this fixture becomes a parse fixture, and the generator can drop `propertyNames/type`.
- Spec §10's defaults paragraph, CLAUDE.md "Codes on constraints" and design D13: "`type` (`WRONG_TYPE`), except on a map key, which takes the key's code", and "`minProperties`/`minItems` of 1 (`EMPTY_COLLECTION`)".

**Why this route.** None of this changes what is valid. It only makes `rules.json` give the codes the sources already give: §9's `INVALID_ID` row ("Every map key matches its key's type"), tidy-up change 15 ("A map's keys follow the id grammar … The schema checks the keys"), §9's `EMPTY_COLLECTION` row ("at least one entry"), and §10's definition of `errors` ("every keyword … that can report an error").

**Done when.** `just test` passes with the widened map-key test, the empty-`required` test, the minimum-of-2 build test and Analysis-187. `rules.json` has no `WRONG_TYPE` under `/propertyNames` and no `required` entry that is empty in its schema.

### X02. Runner rules: a short list of MUSTs that protect the record, restored from RFC 0004, 0005 and 0007

- **Resolves:** F016, F017, F029, F030, F036, F038, F078, F099 · **Size:** M · **Depends on:** none; shares the `UNCOMMITTED_CHANGES` row with X40 and X56, and the `commit` wording with X55 · **Decision:** decided · **Done:** `cd78a95`

**The problem.** The runner table copied the RFCs' rule tables but left out their prose qualifiers and their outcome column. `UNDECLARED_READ` lost "the files inputs name", so it now forbids a recipe from reading its own script (F016). `STALE_INPUT` covers every result read (F017). `REBUILT_FROM_COMMIT` covers only part of RFC 0007 §3 and §7 (F029). RFC 0004's working directory has no row (F030). Without the outcome column, nothing says that `UNDECLARED_READ` binds the recipe, or what a runner does and reports (F036, F038). §1 and §9 call the runner's recommendation and permission "duties", which §8 writes in lowercase (F078). §7 "Driving" dropped RFC 0007 §7's exemption for the driven files and "Only what the driver's universes build is built" (F099). `UNCOMMITTED_CHANGES` binds "the repository", so a runner can't run in a clean checkout of its own while the author's working tree has changes, though RFC 0005 §3's rule is "Only committed code is run" (l.161) and refusing on a dirty repository (l.149) is only one way to keep it. Behind all of it, the specification never says what the runner rules are for.

**The fix.** Nothing can check a runner as such. A record is the runner's testimony and names the runner, so the runner rules are a short list of MUSTs that keep what a record says true. The rest is advice. How a runner works (where it stores bytes, how it makes a checkout, containers) stays out. No Rule cell carries a keyword. Line numbers are those of `docs/specification.md` now.
- §9 runner table (:1280-1288), in this order:
  - `UNRECORDED_RESULT`: unchanged.
  - `FALSE_RECORD` (new) | MUST | "Every field of a record a runner writes is true of the run it records: the commit the recipe ran from, the hash and size of what it read and of what it made, the environment it ran in, the runner and its version, and the times" | 0005 §2, 0005 §3. "The commit the recipe ran from" is X55's `commit` wording: the commit of the checkout the recipe ran in.
  - `UNRECREATABLE_ENVIRONMENT`: unchanged.
  - `UNCOMMITTED_CHANGES` | MUST | "A runner runs each recipe in a checkout of the commit its record names, with no changes but the results and records it writes itself, the results the recipe reads, at their recorded bytes, and what a tool keeps of, or puts in place to drive, an analysis from a commit" | 0005 §3, 0007 §2, 0007 §7. This one wording carries X40's "itself" and X56's exemption for analyses from a commit, so X40 and X56 make no further edit to the row now. X56's runner-phase definition of a change later replaces the list of exemptions, and keeps "a checkout of the commit its record names".
  - `WRONG_WORKING_DIRECTORY` (new) | MUST | "A runner runs each recipe in the directory of the recipe's own analysis, in the checkout it runs in, even when another analysis drives it" | 0004 §1, 0004 §5.
  - `NOT_AT_COMMIT`: unchanged.
  - `REBUILT_FROM_COMMIT`: "A runner builds no result that belongs to an analysis from a commit, or to an analysis reached from it through declarations, and reads each such result through its record at the commit that analysis is read at" | 0007 §3, 0007 §7. This covers §7 "Use"'s `upstream.stage.report` and F029's `x_undriven.out#v1`, as RFC 0007 §3 counts `upstream.subanalysis3.output3`.
  - `STALE_INPUT`, option (a): "A runner builds no output that reads a result belonging to an analysis from a commit, or to an analysis reached from it through declarations, while that result is `missing` or `stale`, or its bytes can't be got or aren't the recorded ones" | 0007 §4, 0007 §5. What the driver's universes build from a driven analysis belongs to the driver, so it falls outside this row.
  - `UNDECLARED_READ` | **SHOULD** | "A recipe reads the files inputs name, and other outputs' results, only through its output's inputs" | 0005 §3, tidy-up. It binds the recipe, not the runner.
- Tidy-up RFC, one new change in Modifying (numbered in the joint pass): "`UNDECLARED_READ` is a SHOULD. RFC 0005 §3 makes it a MUST on the recipe, but nothing checks a recipe except a runner that chooses to. The analysis stays valid when a recipe breaks it, and a runner conforms whether or not it enforces it." Design §11 lists it. Item 15 of the pending decisions doesn't name X02 in either of its lists. This change lowers a keyword of an accepted RFC, so it stays RFC text; confirm with Tom when item 15 is confirmed.
- No RFC text for the checkout `UNCOMMITTED_CHANGES` applies to (item 17): it is a reading of RFC 0005 §3's "Only committed code is run". Design §11 gets one line, numbered in the joint pass: "`UNCOMMITTED_CHANGES` applies to the checkout a recipe runs in. A runner may run in a clean checkout of its own at a commit while the working tree has changes, and write the results and records into the working tree, to be committed in a commit that has that commit in its history. RFC 0005 §3's refusal on a dirty repository is one way to keep its rule, 'Only committed code is run'. No RFC text." §11's closing sentence (now "The first seventeen are in the tidy-up RFC draft …; item 18 is not yet") says which items are RFC text and which aren't.
- §9 consequence table, runner row (:1138, X12's "The runner doesn't conform. Where a rule says the runner refuses, it reports the code"): "A runner that breaks a `MUST` doesn't conform. It runs no recipe while `UNCOMMITTED_CHANGES` would be broken, and builds no output while `STALE_INPUT` would be, and when it refuses for either it reports the code. `UNDECLARED_READ` binds the recipe: breaking it leaves the analysis valid, and a runner that enforces it fails the recipe and reports the code." X12's `MAY` paragraph (:1145-1148) stays.
- §9 closing note (:1290-1293): "A recommendation and a permission. Neither is a rule of its own, because nothing can check them: a runner SHOULD let someone see what an environment it recorded holds, and MAY enforce `UNDECLARED_READ`, with a sandbox say. §5 and §8 explain them." §1 "Rules" (:22-27): "the runner's recommendation and permission" replaces "the two duties of a runner". X58's `MAY` and X12's `PIN_LIKE_COMMENT` no longer touch this list (items 13 and X49).
- §2 layer table, runner row (:83): Sees "its own actions, the checkouts it runs recipes in, and what the recipes read". §2 runner bullet (:94-95): "A runner runs recipes and writes each result's bytes and record. A record is its runner's testimony, and names it. A runner conforms when it keeps the runner `MUST`s, which keep its records true. How it stores bytes or builds environments is its own."
- §5 "Recipes" (:443-444), replacing "A recipe reads files and results only through its output's inputs ([`UNDECLARED_READ`])": "A recipe should read the files inputs name, and other outputs' results, only through its output's inputs ([`UNDECLARED_READ`](#runner)), since a checker sees a change to them only through those inputs. Anything else it uses, its own scripts among them, is pinned by the commit or is part of the environment. A runner can enforce this, as §9 permits; one that does fails the recipe." X55's submodule sentence follows it. Lowercase here, as §1 keeps keywords to §9 and the places it lists. §5 (:422-423, "A recipe runs in its analysis's directory") and §3 (:160-161, "Its recipes run in that directory") link `WRONG_WORKING_DIRECTORY`.
- §8 "What a runner does" (:1031-1045), rewritten as the list:
  - "A runner writes a record for every result it makes … ([`UNRECORDED_RESULT`])." Kept.
  - "Every field of the record is true of the run ([`FALSE_RECORD`]). It records each recipe's environment as an IRI that it can recreate at its version ([`UNRECREATABLE_ENVIRONMENT`]), and should let someone see what that environment holds, as PROV for example."
  - "It runs only committed code. It runs each recipe in a checkout of the commit the record names, with no changes but the results and records it writes itself, the results the recipe reads, at their recorded bytes, and what a tool keeps of, or puts in place to drive, an analysis from a commit ([`UNCOMMITTED_CHANGES`]). That checkout is the working tree when the working tree has no other changes. When it has, the runner refuses to run, or runs in a checkout of its own, at HEAD say, and writes the results and records into the working tree. Either way the records are committed in a commit that has the commit they name in its history (below). It runs each recipe in its analysis's directory in that checkout ([`WRONG_WORKING_DIRECTORY`])."
  - "It builds nothing that belongs to an analysis from a commit ([`REBUILT_FROM_COMMIT`]), and builds no output that reads such a result while it is `missing` or `stale` or its bytes can't be got or aren't the recorded ones ([`STALE_INPUT`]). A result of its own analysis that it reads may be stale; what it builds from it is then stale too (see [When a result is unchanged])." X07 adds RFC 0005 §4's made-again example to that section, so the point is stated once.
  - "A checker sees much of a breach afterwards: a result with no record is `missing`, and bytes that aren't the recorded ones are `absent`." (`modified` is gone, X08.)
  - The `UNDECLARED_READ` sentences move to §5; §8 keeps "Enforcing `UNDECLARED_READ` is the runner's choice (§5)". X55's sentence on submodules follows it.
  - The paragraph after the list (:1047-1050, "A record names the commit its recipe ran from …") stays, with X55's "has to stay". How a checker on a working tree with changes judges the results written there is X53's (decided: against the working tree, check phase); X02 adds nothing to "When a result is unchanged".
- §7 "Analyses from a commit": after "Every rule of this specification applies to an analysis from a commit as it is, with its files read at its commit" (:818-819), define the scope once: "Its results are those that belong to it or to an analysis reached from it through declarations, read at the same commit." "What is read" (:827-845) already says "None of its code runs, and no checkout is needed" and that nothing read stops a runner ([`UNCOMMITTED_CHANGES`]): keep both. "Use" (:857) links `REBUILT_FROM_COMMIT`. "Driving" (:883-899): after "they aren't part of the declaring analysis's code", add "and aren't a change that stops a runner from running ([`UNCOMMITTED_CHANGES`](#runner))"; after the `REBUILT_FROM_COMMIT` sentence, add "Only what the driver's universes build is built."
- Design §7 runner section (the table and the closing note after it): mirror the spec. Design §10:
  - RFC 0004 row "An analysis is a directory with an `astra.yaml`; recipes run in its directory": Lands in "spec 3, spec 5; `WRONG_WORKING_DIRECTORY`".
  - RFC 0005: a new row "The record's fields state the run" → `FALSE_RECORD`. "Only committed code is run; the record's commit stays in history (merge, don't squash)" → "`UNCOMMITTED_CHANGES`, in the checkout the recipe runs in (§11); spec 8 (SHOULD)". "Environment as a recreatable IRI; SHOULD be inspectable" → "`UNRECREATABLE_ENVIRONMENT`; §9's closing note (SHOULD)". "Recipes read only through their inputs; runners MAY enforce" → "`UNDECLARED_READ` (SHOULD, tidy-up); §9's closing note (MAY)".
  - RFC 0007: "An undriven analysis from a commit gives pinned outputs, outputs built once and inputs" adds `REBUILT_FROM_COMMIT` to its Lands-in. "Doesn't build from a missing or stale result" becomes "Doesn't build from a missing or stale result of an analysis from a commit, or one whose bytes can't be got"; Lands-in stays.
  - "Checked on 2026-11-03: 100 rows, all landed. No corrections were needed." drops its last sentence.
- Design §12: "Runner rules are tested by behaviour, which needs a runner harness: future work in astra-tools (its design §13, §15). The cases include a run in a clean checkout of the runner's own while the working tree has changes."
- Run `just gen-schema` and commit `rules.json`.

**Decided.** Option (a): `STALE_INPUT` keeps RFC 0007's scope, so a partial local rebuild conforms and its results show as stale through `STALE_RESULT`; the wide rule (b) is not taken. Tom also agreed the runners approach: a short list of MUSTs that keep the record true, `UNDECLARED_READ` as a SHOULD on recipes that a runner MAY enforce, advice for the rest, and no runner mechanics. And (Tom, 2026-11-04, item 17, from astra-tools): `UNCOMMITTED_CHANGES` applies to the checkout a recipe runs in. A runner may run in a clean checkout of its own at commit C while the main checkout has changes, and write results and records naming C into the main checkout, to be committed later. Its conditions: that checkout is a checkout of C with no changes but what the runner writes and supplies; the results a recipe reads are their recorded bytes; the records are committed in a commit that has C in its history (X55). A checker on the main checkout judges them against the working tree (X53). No RFC text; one design §11 line.

**Why this route.** Most of the fix restores text the sources already have. RFC 0004 §1: "An analysis' recipes always run in its own directory". RFC 0005 §3: "the files inputs name", "MAY enforce", "refuses to run", "the recipe fails". RFC 0007 §2: "Reading it runs none of its code". RFC 0007 §7: "aren't a change that stops a runner from running" and "Only what the driver's universes build is built". RFC 0007's table: "isn't built". `FALSE_RECORD` codes what RFC 0005 §2 already defines each field to be, and its "a record is its runner's claim" (Possible future RFCs). Reporting the code is tidy-up change 19's "Every rule has a code". The checkout reading keeps RFC 0005 §3's rule, "Only committed code is run", and what it protects: the record's commit holds every file the recipe used, and the recipe reads its results at their recorded bytes. So it needs no RFC text. The one departure, `UNDECLARED_READ` from MUST to SHOULD, changes an accepted RFC, so it goes into the tidy-up RFC as one line and lands in the joint tidy-up pass; the rest is just do.

**Done when.** `rules.json` has 9 runner rules with the statements above: 8 MUSTs (`UNRECORDED_RESULT`, `FALSE_RECORD`, `UNRECREATABLE_ENVIRONMENT`, `UNCOMMITTED_CHANGES`, `WRONG_WORKING_DIRECTORY`, `NOT_AT_COMMIT`, `REBUILT_FROM_COMMIT`, `STALE_INPUT`) and `UNDECLARED_READ` as a SHOULD. `UNCOMMITTED_CHANGES` says "a checkout of the commit its record names" and "writes itself", with Source `0005 §3, 0007 §2, 0007 §7`. `just test` passes. The tidy-up draft has the `UNDECLARED_READ` change; design §11 lists it and the checkout line. In the spec and the design, a case-sensitive `grep -Pzo 'may\s+enforce'` and a grep for "duties" find nothing (the permission is written once, as `MAY`, in §9's closing note), "the repository has no changes" is gone, and no sentence says how a runner stores bytes, makes a checkout or builds environments.

### X03. Records restored from RFC 0005/0007 and change 28: required marks, inputs read at the record's commit, the driver's commit, planned-result scope, unchecked day-of-month

- **Resolves:** F025, F086, F087, F096, F101, F130 · **Size:** M · **Decision:** no · **Done:** `0791b43`

**The problem.** §8 "What a record holds", §9's record rows and the `provenance.yaml` and `types.yaml` descriptions each lost something their sources say. RFC 0005 §2's required and optional marks are gone (F096). Its "for each input the output lists", read at the commit the record names, became a one-sided rule with no commit (F025, F086). RFC 0007:179's "those records name the driver's commit" never reached `Generation.commit` (F087). RFC 0005 §4 checks records through planned results, but three rows say "every record" (F101). Change 28's "whether a day exists in its month isn't checked" is missing (F130).

**The fix.**
- Spec §8 "What a record holds": mark the required fields (`spec_version`, `address`, `content_hash`, `size`, `generated_by`; `commit`, `environment`, `runner`; `iri`; `id`, `version`; each item's `entry`, `content_hash`, `size`). Say that `started` and `finished` are optional, and that `inputs` is optional in the schema but held to `MISSING_RECORD_INPUTS`.
- §8 `commit` bullet and `Generation.commit`: "The full id of the git commit the recipe ran from, the driver's commit for what a driver's universes build. It pins every file of the code and every `astra.yaml`: an analysis in a submodule through the commit the submodule is pinned at, and an analysis from a commit through its declaration's `commit`." Link the bullet to §7 Driving. Leave the submodule clause as it is (X55).
- §8 `inputs` bullet, `Generation.inputs` and `RecordedInput.entry`: one item for each entry the output lists in the `astra.yaml` files at the record's `commit`, and no other item. Add: "A later change to the list makes the result `stale`, not `missing`."
- §8 Statuses: "A checker reads records only through planned results: a record at no planned result's place gets no status, and no check rule applies to it."
- §9 rows:
  - `INVALID_RECORD`: "The record at each planned result's place is valid against …"
  - `MISPLACED_RECORD`: "The record at each planned result's place names that result in its `address`."
  - `MISSING_RECORD_INPUTS`: "The record at each planned result's place has exactly one `inputs` item for each entry its output lists at the commit the record names, matched by `entry`, and no other item (their order carries no meaning)."
  - `MISSING_FIELD`: name the record's fields above in place of "the record's (§8)".
  - `INVALID_SYNTAX`: leave as it is.
- `timestamp` (types.yaml), the §4 named-types row and §8 `started`/`finished`: "each field in its range; whether the day exists in its month, such as 30 February, isn't checked". §10 RDF: such a time converts to an ill-typed `xsd:dateTime`.
- Design §6 (:291-294) and §7 rows (:351, :430-432): make them match.
- `just gen-schema`: the provenance schemas' descriptions and `rules.json` change. No keyword changes.

**Why this route.** Each change restores a source as written. RFC 0005 §2 gives the marks, "for each input the output lists" and "the output's declaration … [is] in the `astra.yaml` files at that commit". RFC 0005 §4 says that a change to the declaration's inputs makes a result stale, which holds only if the list is read at the record's commit. Its check table evaluates "each planned result". RFC 0007:179 and tidy-up change 28 settle the rest. Nothing changes what is valid.

**Done when.** `just test` passes, with the regenerated `rules.json` and provenance schemas committed. The new valid fixtures `ProvenanceRecord-003-minimal` (no `inputs`, `started` or `finished`) and `ProvenanceRecord-004-day-not-in-month` (`2026-02-30`) validate.

### X05. §10 'From an error to its code': each validator described on its own, and only errors that make the instance fail are mapped

- **Resolves:** F049, F105, F106, F115, F120 · **Size:** M · **Depends on:** X14 · **Decision:** no · **Done:** `30f68d6`

**The problem.** §10's "From an error to its code" describes `jsonschema` and Ajv as one validator that reports "the innermost errors". Neither does that exactly (F105). Tools that use standard output get two kinds of innermost error that don't make the instance fail: an untaken `if`'s own failure, and a failing branch of a `oneOf` that passed. The first has no entry and the second gets the wrong code (F049). The jsonschema bullet blames the omitted `if` for the schema path not being the location, but only the unnamed `$ref` causes that (F106). The paragraph also doesn't say where a bad map key is (F115), and it leaves out Ajv's `uniqueItems` departures (F120).

**The fix.**
- Spec §10, opening paragraph, rewritten in this sense: a tool maps each error that makes the instance fail. That means the failing assertions, plus a `oneOf`, `not`, `if` or `propertyNames` that fails as a whole, together with the errors that say why a failing `oneOf` failed. Each has an entry. A tool drops any error inside an `if` subschema (JSON Schema 2019-09 core §9.2.2.1: its outcome "has no direct effect"). It also drops any error inside a `oneOf`/`anyOf` branch unless that combinator is reported as failing, and the applicators that standard output lists above an error. Any validator then gives the same set of (instance location, code) pairs, though the number of errors may differ.
- The jsonschema bullet says that it reports the errors inside a failing `then` and inside `propertyNames`, never the `if` or `propertyNames` itself, and that a failing `oneOf` comes as one error with its branch errors in `context`. The parenthetical about `if` goes. For a bad key, `error.instance` is the key.
- The Ajv bullet says that Ajv reports both levels (the inner error and the `if`/`propertyNames`/`oneOf` above it, with the same code), and that the key is `propertyName`, or `params.propertyName` on the `propertyNames` error. It also says that Ajv's `uniqueItems` skips items of the wrong type and misses a repeated `__proto__`. An Ajv-based tool checks for `__proto__` itself, and a comparison of code sets ignores `DUPLICATE_ENTRY` beside a `WRONG_TYPE`.
- One sentence before the bullets: a bad key is reported at the map's pointer, since a JSON Pointer can't name a key, and a tool reports the key with the code.
- `tests/conftest.py` `absolute_keyword_location` docstring: the `if` clause is dropped. The comment at `tools/rules.py:410` and the `REPORTING` docstring in `tests/test_rules.py` cite §10.
- `tests/test_rules.py`: jschon goes into the dev group, pinned. A new test applies §10's filter to jschon's basic output for every schema-layer invalid fixture. It asserts that every remaining error has an entry and that the (pointer, code) set equals jsonschema's. Analysis-150 and Analysis-166 are the two fixtures that show the leak.
- Design §12 records the Ajv `__proto__` bug. The upstream Ajv issue is filed if Tom agrees.
- The `dependentRequired` option is not taken. X14 reshapes these `if`/`then` pairs (typed presence, an `if: {type: object}` guard), and §10's filter covers any `if` it adds.

**Why this route.** §10 only explains how to use D13's error map. The fix restores what `rules.json` already means and adds nothing to it: no change to what is valid, and no change to any code. The tidy-up draft doesn't mention `rules.json`.

**Done when.** The jschon test passes on every invalid fixture, Analysis-150 and Analysis-166 included. `just check-generated` shows no change to the contract, and §10 names no `if` difference in its schema-path sentence.

### X06. Driving and ownership stated from the root: the root is never driven, results are named through driven declarations

- **Resolves:** F054, F074, F090, F109 · **Partly:** F033, F047, F055 · **Size:** S · **Decision:** no · **Done:** `dd59f3e`

**The problem.** §1's Terms row, §7's Driving bullets and §8's Results paragraph treat driving and ownership as fixed facts about the files. The RFCs instead state them from the root being built. So the specification never says that only the root's universes drive anything, or that the root is never driven. A back-edge such as `iris: {path: ..}` then lets the root be driven from its own stack (F054). "A driven analysis's own universes aren't used" contradicts §7's own pin example (F074). §8 gives the driver every result of a driven analysis, built-once results included (F090), and names a result through any declaration, undriven ones included (F109).

**The fix.**
- §1 Terms, `driver, driven` row: "in the root's universes, the root, and an analysis whose decisions they set through a declaration; the root is never driven". The `address` row stays as it is, because an element's address may go through any declaration.
- §7 The analysis stack, after its first paragraph: "The universes being built are the root's. In them the root is the driver and is never driven, even when a declaration in its stack leads back to it. Another analysis's universe is used only where a pin names it. That analysis is then its driver, and what is built there belongs to it (§8)."
- §7 Driving, the last bullet becomes: "In the root's universes, a driven analysis's own universes don't set its decisions. A pin in its own file, or through an undriven declaration, still reads one of them (below). Each universe being built has one driver, the analysis whose universe it is."
- §8 Results: "A result belongs to the analysis whose universe it was built in. So what a driven analysis builds in its driver's universes belongs to the driver. The result of an output built once belongs to the analysis whose output it is. In that analysis, the result's name is the output's id after the ids of the declarations its universe is pushed through: driven ones, never an undriven one, since a pin through an undriven declaration names that analysis's own universe (§7)."
- §8 Where records and bytes go: "so a driven analysis's recipes write what they build in the driver's universes into the driver's results."
- §9 `NO_UNIVERSES` Rule cell: "unless the root's universes drive it, or it consumes a decision". Do this in the same pass as X11.
- Design §13 "One result, two names": add a note that only driven declarations name a result. That leaves the open point (settled in X52) with two driven declarations, never back-edges.

**Why this route.** The RFCs already decide this. RFC 0004:37 says a subanalysis validated on its own "becomes the root". RFC 0004:281 says the universe being built "is pushed through every analysis the root drives". RFC 0004:149 and :361-365 give a driven analysis's own universes no say and put built-once results with their own analysis, and RFC 0005:31 says the same. RFC 0004:334 has only the driven declaration share the driver's universes. The schema doesn't change, and what validators must do doesn't change beyond those sentences. One consequence: a pipeline stage validated alone now gets the `NO_UNIVERSES` warning.

**Done when.** The F054 stacks (`classification` as root, the mutual `m`/`fe` pair) and the F074, F090 and F109 stacks each have one reading under the new text. `just test` passes.

### X07. §8 summary sentences restored to RFC 0005/0007's scope

- **Resolves:** F042, F058, F092, F110 · **Partly:** F089, F007 · **Size:** S · **Decision:** decided · **Done:** `526f9c5`, `41a90e6`

**The problem.** Four summary sentences in §8 restate an RFC sentence more broadly than the rules beside them. "Makes nothing stale" drops RFC 0005 §3's "them", the results whose records the commit adds, but a result made again with other bytes makes the results that read it stale (F042). "Editing a finding leaves a result unchanged" ignores an input that names an `astra.yaml` (F110). "Checked by the same rules" loses RFC 0007's split: results that are read or cited get a record status, and only results a recipe reads get a bytes status (F058). "The record its address names" doesn't say that a reference names a result and the record comes from that result's address, so a pin on an output built once plays no part (F092).

**The fix.**
- §8 "What a runner does" (:975-977): "That commit adds only records and results, so it makes none of the results whose records it adds stale."
- §8 "When a result is unchanged" (:995-996): "Nothing else counts: editing a finding, a description or another output leaves a result unchanged, unless the edit changes something listed above (an `astra.yaml` named as an input, or an output whose results it reads, say)." An `astra.yaml` named as an input is an ordinary data file: its content counts like any input's, and nothing else treats it differently. After the §2 example, add RFC 0005 §4's case: editing `src/train.py` leaves both unchanged, but once `accuracy#svm` is made again with other bytes, `comparison` is stale until it is rebuilt.
- §8 "Statuses" (:1016-1018): "Evidence citing a result gets the status of that result's record ([`STALE_EVIDENCE`]). An `artifact` that is pinned, or that names an output built once, names exactly one result, and its record is the one that result's address gives." The unpinned per-universe citation (F007, valid since `UNPINNED_EVIDENCE` is a SHOULD) stays with X52.
- §6 (:604): "a pin on it doesn't change which result a reference names ([`REDUNDANT_PIN`]): pinned or not, the reference names its one result, and `{inputs.<entry>}` gives that result's path; the pin still names a universe ([`UNKNOWN_PIN_UNIVERSE`])."
- §8 (:1019-1021) and the prose after §9's check table (:1187-1189): "The results of an analysis from a commit that are read or cited get their record status (`unchanged`, `stale`, `missing`) by the same rules, through their records at the declared commit. Only those a recipe reads get a bytes status (`ABSENT_RESULT`), from what git holds at the declared commit (0007 §4–5)."
- Design: the same change at :437-439; the traceability row at :638 reads "`ABSENT_RESULT`, at the declared commit, for results a recipe reads".
- Note: the restored :975-977 sentence is independent of X40, but it holds for every valid file only once X40 lands; until then an input may point into `results/` or `.astra/`, and a commit that adds only records changes its content (F053). X40 keeps "an input may name an `astra.yaml`".

**Decided.** Option (a): an `astra.yaml` read as an input is an ordinary data file with no special meaning, so §8's sentence is qualified and nothing is handled differently. Dropping the sentence (b) and forbidding such an input (c) are not taken.

**Why this route.** Each sentence is wrong only against rules already written. RFC 0005 §3 says "it doesn't make them stale"; §4 lists "the content of its inputs" and gives the made-again example. RFC 0007's "What's checked" has separate rows for "read or cited" (`unchanged`, `stale`, `missing`) and "that a recipe reads" (`present`, `modified`, `absent`). RFC 0003 :201 says built once is "materialised once per project, not once per universe", and :208 that a pin on it "selects nothing"; RFC 0005 §1 puts its record at `.astra/records/{name}.json`, so no pin enters the lookup. Qualifying the F110 sentence changes nothing valid and makes it agree with `STALE_RESULT`'s list; it keeps the reassurance that editing a finding is usually safe, and keeps valid files that render findings from an `astra.yaml` valid.

**Done when.** None of the four old sentences remains in the spec or the design, and §8 says nothing that treats an `astra.yaml` input differently from another input. `just test` passes, and `rules.json` has no diff, since only prose outside §9's tables changes.

### X08. Bytes and stand-ins restored from RFC 0005/0006, with a MODIFIED_RESULT row

- **Resolves:** F018, F032, F095, F154 · **Partly:** F071, F097 · **Size:** M · **Decision:** no · **Done:** `887946f`

*Updated by X46 (2026-11-04): the specification names no stand-ins. X08 keeps "Something standing in for the content isn't the content"; its §8 Statuses example (an MD5E git-annex key) goes, and "plainly isn't a stand-in" becomes "a file it can tell is no stand-in".*

**The problem.** §5 and §8 drop four things RFC 0005 and 0006 say about bytes. Nothing says a result is one file: RFC 0005 says "The result's file" (F095). Nothing says a stand-in isn't the content (RFC 0006 §1), so an input's recorded hash can be a pointer's (F032). RFC 0005 §1's MUST on what the default place holds has no code, and its breach, `modified`, sits under `ABSENT_RESULT`, a SHOULD (F018). And `modified`'s test, "the stand-in names another hash", has lost the scope it had in RFC 0005, a SHA-256 hash (F154).

**The fix.**
- §5 Inputs, third bullet: end with "Something standing in for the content isn't the content."
- §8 'Where records and bytes go': add "A result is one file, which its recipe writes at `{output}`. An artefact of many files is written as an archive, or as several outputs." Turn the sentence at :891-892 into an explanation that links to `MODIFIED_RESULT`.
- §9 check table: add `MODIFIED_RESULT` | MUST | "Whatever is at a result's default place is its recorded bytes or something standing in for them" | `modified` | 0005 §1. Narrow `ABSENT_RESULT`'s status to `absent`.
- §9 `STALE_RESULT`: change "its inputs' content" to "the content of its inputs, never what stands in for it". Make the same change in §8's list at :991.
- §8 Statuses (:1011-1014) and the `modified` row (:1173): `modified` means `MODIFIED_RESULT` doesn't hold. Tooling that recognises stand-ins (MAY) reports it when a stand-in names a SHA-256 hash or a size other than the record's, or when a file plainly isn't a stand-in and doesn't match. A stand-in that names neither (an MD5E git-annex key of the right size, say) leaves the bytes `absent`.
- `provenance.yaml`: `ProvenanceRecord.content_hash`/`size` become "of the result's file, never of something standing in for it". `RecordedInput.content_hash`/`size` become "of the content read through the entry, another output's result or the file an input names, never something standing in for it". `Output.format` becomes "the extension of the result's file".
- Design §10 (:598-599): map the MUST to `MODIFIED_RESULT`. Add folder formats such as Zarr to design §12, as RFC 0006 does for inputs.
- Run `just gen-schema` and commit `generated/` with `rules.json`.

**Why this route.** Every sentence comes from the RFCs: RFC 0005:46 states the MUST and gives it its own row at :87; RFC 0006:55 says "Something standing in for the content isn't the content"; RFC 0005:25 says "The result's file"; and RFC 0005:41-42, :217 make the stand-in's hash a SHA-256. Nothing becomes valid or invalid beyond what they already say.

**Done when.** `rules.json` has `MODIFIED_RESULT` (check, MUST, `modified`) and `ABSENT_RESULT` has the status `absent` alone. `just test` passes, drift check included. A grep finds "isn't the content" in §5, and finds "one file" in §8.

### X09. The invalid-fixture test asserts exactly what its header says

- **Resolves:** F060, F062, F118, F134 · **Size:** M · **Decision:** no · **Done:** `a36673c`

**The problem.** The invalid-fixture test checks less than the fixture's header lines say. A parse fixture passes if the loader raises any subclass of the error mapped to its code. Because `INVALID_SYNTAX` maps to `YAMLError`, a duplicate key headed `INVALID_SYNTAX` passes and counts as coverage (F060). Every `oneOf`/`anyOf` error at an ancestor is exempt, and in this contract each such error is a second, independent violation, so a fixture with two breaks passes (F062). The header regex `[A-Z][A-Z_]*` rejects the digits that §9 and `tools/rules.py` allow (F118). `tests/data/README.md` describes the harness as it was before the rewrite and `rules.json` (F134).

**The fix.**
- `tests/conftest.py`: replace `PARSE_ERRORS` with `parse_code(error: YAMLError) -> str`. It gives `DUPLICATE_KEY` for a `DuplicateKeyError`, and `INVALID_SYNTAX` for any other loader error, `ConstructorError` included. Add `PARSE_CODES` for `test_harness_checks_every_parse_and_schema_code`.
- `tests/conftest.py`: change `_RULE_LINE` to `# rule: ([A-Z][A-Z0-9_]*)`. `fixture_header` also reads an optional third line, `# also: <CODE> at <pointer>`, and returns it (or `None`). Delete `COMBINATORS`.
- `tests/test_fixtures.py`: move the check into `check_invalid_fixture(path)`. The parse branch catches `YAMLError` and asserts that `parse_code` gives the fixture's rule, and it allows no `# also:` line. In the schema branch, every error away from `fails at` counts as `elsewhere`, except the errors at the `# also:` pointer. Those must be present, and `rules.json` must give them exactly the `# also:` code, which must be a schema-layer code.
- `Analysis-159-path-null-beside-url.yaml`: add `# also: INVALID_DECLARATION_SOURCE at /analyses/up`.
- Self-tests in `tests/test_fixtures.py` run `check_invalid_fixture` on scratch fixtures under `tmp_path`. Each of these must fail: a duplicate key headed `INVALID_SYNTAX`, F062's short commit beside `path` and `url`, Analysis-159 without its `# also:` line, and an `# also:` line naming an error that isn't reported. A further test checks that `fixture_header` reads `# rule: SHA256_MISMATCH`.
- `CLAUDE.md`, Tests section: describe the optional `# also:` line, the exact parse code (`parse_code`), and the numbering (`001` for valid fixtures; `101` for invalid `Analysis` fixtures and `201` for invalid `ProvenanceRecord` fixtures). Bring in the README's table of where `jsonschema` reports each kind of error. Replace "a `oneOf`/`anyOf` error at an ancestor aside" with the `# also:` rule.
- `tests/data/README.md`: cut it to a two-line pointer to CLAUDE.md's Tests section.
- Design §9 R24 and the §12 note on Analysis-159: a second error is allowed only where the fixture declares it in `# also:`.

**Why this route.** Only the tests, CLAUDE.md, the README and the design notes change. The contract, the specification and what tools must do stay the same. The fix makes the harness check what CLAUDE.md and R24 already require: "a minimal valid document with one thing broken" that "fails for the right reason".

**Done when.** `just test` passes with the five self-tests. Each mutant above fails `check_invalid_fixture`. The four committed parse fixtures still pass, under their exact codes. Every committed schema fixture passes with no exemption, and Analysis-159 passes only with its `# also:` line. X21's per-constraint coverage builds on this exact check.

### X10. Harness loader brought to YAML 1.2.2: strict scanner, `!` as string, node-equal keys, escapes as YAMLError

- **Resolves:** F072, F142, F143, F159 · **Size:** M · **Decision:** no · **Done:** `3fbdfe3`

**The problem.** `tests/conftest.py` restricts ruamel.yaml 0.19.1 only at the `%YAML` directive, the resolver and the constructor. Everywhere else it inherits ruamel's departures from YAML 1.2.2, which §3 and change 29 already require. Its scanner accepts ill-formed text: 9 of yaml-test-suite's 94 error cases, among them a comment glued to a quoted scalar and an under-indented flow or quoted continuation (F072). It resolves a `!`-tagged scalar like a plain one, so `! 12` is 12 and `!<!> 12` loads (F142). It finds duplicate keys by Python equality, so `1`/`true` clash and `.nan`/`.nan` don't, and §3 never says when two keys are the same (F143). A `\U` escape beyond U+10FFFF raises `ValueError`/`OverflowError`, which isn't a `YAMLError` (F159).

**The fix.**
- `tests/yaml-test-suite/`: vendor the error cases of yaml-test-suite at a pinned release (data-2022-01-17, MIT), each one's `in.yaml`. `tests/test_loader.py` runs `load_yaml` over every case and expects a `YAMLError`.
- `_CoreSchemaScanner` checks the four things ruamel skips: whitespace before `#`; a flow or quoted continuation line indented past the enclosing block; `-`, `?` and `:` starting a plain scalar only when a safe character follows; a tab only where YAML allows one. It also rejects the verbatim tag `!<!>`.
- A composer override gives a `!`-tagged scalar `tag:yaml.org,2002:str` (an empty `!` is `""`).
- The constructor compares keys by (resolved tag, canonical form) and raises `DuplicateKeyError` on a match. So `a`/`"\x61"` and `.nan`/`.nan` clash, and `1`/`1.0`/`true` are distinct nodes. A Python dict can't hold those three apart, so the loader raises a `ConstructorError` for them, a limit the docstring states. Once X31 lands, a key that isn't a string fails at parse before this check.
- `load_yaml`/`load_yaml_text` re-raise `ValueError`/`OverflowError` as `ScannerError`, chained `from` the original. `RecursionError` stays unmapped. Fix the `PARSE_ERRORS` and `load_yaml` docstrings to match.
- `tests/test_loader.py`: `! 12`, `! '12'`, `! true`, `!`, `!<!> x`, and the key pairs above.
- New `# fails at: parse` INVALID_SYNTAX fixtures, at the next free Analysis numbers: a glued comment, an under-indented flow continuation and `label: "\U00110000"`. Add them and `tests/yaml-test-suite/` to check-yaml's `exclude` and to `.yamllint.yaml`'s `ignore`.
- §3's duplicate-key bullet and §9's DUPLICATE_KEY row: two keys are the same when they are equal YAML nodes, with the same tag and canonical form (YAML 1.2.2 §3.2.1.3). So `a`, `'a'` and `"\x61"` are one key, and `1` and `"1"` are two. Make this edit together with X19 (which writes "an untagged plain scalar") and X11, then regenerate `rules.json`.
- CLAUDE.md Tests: describe the loader as above, and add the suite directory to the list of files kept out of the linters.

**Why this route.** Change 29 already makes `astra.yaml` "YAML 1.2, read under its core schema", with a repeated key invalid "as YAML requires". YAML 1.2.2 decides each case: §6.6 and §8.2.3 on comments and indentation, §6.9.1 on `!`, §3.2.1.3 on node equality and §5.7 on escapes. The fix brings the harness and §3's wording into line with that, so nothing valid changes. The fixtures added are invalid YAML today. X36 also records `!` in change 29.

**Done when.** The yaml-test-suite test passes on every error case. The new loader tests and the three parse fixtures pass under X09's exact-code check. `just test` is green.

### X11. §9 Rule cells: one self-contained sentence each, as the sources say

- **Resolves:** F015, F124, F125, F126 · **Partly:** F075 · **Size:** M · **Depends on:** X13 · **Decision:** no · **Done:** `154fc0a`

*Updated by X49 (2026-11-04): `PIN_LIKE_COMMENT` is dropped, so the bullet about it below is withdrawn.*

*After the review, Tom added (2026-11-05) that a path is non-empty: INVALID_PATH's cell begins "Every declaration's and input's `path` is non-empty,", and §5, §7, the two `path` descriptions and the draft's change 30 say so too (`092109b`).*

**The problem.** `rules.json` ships each §9 Rule cell as a standalone `statement`, and tools print it. Several cells say more or less than their sources, or break §9's own convention ("one sentence saying what holds … never says what happens when it is broken"). INPUT_IN_ANOTHER_ANALYSIS drops RFC 0006's "within it" and so rejects every input of a nested analysis (F015). INVALID_REFERENCE drops §4's "other" and claims the keys of `Universe.decisions` (F126). WRONG_TYPE leaves out `number` (F124). DUPLICATE_KEY, INVALID_PATH, WRONG_TYPE, UNSUPPORTED_SPEC_VERSION and PIN_LIKE_COMMENT carry extra sentences or notes, and UNDRIVEN_DECLARATION leans on the row above (F125).

**The fix.** In `docs/specification.md` §9, with the same edits to the design's §7 catalogue (design:348, 350, 357, 360, 368, 382, 393, 402):
- INPUT_IN_ANOTHER_ANALYSIS: "An input's file is inside its analysis's directory and outside every directory below it that holds an `astra.yaml` of its own". "Below" means `path: ., commit: X` doesn't catch the file. Use the same "below it" in §5 (:352-354).
- INVALID_REFERENCE: "Every address, reference, option reference and condition, other than a key of `Universe.decisions`, matches its grammar".
- WRONG_TYPE: "Every value has the JSON type its field takes: a string, a number, an integer, a list or a map". §5 (:450) already says `excluded: true` fails. Add to §4's Ids and maps that a list where a map is expected (`outputs: [f1_score]`) is WRONG_TYPE.
- DUPLICATE_KEY: "Every key of a map is distinct". §4 (:264-265) already says ids are unique because keys are.
- INVALID_PATH: "Every declaration's and input's `path` starts with neither `/` nor `~` and holds no backslash and no ASCII control character, and an input's `path` also has no `..` segment and doesn't end in `/`" (change 27).
- UNSUPPORTED_SPEC_VERSION: drop the parenthetical. §1 Versions says the file is then checked against that version's schema, at the schema layer.
- PIN_LIKE_COMMENT: drop "(needs the source text)". The note goes into §2's validator bullet, next to X12's RECOMMENDED sentence.
- UNDRIVEN_DECLARATION: take X13's self-contained wording, and check that it names its own subject.
- In "The catalogue as data", Rows: "Its Rule is one sentence". In `tools/rules.py`, reject a Rule cell whose rendered text, code spans aside, has a sentence break. Add a MALFORMED case beside X15's.
- Run `just gen-schema` and commit `rules.json`.

**Why this route.** Each wording restores a source the specification already has. RFC 0006 §1 says "outside any analysis within it". §4 (:235-237, :314-316) and tidy-up change 15 give `Universe.decisions` keys to INVALID_ID, and change 27 words the paths. §9 (:1031-1034) sets the convention. No document's validity changes, and no tool's duty does.

**Done when.** `just test` passes with the regenerated `rules.json`. No statement has a second sentence, or "such", "fails" or "needs". The new MALFORMED test rejects a two-sentence Rule cell.

### X12. Layer tables state what each layer reads and what each level obliges

- **Resolves:** F009, F102, F127, F149 · **Size:** S · **Decision:** no · **Done:** `1084fe5`

*Updated by X49 (2026-11-04): `PIN_LIKE_COMMENT` is dropped, so the RECOMMENDED sentence in §2, its §1 entry and the design §10:551 mapping below are withdrawn. F009 is resolved by the row's removal.*

**The problem.** The two layer tables in the specification misstate the layers. §2 says a checker sees only records and results, but `MISSING_RECORD`, `MISSING_RECORD_INPUTS`, `STALE_RESULT` and `STALE_EVIDENCE` need the stack's `astra.yaml` files, now and at each record's commit, and git (F102). §9 says that breaking any parse, schema or validator rule makes the file fail, SHOULDs included (F127), and gives a `MAY` row no outcome (F149). §2's "reports every … rule" makes `PIN_LIKE_COMMENT` mandatory, though RFC 0003 only recommends it (F009).

**The fix.**
- §2 layer table, check row, Sees: "every `astra.yaml` of the analysis stack, now and at each record's commit, and git; records and results". Checker bullet: "reads an analysis, every `astra.yaml` of its stack and git, and its records and results, and gives …". §7: "validators, checkers and runners can work offline from what they kept".
- §2 validator bullet: add "Reporting `PIN_LIKE_COMMENT` is RECOMMENDED, not required, since it needs the file's text and not only its data." Add this statement to §1's list of keyworded statements outside §9. The §9 row stays a SHOULD.
- §9 consequence table, parse/schema/validator row: "A broken `MUST` is reported as an **error** and makes the file invalid; a broken `SHOULD` is reported as a **warning**, and the file stays valid". The paragraph after the table becomes: "…changes what a result is. A parse, schema or validator rule decides whether a file is valid, and only through its `MUST`s."
- Under that table: "A `MAY` row states what a tool may do. Leaving it out breaks nothing and makes no tool non-conforming. The code is what a tool that does it reports. Only the runner table, whose rules are about a tool's own actions, has `MAY` rows." The Level bullet and the Rows paragraph of "The catalogue as data" say the same.
- `tools/rules.py`: accept `MAY` only in `### runner`. Elsewhere a `MAY` fails with "a MAY row is allowed only in the runner table".
- `tests/test_rules.py`: add malformed cases for a `MAY` row in the parse-and-schema, validator and check tables. The runner `NO_SANDBOX` row stays a valid case.
- Design §7 (Layers table, consequence table and paragraph) is changed to match. Design §10:551 maps the row to `PIN_LIKE_COMMENT` "(recommended)".
- Leave the runner row's "refuses" wording to X02 (F036, F038) and the meaning of levels in the check layer to X58 (F097).

**Why this route.** The sources already decide every point. RFC 0003:67-68 says an analysis "is valid, but … validators should warn", and RFC 0003:242 marks the pin-like comment check "warning (recommended)". RFC 0005 §2 (:128) puts the declaration "in the `astra.yaml` files at that commit". BCP 14 makes a `MAY` "truly optional". D9 and change 19 keep the three levels.

**Done when.** `just test` passes with the new malformed-level cases. `rules.json` is unchanged, since no row's level changes. Both layer tables in the specification match the design's.

### X13. One self-contained wording for NOT_BUILT_IN_UNIVERSE and UNDRIVEN_DECLARATION: per-universe outputs, addresses in the built analysis's file

- **Resolves:** F005, F035, F075 · **Partly:** F125 · **Size:** S · **Decision:** no · **Done:** `4fdb0da`

**The problem.** §7 (l.718-727) and the §9 rows `NOT_BUILT_IN_UNIVERSE` and `UNDRIVEN_DECLARATION` (l.1142-1143) paraphrase RFC 0004:410-411 in two different ways, and each loses part of it. Both apply to every output read without a pin, built once or not.
