So they reject an unpinned read of an undriven analysis's output built once, which §7 'Use' allows (F005). §7 judges "another analysis" element by element, from the file that uses it. Read that way, it rejects a driven analysis that reads its driver's own decision back through `..`, as `examples/iris_pipeline` does (F035). §9 says "every such read", which has no subject alone and leaves out decisions (F075, and part of F125).

**The fix.**
- §7 'The universe being built': replace the two bullets with this. Wherever an output built per universe is read without a pin, or a decision is consumed: (1) the analysis it belongs to is built in the universe being built. The analysis whose universe it is always is built there, so `iris.random_seed`, read through `..`, is allowed. (2) In the file of the analysis whose universe is being built, each such address of another analysis goes through a declaration that the file's universes drive. Uses in other files come under (1) alone. Then add a sentence: an output built once has one result, so it is read without a pin from any universe, through any declaration.
- Below the §7 example, add RFC 0004's failing case, `inputs: [inner_undriven.result]  # UNDRIVEN_DECLARATION`.
- §9 rows, each one self-contained sentence:
  - `NOT_BUILT_IN_UNIVERSE`: "Every output built per universe of another analysis read without a pin, and every decision of another analysis consumed, belongs to an analysis built in the universe being built".
  - `UNDRIVEN_DECLARATION`: "In the file of the analysis whose universe is being built, every address that reads another analysis's output built per universe without a pin, or consumes another analysis's decision, goes through a declaration that the file's universes drive".
- Run `just gen-schema` to regenerate `rules.json`.
- Design: copy the rows at :392-393. At :635, change the source to "0004:336, 0007 §3". In §13, note beside the 'no decisions' point (which stays open) that the driver's own elements are now settled.
- X11 leaves these two rows alone. X34 defines which declarations a key drives. That definition decides `stage.inner_undriven.result` in the root's file.

**Why this route.** RFC 0004:336 and RFC 0007 §3 already exclude outputs built once ("RFC 0004 already allows only these through a declaration that isn't driven"). RFC 0004:411 limits the rule to "the file of the analysis whose universe is being built", and its examples (:581, :618, :651) read the root's seed and a sibling's features from the stages' files. The fix restores the RFCs.

**Done when.** `just test` passes with the regenerated `rules.json`. Read by hand, the reworded rules accept `examples/iris_pipeline` and F005's `upstream.stage.report` read, and reject F075's two stacks and `inner_undriven.result`.

### X14. Generator: guarded, typed class-level checks that compile under Ajv strict

- **Resolves:** F079, F119, F139 · **Partly:** F062 · **Size:** M · **Decision:** no · **Done:** `53d605e`

**The problem.** The generator copies LinkML's class-level checks as they are: the `exactly_one_of` `oneOf` and the class rule's `if`/`then` on `AnalysisDeclaration` and `Evidence` only test whether a key is there. A `required` passes when the value isn't an object, and a key whose value is null or has the wrong type still counts as present. So one wrong-type mistake also brings INVALID_DECLARATION_SOURCE, INVALID_EVIDENCE_SOURCE or URL_WITHOUT_COMMIT, sometimes at the same pointer (F079, F119). The Evidence rule also puts each `properties` beside its `not` rather than inside it, so `analysis.schema.json` fails to compile under Ajv `strict: true`, although §10 says strict validators compile it (F139).

**The fix.**
- `tools/jsonschema_generator.py` (new step 5) moves each class-level check into its own member of `allOf`. Each member is guarded: `{if: G, then: {oneOf: […]}}`, and for the rule `{if: G + the rule's if, then: the rule's then}`. G is `{type: object, properties: {f: {type: <f's JSON type>}}}` over the fields that check reads. A check is then skipped only when a `type` keyword already fails, so no document's verdict changes. Each `required` the generator writes gets `properties: {f: {}}` beside it in the same object, inside the `not` too.
- `tools/rules.py` walks `allOf`. `allOf/N/if` gets the member's code (Ajv reports it), and so do `allOf/N/then/oneOf`, its branches' `required`, and `allOf/N/then/required` or `/not`. Nothing inside an `if` or a `not` gets a code.
- Run `just gen-schema`. In §10, the example key becomes `…/allOf/1/then/required`, and `allOf` joins the list of applicators. §10's "strict validators (such as Ajv)" stays as written.
- Add invalid WRONG_TYPE fixtures for: a string declaration, a string evidence item, `url: null` beside `path`, and `version: null` beside `artifact`.
- Add a pinned Ajv under `tools/ajv/` and a `just check-ajv` recipe that compiles all three schemas with `strict: true`. Run it in `main.yaml` (setup-node).
- Drop the Analysis-159 item in design §12 and the `strictRequired` caveat in `no-python-models.md:70-72`. Describe step 5 in design "The generator extension" and in CLAUDE.md.

**Why this route.** This is generator and test work. It only stops a check from running where `type` already fails, so it changes no verdict. It also makes the contract give what §4 promises: a value of the wrong type is WRONG_TYPE, and "no field takes `null`".

**Done when.** The four new fixtures and Analysis-159 each report one code at one pointer. Every existing fixture keeps its verdict. `just check-ajv` compiles all three schemas under `strict: true` in CI.

### X15. Harden the §9 extractor: cell count without blockquote prefix, keyword check on rendered text, a failing test for every check

- **Resolves:** F064, F138, F150 · **Size:** M · **Decision:** no · **Done:** `61ffcbe`

**The problem.** The §9 extractor in `tools/rules.py` checks the raw source rather than what the parser reads and renders. `_source_cell_count` splits the raw line, so a blockquote's `>` counts as a cell and a well-formed quoted rule table fails (F138). The keyword/level check joins tokens with spaces, so a keyword split across tokens or by an invisible character goes unseen: `MU*ST*`, `[MU](#x)ST`, `` `MU`ST ``, `MU<span></span>ST`, `MU&shy;ST` and `MU`+U+200B+`ST` all pass (F150). On top of that, `tests/test_rules.py` has no failing case for four of the extractor's checks, or for its disallowed-token branch. A refactor can drop any of them and the suite stays green (F064).

**The fix.**
- `tools/rules.py` `_source_cell_count`: before `escapedSplit`, strip the line's container prefix as CommonMark reads it: repeatedly, up to 3 spaces, `>` and one optional space. Use a short loop, not a regular expression (CLAUDE.md). The count stays on the source line, so an extra cell is still caught. markdown-it pads short rows and truncates long ones, so counting tokens would miss it.
- `tools/rules.py` keyword check: also read the cell as it renders. Join the `text` and `code_inline` contents with no separator, drop `html_inline` (markdown-it has already decoded entities), and remove Unicode format characters (category `Cf`, which covers U+00AD and U+200B). Fail on the union of the keywords found there and in today's space-joined `_text`, so `` see`SHOULD` `` and `[the](#a)SHOULD` are still caught.
- `tests/test_rules.py` `MALFORMED`, new cases:
  - subsections out of order;
  - two rule tables in one subsection;
  - a repeated column (`| Source | Source |`);
  - `\*output\*` in a Rule cell ("can't be written back");
  - an image in a Rule cell (matched on "holds a image");
  - a quoted row with an extra cell;
  - the six splits above, each failing `LOOSE_PIN.*MUST`.
- A new passing test: a `>`-quoted runner table extracts the same rules as the unquoted one.
- §9 "The catalogue as data": Columns gets "each once", and Rows says "as the Rule cell reads once rendered" for the keyword check. X24 edits the same paragraph, so make both changes in one pass.
- Bring `tools/rules.py`'s module docstring (design §12) and CLAUDE.md's list of extraction errors up to date: add order, one rule table per subsection, a repeated column, the cell count and the rebuild check.

**Why this route.** This is tooling and tests. The extractor is brought up to what §9's "The catalogue as data" already requires: "one cell per column", and "uses no RFC 2119 keyword in capitals … of another level". No ASTRA file's validity changes, and nothing changes for any tool other than `just gen-schema`.

**Done when.** Each of F064's four mutations of `tools/rules.py`, and removing the disallowed-token branch, makes `pytest tests/test_rules.py` fail. The quoted-table and split-keyword tests pass. `just check-generated` shows `rules.json` unchanged.

### X16. Environment.iri as an RFC 3987 IRI, and the record's RDF reading defined by JSON-LD 1.1 toRdf with a prefix-free context

- **Resolves:** F021, F070, F148 · **Partly:** F061, F161 · **Size:** M · **Decision:** decided · **Done:** `c05c40d`

**The problem.** The specification never defines the record's RDF reading. §10 points at LinkML's RDF conversion, which no test runs, and which crashes on any `iri` without `://`, §8's own `urn:` example included (F021). The stock context makes `astra`, `linkml`, `prov`, `schema` and `xsd` prefix terms, so an `iri` with one of those schemes becomes another IRI (F070). The `iri` pattern accepts strings that RFC 3987 rejects, while the spec says "absolute IRI" and RFC 0005 says "an IRI" (F061). §10 calls the root node "the record", though in PROV it can only be the result (F148).

**The fix.**
- Spec §10, **RDF**: define the reading once. A record's RDF is what the JSON-LD 1.1 toRdf algorithm gives for it, with `provenance.context.jsonld` supplied as its context (a record carries no `@context`), plus each node's class URI as its `rdf:type`: the root, each `inputs` item and the `environment` are `prov:Entity`, `generated_by` a `prov:Activity`, `runner` a `prov:SoftwareAgent`. No term carries JSON-LD 1.1's prefix flag, so an `iri` is its node's `@id` exactly as written. The root node stands for the result: its `address`, `content_hash` and `size` describe the result, its `spec_version` the record. Drop "through LinkML's RDF conversion". The paragraph also carries X03's `xsd:dateTime` sentence and X39's `oa:refinedBy` sentence: the three edits are made in one pass. §8's closing sentence becomes "Read as RDF (§10), a record is W3C PROV-O."
- `tools/`: a step after `gen-jsonld-context`, run by `just gen-schema`, drops the five prefix terms, writes every `@id` and `@type` as a full IRI and adds `"@version": 1.1`, so that a JSON-LD 1.0 processor fails rather than reading `commit:env` as `https://w3id.org/astra/commitenv`.
- `provenance.yaml`, `Environment.iri`: `range: string` (not `uri`: LinkML emits `"format": "uri"`, which Ajv strict rejects). Pattern per the decision below; under (i), `^[A-Za-z][A-Za-z0-9+.-]*:(C|%[0-9A-Fa-f]{2})+(#(C|%[0-9A-Fa-f]{2})*)?$(?!\n)` with C = `[^\x00-\x20\x7f-\x9f<>"{}|\\^`%#‎‏‪-‮]`. Description: "An IRI (RFC 3987), never a CURIE, naming exactly that environment …; the schema checks its scheme, characters and percent-encoding, not its structure (authority, port, IP literal)." §8 "absolute IRI" becomes "an IRI (RFC 3987)". `ProvenanceRecord`'s description: its RDF node stands for the result. Design §13: the structural gap. Design §12: separating record from result (a `prov:Bundle`).
- Fixture `ProvenanceRecord-217-iri-forbidden-character.yaml`: `iri: "urn:x<y>"` (INVALID_VALUE, `/generated_by/environment/iri`).
- Test (rdflib in the dev group): convert both valid record fixtures, §8's example, and records with `astra:`, `prov:`, `xsd:`, `commit:`, `size:` and `tag:` schemes; check each node's type, that `prov:used` gives the `iri` as written, and that no term is a prefix. Update `test_context_maps_the_record_onto_prov`.
- CLAUDE.md and design §3, §4, §6, §10: the context is post-processed.
- For X41: under (i), the class still admits for `iri` non-ASCII spaces, format characters such as U+200B, U+2028/U+2029, private use and noncharacters (under (ii), only the spaces and format characters RFC 3987 allows; with neither, the bidi controls too). X41's §12 item describes `iri` by this class, not by `[^\x00-\x20\x7f]`.

**Decision** (Tom, 2026-11-04: decided as recommended). Context: (a) post-process it, as above, so every scheme keeps its IRI and validity is unchanged; (b) keep the stock context, declare `urn:` and advise against prefix-named schemes, which runner authors must track as the context grows. Recommended: (a). Pattern: (i) RFC 3987's characters, as above, with the structural gap recorded; private use is legal only in a query, so a character class can't place it; (ii) RFC 3987's full grammar as the pattern, closing F061 entirely at the cost of a long pattern in the shared regex subset. Recommended: (i).

**Why this route.** RFC 0005 says the schema checks "an IRI for the environment" (l.142), "an IRI naming exactly that environment" (l.122), and that "converted to RDF through the schema, a record is PROV-O" (l.132). The pattern is a contract bug and the spec's route and wording are drift; no tool writes records yet.

**Done when.** Fixture 217 fails at its pointer; the conversion test passes on the fixtures, §8's example and the prefix-named schemes; `just check-generated` is clean.

### X17. `url` follows change 25: no one-letter-host exclusion, no bare `scheme://`, a two-sentence description

- **Resolves:** F011, F129, F155 · **Size:** S · **Decision:** decided · **Done:** `82d4098`

**The problem.** Change 25 says a `url` is in one of git's two remote forms, `scheme://...` or `[user@]host:path`, and nothing more. The contract says more in two places and less in a third. The pattern's guard `(?![A-Za-z]:)` rejects a value that starts with a letter and `:` (`C:/repo`), and §7 and design §4 justify it with "a host of one letter is not scp-like ... as git itself does", which matches neither the pattern (`git@c:repo` passes) nor git on Linux and macOS (F011). The scp-like branch also matches every `scheme://` value, so a bare `https://` passes (F155). The description has three sentences and repeats §7 (F129).

**The fix.**
- `src/astra/schema/analysis.yaml`, `AnalysisDeclaration.url`: the pattern becomes `'^(?:[A-Za-z][A-Za-z0-9+.-]*://[^\x00-\x20\x7f]+|(?![A-Za-z][A-Za-z0-9+.-]*://)(?:[^\x00-\x20\x7f/@:]+@)?[^\x00-\x20\x7f/@:]+:[^\x00-\x20\x7f]+)$(?!\n)'`. The drive-letter guard goes; the new lookahead sends a value that starts `scheme://` to the scheme branch. Checked with Python `re`: `https://`, `file://`, `ssh://`, `git+ssh://`, `a://` fail; `C:/repo`, `c:repo`, `git@g:owner/repo`, `https:/` and every Analysis-005 value pass.
- The description becomes: "Where git fetches another repository from, as a remote: `scheme://…`, such as `https://github.com/owner/analysis` or `file:///srv/repo`, or `[user@]host:path`, such as `git@github.com:owner/repo`. The declared analysis is the one at the top level of that repository, at `commit`."
- Spec §7, `url` paragraph: delete "A host of one letter is not scp-like, so that a Windows drive path is never read as a remote, as git itself does.", and take `C:/repo` out of the list of non-urls. §3's sentence from X35 says Windows paths are out of scope.
- Design §4, lines 232-241, become:

  > `AnalysisDeclaration.url` takes one of git's two remote forms:
  > `scheme://…` (`https://github.com/owner/analysis`, `ssh://git@host/x.git`,
  > `file:///srv/repo`) or scp-like `[user@]host:path` (`git@github.com:owner/repo`),
  > with the pattern `^(?:[A-Za-z][A-Za-z0-9+.-]*://[^\x00-\x20\x7f]+|(?![A-Za-z][A-Za-z0-9+.-]*://)(?:[^\x00-\x20\x7f/@:]+@)?[^\x00-\x20\x7f/@:]+:[^\x00-\x20\x7f]+)$(?!\n)`.
  > A value that starts `scheme://` must have something after `://`.
  > A bare word or a local path (`a`, `owner/repo`, `../repo`) fails:
  > an analysis inside the root's repository is declared by `path`, and another
  > repository on this machine by an explicit `file://` URL. Whether the URL leads to a valid ASTRA project is the
  > validator's (`REMOTE_UNREACHABLE`, `MISSING_DECLARED_ANALYSIS`,
  > `DECLARED_ANALYSIS_INVALID`).

  If X28 lands first, make this edit inside its pass.
- Tests: delete `tests/data/invalid/Analysis-180-url-drive-path.yaml`. Add `Analysis-NNN-url-bare-scheme.yaml` (`url: "https://"`, `# rule: INVALID_VALUE`, `# fails at: /analyses/up/url`). Add `C:/repo` and `git@g:owner/repo` to `Analysis-005-declarations.yaml` as valid entries.
- Run `just gen-schema` and commit `generated/`.

**Decision** (Tom, 2026-11-04: decided as recommended). Tom's decision 4, still to be confirmed. (a) Drop the exclusion (recommended): `C:/repo` is a remote at host `C`, as git reads it outside Windows, and fails as REMOTE_UNREACHABLE; change 25 already reads this way. (b) Keep the start-of-value guard: its prose must say "a value starting `<letter>:` is a drive path, as Git for Windows reads it", and change 25 needs a clause back (tidy-up RFC). (c) Exclude every one-letter host, `user@` included: tidy-up RFC, and it rejects ssh aliases git fetches. (a) follows decision 1: Windows-only problems are by design.

**Why this route.** Change 25 allows exactly "one of git's two remote forms: `scheme://...` ... or `[user@]host:path`" and has no one-letter clause. Rejecting a bare `scheme://` narrows nothing: git's `connect.c` tests `strstr(url, "://")` before the scp-like parse, so such a value is only ever the scheme form; change 25 writes that form `scheme://...`, and both the design's pattern (`://\S+`) and the contract's scheme branch (`[^\x00-\x20\x7f]+`) require something after `://`. The scp branch's overlap is the bug that defeats that `+`. CLAUDE.md sets descriptions at one or two sentences.

**Done when.** `just test` passes with Analysis-180 gone, the bare-scheme fixture failing at `/analyses/up/url` with INVALID_VALUE, and Analysis-005 accepting `C:/repo` and `git@g:owner/repo`. `grep -n -i -e 'one.letter' -e 'C:/repo' docs/specification.md design/2026-11-03-v0.1-schema-design.md src/astra/schema/analysis.yaml src/astra/schema/generated/analysis.schema.json` finds nothing (Analysis-005's valid entry is outside these files).

### X18. Output's descriptions say exactly what `inputs` and `decisions` list; OutputType in house style; examples follow

- **Resolves:** F008, F114, F128 · **Size:** S · **Decision:** decided · **Done:** `6f05a84`

**The problem.** Output's descriptions say less than the rules do, or something else. `Output.inputs` keeps RFC 0003 §2's opening sentence ("may pin an artifact reference") and drops its restriction of pins to outputs, so it contradicts `PIN_ON_INPUT` (F008). `Output.decisions` ("depends on") says neither in which universe nor whether decisions reached through inputs belong, and the iris examples answer both ways (F114). `OutputType`, carried over from `main`, has fragments with no full stop and US spelling (F128).

**The fix.**
- `analysis.yaml`, `Output.inputs`: "What the output is made from, in order: references to inputs and outputs of the analysis or of analyses it declares. A reference to an output can be pinned to a universe with `#`; a reference to an input can't."
- `analysis.yaml`, `Output.decisions`: "The addresses of the decisions whose option the output is made with directly, not through what it reads: a different option for any of them, in the universe being built, can give a different result."
- Spec §4 `decisions` bullet (l.387-389): the same definition; it keeps "Their order carries no meaning." Then, descriptively: "Listing a decision that reaches the output through an unpinned input gives the output the same results, though listing a decision of another analysis consumes it, and the address it is listed under is then held to §7's rules on consuming ([`UNDRIVEN_DECLARATION`](#validator)). Listing a decision that only a pinned input brings in makes the output built per universe (§6), so a comparison between universes lists none." No rule is added.
- `OutputType`: "The kind of artefact an output is."; values "A metric or measurement.", "A figure or visualisation.", "A table of data.", "A data file or dataset.", "A report or document."
- Examples: drop `decisions` from `accuracy`, `f1_score` and `confusion_matrix` in `examples/iris`, from `feature_plot` in `iris_pipeline/feature_extraction`, and from `accuracy` in `iris_pipeline/classification`. Each still reads an unpinned per-universe output, so each stays built per universe; every dropped decision is the analysis's own, so no warning changes.
- `just gen-schema`; commit `generated/`.

**Decision** (Tom, 2026-11-04: decided as recommended). F114 needs a convention. (a) Only the decisions the output is made with directly (recommended). (b) Every decision that reaches it. (c) Every decision of its own analysis that reaches it, plus another analysis's only when used directly: what the RFC examples do (RFC 0003 l.257-264 `f1_score`; RFC 0004 l.609-616 `feature_plot`, l.641-648 `accuracy`, each listing decisions its command never names). Errors are the same under all three. Warnings differ under (b): `classification.accuracy` would list `iris.test_split`, which consumes it (§7 l.683-688), tying the output to `iris` (RFC 0004 l.245), and consuming lifts `NO_UNIVERSES` (§9 l.1151). So `iris_pipeline/classification`, validated alone, is warned under (a) and (c) but not under (b). That is the strongest case for (a): a list never silently binds an analysis meant to stand alone. (a) also matches `main`'s "Decision IDs ... that parameterize this output" and the spec's own §2 example (l.121-130), and needs no upkeep when an upstream output changes. It departs from the RFC examples' style, which (c) keeps.

**Why this route.** RFC 0003 §2 (l.181-182) restricts pins to outputs, and l.196-201 already decide what listing does; §7 decides what consuming does. The fix restates them and changes nothing that is valid.

**Done when.** `just test` passes. `analysis.schema.json` carries the new descriptions. No `astra.yaml` under `examples/` and no example in the specification lists a decision outside the chosen convention. README.md, docs/index.md, docs/getting-started.md and docs/llms.txt still use the pre-0.1 format, outside this fix.

### X19. State the parse layer exactly in §2, §3 and §10

- **Resolves:** F065, F144 · **Partly:** F140, F142, F143 · **Size:** S · **Decision:** no · **Done:** `6d7273d`

**The problem.** The specification gives the parse layer to "any strict YAML 1.2 loader" (§2) and then describes the core schema only loosely. §3 leaves out maps and sequences and decides numbers by what "looks like a number", not by YAML 1.2.2 §10.3.2's table (F144). It never says that a YAML integer or float is a JSON number, typed as JSON Schema types it, which is what change 29's "the core schema's types are JSON's" means (F140). It also credits the `%YAML` rule to YAML and to loaders (§2, §10), though YAML 1.2.2 §6.8.1 requires a 1.2 processor to accept `%YAML 1.1` (F065). Stock loaders that call themselves YAML 1.2 therefore read files differently from the contract.

**The fix.**
- §3, the `%YAML` bullet: "a `%YAML` directive other than `%YAML 1.2` makes the file invalid. This is ASTRA's rule, not YAML's: YAML 1.2.2 accepts `%YAML 1.1` (§6.8.1), so a tool checks the directive itself." A key written twice is two keys that are equal YAML nodes, with the same tag and canonical form (§3.2.1.3). So `a`, `'a'` and `"\x61"` are one key, and `1` and `"1"` are two.
- §3, the types bullet: "the core schema's only scalar types are strings, nulls, booleans, integers and floats; its integers and floats are JSON numbers, and a field's type is checked as JSON Schema checks it, so `2.0` is an integer."
- §3, the numbers bullet: replace "a plain scalar that looks like a number" with "an untagged plain scalar that matches a row of YAML 1.2.2 §10.3.2's table is that type, and any other is a string". Give examples on each side: `1e3`, `0x1F`, `-.5` and `010` (10) are numbers, while `1_000`, `0b101`, `12:30` and `+.nan` are strings. Widen the commit-id warning: an all-digit id is an integer, and one of digits with a single `e` is a float.
- §2's parse row: "a YAML 1.2 loader under the core schema, plus §3's checks". Make the same change to the design's copy of the row (design:302).
- §10: change ~1262 to "because loaders and §3's checks report them". Add a short **Reading `astra.yaml`** paragraph saying that ruamel's stock safe loader and js-yaml's `CORE_SCHEMA` depart from the table (`1_000`, `0b101`). It also gives the `yaml` package's options, `{version: '1.2', schema: 'core', uniqueKeys: true, merge: false}`, together with a check of `doc.directives.yaml`.
- Tidy-up change 29, wording only: "the core schema's scalar types are JSON's (its integers and floats are numbers)". "So does a `%YAML` directive" becomes a separate sentence that names it as ASTRA's rule.
- `tests/conftest.py`: the `_CoreSchemaScanner` docstring cites §6.8.1. `tests/test_loader.py` gets one case for each example in §3.

**Why this route.** Change 29 already says that a file "means the same as the JSON it converts to, which is what the schema checks", and §3 already reads files "under the core schema only". These edits state those two sentences exactly and change no verdict. F140's `.inf`/`.nan` half changes what is valid, so it goes to X31. The `!` and key-comparison changes in the harness go to X10.

**Done when.** The phrases "any strict YAML 1.2 loader" and "looks like a number" no longer appear in the specification or the design. Each example in §3 loads as stated in `tests/test_loader.py`. `just test` passes with no change to `generated/`.

### X20. Evidence selectors: 'the cited work or output', and RFC 8118 for PDF fragments

- **Resolves:** F123, F146 · **Partly:** F057, F080 · **Size:** S · **Decision:** no · **Done:** `ff7fc8b`

**The problem.** Evidence cites either a work, by `doi`, or an output, as an `artifact`. Its selectors apply to both kinds: the module header and §5 say so, and the schema allows them on both. But the selector descriptions use the DOI branch's wording, "the cited work", so read literally they cover only `doi` evidence (F123). The same descriptions, §5, the design and tidy-up change 4 cite RFC 3778 for the PDF fragment syntax. RFC 8118 obsoleted RFC 3778 in 2017, and the obsolete text doesn't say where `page=` starts counting (F146). Both are wording slips. No document validates differently.

**The fix.**
- `src/astra/schema/insight.yaml`: `TextQuoteSelector` becomes "A passage of the cited work or output, quoted exactly, ..."; `exact` becomes "The passage, exactly as the cited work or output writes it."; `Evidence.quote` becomes "The passage of the cited work or output the claim rests on."; `Evidence.location` becomes "The place in the cited work or output the claim rests on, such as `page=6`." `doi` and `version` keep "the cited work", since they belong to the DOI branch.
- `FragmentSelector` becomes "A place in a PDF, given as a PDF fragment identifier (RFC 8118 §3), such as `page=6`." `value` becomes "The PDF fragment identifier (RFC 8118 §3), without the leading `#`, such as `page=6` for the first page counted from 1." (The second sentence sits within the two-sentence limit.) The PDF-only scope stays as it is. X39 settles that scope.
- Spec §5, evidence paragraph: "whose `exact` is the passage as the work or output writes it", and "a PDF fragment identifier ([RFC 8118 §3](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8118#section-3), which defers to ISO 32000-2) without the `#`, such as `page=6`; pages count from 1".
- Design §5 table row (:274): "described as an RFC 8118 PDF fragment (`page=6`)". Below it, add one clause: Web Annotation still names this syntax by the RFC 3778 IRI, which matters only if `conformsTo` is ever added.
- Tidy-up change 4: "For a PDF `value` is a fragment identifier as RFC 8118 §3 gives it, such as `page=6`, ...". This is an editorial citation fix.
- `just gen-schema`, then commit `generated/analysis.schema.json`. `rules.json` doesn't change.

**Why this route.** Tidy-up change 4 and §5 already apply selectors to every evidence item, and change 4 already defines `value` as the PDF fragment syntax. The fix corrects that syntax's citation and the wording. It doesn't change what is valid, since `value` takes any string and no rule checks it.

**Done when.** `grep -rn "RFC 3778\|3778"` finds nothing in `docs/specification.md`, `src/astra/schema/` or design :274, and finds "cited work" only on `doi` and `version`. `just test` passes, `check-generated` included, with no fixture changed.

### X21. Tests pin every constraint with a hand-written table of negative cases

- **Resolves:** F031 · **Size:** M · **Depends on:** X01, X17 · **Decision:** no · **Done:** `6cc163e`

**The problem.** The suite asks for one invalid fixture per code, not one per constraint. In the closed schemas one code covers many keywords (`WRONG_TYPE` 120, `INVALID_ID` 21), and only 71 of their 231 error-map keywords are reached by any fixture. So a `required` entry, an `integer`, a `minimum`, a typed slot's type, a map key's type, a facet of a slot's own pattern or a slot's code can be loosened in the LinkML source with `just test` green (F031). Any guard that reads the generated schema or the sources is no help here, since the mutant edits them too. The guard has to be a table written by hand.

**The fix.**
- New `tests/test_constraints.py` with a table, `NEGATIVE`. Each row is a valid fixture, a JSON Pointer, an edit (delete the field, or set a value) and the code we expect. The test applies each row's edit and checks, through the committed closed schema, that the edit fails at that pointer with that code, the way `test_invalid_fixture` checks a fixture. Record rows also run against the open schema. We can draft the rows with a script, but every row is reviewed and committed by hand. Once committed, a loosened constraint makes its row pass validation, and the test fails.
- In the same file, a meta-test that every closed error-map location in `rules.json`, and every entry of every `required` list (keyed by location and property name), is reached by some row. Its allow-list is empty once X01 drops `required: []`. This means a new constraint needs a new row.
- Add accept/reject rows, as in `test_types`, for each facet stated for a slot's own pattern: `format` with a leading dot; `doi` with fewer than four registrant digits; a `url` or `iri` with a space; a declaration path with a tab; `excluded: "\t"`; an `Input.path` of `~/data.csv`, `..` or `data/..`; and a trailing `\n` on each. Fix `test_types.py:55` so its `sha1:` reject has 64 hex digits and fails on the prefix alone.
- `tests/test_contract.py`: a hand-written `TYPED` table (class, slot) → type, checked both ways against the sources' ranges. Each slot's generated pattern and code must equal its type's. Every map key's `propertyNames` pattern must equal `id`'s, except `Universe.decisions`, which takes `address`. Every map key's code must be `INVALID_ID`. This replaces the bare `isinstance` check at `test_contract.py:195`.
- `test_provenance.py`: check `@type` for every typed term (`size` takes `xsd:integer`).
- `CLAUDE.md:50` and `:65`: every schema constraint needs a row in `NEGATIVE`, and a new code also needs an invalid fixture.

**Why this route.** This adds tests and a CLAUDE.md sentence and changes nothing that is valid. It makes good on what `test_contract.py:1` and design changes line 87 already claim: "Guards so the contract can't loosen quietly".

**Done when.** We re-run each mutant in F031's table, then `just gen-schema && just test`, and every one fails. The meta-test is green with an empty allow-list.

### X22. The wheel ships exactly its artefacts

- **Resolves:** F063 · **Size:** S · **Decision:** no · **Done:** `6c7bd70`

**The problem.** D7 says the package ships only artefacts, and `CLAUDE.md` names them: the LinkML sources, `generated/`, and `astra/docs/llms.txt`. The wheel also ships `astra/schema/README.md`, a leftover from the project template, because `[tool.hatch.build.targets.wheel] packages = ["src/astra"]` packs everything under `src/astra`. The test that guards D7, `test_wheel_ships_only_schema_artefacts`, accepts any name under `astra/schema/`. A stray file, or a deleted module such as `universe.yaml` left behind, would ship and the test would still pass (F063).

**The fix.**
- Delete `src/astra/schema/README.md`. It says nothing that `CLAUDE.md` doesn't, and it leaves out `generated/`.
- In `tests/test_package.py`, make `test_wheel_ships_only_schema_artefacts` compare the wheel's names outside `*.dist-info/` with one exact set, written once at module level: `astra/schema/{types,analysis,insight,provenance}.yaml`, `astra/schema/generated/{analysis.schema.json,provenance.schema.json,provenance.open.schema.json,provenance.context.jsonld,rules.json}` and `astra/docs/llms.txt`. On a mismatch it reports the names missing and the names extra. The `.py` check becomes redundant and goes. `test_wheel_ships_the_contract` and `test_wheel_ships_the_sources` are then covered by the same set: fold them in, or have them read the shared constant.
- Leave the hatch target as it is. The exact-set test fails on any extra file, an untracked one in a developer's tree included, so restricting the target with `only-include` adds a second list to keep in step and catches nothing more.

**Why this route.** D7 (design §2) and `CLAUDE.md` ("The package ships only artefacts ... plus `astra/docs/llms.txt`") already say what the wheel holds. This change makes the package and its test match that. It changes nothing about what is valid or what tools do.

**Done when.** `src/astra/schema/README.md` is gone, and `uv run pytest tests/test_package.py` passes. Planting an untracked `src/astra/schema/stray.yaml` makes the test fail, and the failure names that file.

### X23. Restate the Terms rows, §4's `id` row and Addresses parenthetical, and §7's "built once" from their sources

- **Resolves:** F076, F089, F104, F117, F152 · **Partly:** F084 · **Size:** S · **Decision:** no · **Done:** `5bb1010`

**The problem.** Six sentences restate a definition or §4's grammar more loosely than their source. The Terms table gives an option an address and puts the pin after a reference (F089). It also counts a result in every universe for an output with a `when` (F104). §4's `id` row reads as excluding the three fields it covers (F076). §4's Addresses parenthetical puts a condition's `~` inside its address (F152). §7 uses "built once" in its plain sense, beside the defined term (F117). The contract is right in every case; only the wording is loose.

**The fix.** One wording pass in `docs/specification.md` and the design, each sentence rewritten from its source:
- §1 Terms, `address`: "the name of an element other than an option, through the declarations that lead to it; an option is named by an option reference (§4)".
- §1 Terms, `pin`: "`#` and a universe id ending a reference, after its address, which reads the output in that universe".
- §1 Terms, `built per universe, built once`: "whether an output has one result in each universe where it is active, or one in all".
- §4 Named types, `id` row, Used by: "every map key but those of `Universe.decisions`; also `Universe.derived_from`, `Option.insights` and the values of `Universe.decisions`". Design §4 `id` row (design:196) the same.
- §4 Addresses, the parenthetical: "(In an option reference, the address is the segments before the last, which is the option; a condition's leading `~` and a reference's `#` pin are not part of the address.)"
- §7, the end of the same-directory sentence: "it is read once, and each of its results is made at most once, however many declarations reach it." Design traceability row (design:571) to match. X04 rewrites the first half of this sentence (identity) and keeps this clause.
- §8 Results, after the planned-results sentence: "An output plans no result in a universe where it is inactive (§5)." X52 later defines planned results in full.
- §8 Statuses: "the address, pinned when …" becomes "the reference, pinned when …". If X07 lands first, its rewrite of this sentence already does this.

**Why this route.** Each sentence returns to text the sources already fix: the grammar of §4 (RFC 0004 §2, RFC 0003 §2: `artifact-id [ "#" universe-id ]`), RFC 0003's "has a `when` (whether it exists depends on the universe)", and §5's "An inactive output isn't built in that universe". Nothing valid changes, and no tool does anything new.

**Done when.** `git diff` touches only `docs/specification.md` and the design. `grep` finds no "built once per universe", "name of an element," or "or a condition, the address". `just test` passes, and `just check-generated` shows no change: §9 is untouched, so `rules.json` is too.

### X24. §9 cells as data: one Markdown dialect, a defined Source form, and complete Source cells

- **Resolves:** F082, F132, F135, F136 · **Size:** M · **Decision:** no · **Done:** `680ed8b`

**The problem.** §9 says what a cell may hold, but not which Markdown it is read in, or what form each column takes. The extractor reads CommonMark with GFM tables, and the site renders Python-Markdown with Zensical's extensions. So a cell can read one way in `rules.json` and another on the page (F135). §10 calls only `statement` Markdown and glosses `status` as "the status a result gets", which doesn't fit `STALE_EVIDENCE` (F136). The Source column has no defined form. Its cells leave out sources (`tidy-up`, `main`, 0004) and put only `main` in a code span (F082), and §9 calls the pull request `#73` an issue (F132).

**The fix.**
- §9 "Source" bullet: "every source of the rule, as plain comma-separated tokens: the RFCs it comes from, and the section of this specification that explains it."
- §9 token paragraph: `tidy-up` is the draft RFC *Settling the schema for v0.1*. `#73` is a pull request of the specification's repository, linked. Tokens are plain text, never code spans.
- §9 "Cells" bullet: cells are inline Markdown as CommonMark with GFM tables reads them, and the site shows them as the same text.
- Source cells, and the design §7 copies: INVALID_COMMIT `0007, tidy-up`; INVALID_PATH `0004, 0006, tidy-up`; INVALID_VALUE `0005, 0007, tidy-up, main, #73`; MISSING_FIELD `0004, 0005, 0006, tidy-up, main`; UNKNOWN_OPTION `0003, 0004`. Add `tidy-up` to INVALID_ID (with `main`), DUPLICATE_ENTRY, AMBIGUOUS_ID, WRONG_TYPE, UNKNOWN_FIELD and INVALID_RECORD. Write `main` plain everywhere. Add a `MISSING_FIELD` row to design §10, and fix design line 672 ("#73" is a pull request).
- §10 field list: `statement`, `source` and, in the check layer, `status` are the Rule, Source and Status when broken cells, as inline CommonMark with each link reduced to its text. `status` is display text naming "the status a result, or evidence citing it, gets", not a status token. Use the same gloss in §9's check preamble. In the §10 example, `"source": "0007, tidy-up"`.
- `tools/rules.py`: check that a Source cell parses to plain text, and that each of its tokens is an RFC number with an optional `§n`, `tidy-up`, `main`, `#n` or `§n`. Add a MALFORMED test for this check, and list it in CLAUDE.md.
- New `tests/test_site.py`, run in `test_pages_build.yaml` after `zensical build` and skipped when there is no `site/`. It compares the text of every §9 cell on the built page with the matching `rules.json` field rendered by markdown-it. A test is used rather than pinning `markdown_extensions`, because pinning can't fix Python-Markdown's core differences (`\|` in code spans, a table right after a paragraph).
- Run `just gen-schema`.

**Why this route.** Tidy-up change 19 says each rule has "the RFC it comes from", and D13 makes §9 the hand-written source of `rules.json`. This fix states the existing cell format and credits the existing sources. No document's validity changes. Only `rules.json`'s `source` strings change.

**Done when.** `just test` passes with the regenerated `rules.json`, and no `source` contains a backtick. The new MALFORMED test rejects a bad Source token. The site test reports no differences on the built page.

### X25. Spec examples declare what they name: parent `iris`, and §7's example drives `u1` by a flat key

- **Resolves:** F116, F133 · **Size:** S · **Decision:** no · **Done:** `b742dc6`

**The problem.** Some examples name things the page never declares. Three examples address through `root.` (`root.data` in §5, `root.a.a1` in §5's conditional elements, `"entry": "root.data"` in §8's record example), and so does `RecordedInput.entry`'s description. These addresses come from RFC 0004's running example, which declares the parent as `{ id: root, path: .. }`, but the specification drops that declaration. `root` is also a §1 defined term, and §4 calls the same parent `iris` (F133). §7's composition example pins `inner.result#u1` but has no `universes` block, so `u1` is never declared and no example in the specification shows a flat key, the one form that drives (F116).

**The fix.**
- `docs/specification.md` §5 (Inputs, :355): `root.data` becomes `iris.data`.
- §5 Conditional elements (:460): "by its address (`root.a.a1`)" becomes "by its address, as in the condition `iris.random_seed.seed_42`". That decision is the one §4 (:300) and `examples/iris_pipeline` declare.
- §8 record example (:932): `"entry": "iris.data"`.
- `src/astra/schema/provenance.yaml` `RecordedInput.entry`: "such as `iris.data`". Run `just gen-schema` and commit the regenerated `provenance.schema.json` and `provenance.open.schema.json`, since the description reaches them.
- §7 composition example (:739-752): add `spec_version: "0.1.0"` at the top. At the end, add a block-style universe that declares `u1` and drives `inner` with a flat key:
  ```yaml
  universes:
    u1:
      decisions:
        inner.d: d1                       # a flat key: inner's decision d
  ```
  With `spec_version` the block counts as a whole document, so `tests/test_specification.py` checks it against the contract. The review checked this exact block, and it validates under jsonschema and Ajv.
- Leave `subanalysis1.output1` (§8 :856-858, `provenance.yaml:48`) unchanged. It names a declaration that the example names, not the `root` term.

This fix does not settle F033, which X34 covers. The new example fits every reading of "drives", and X34 can extend it into the three-analysis example it needs.

**Why this route.** Nothing valid changes, because no id is reserved (§4 :273) and `entry` is a plain reference. The examples go back to the declarations that §4 (:300) and `examples/iris_pipeline` already show. The flat key is the form that RFC 0004 (:137, :192) and tidy-up change 15 already set.

**Done when.** `grep -n 'root\.' docs/specification.md src/astra/schema/*.yaml` finds only prose about the root. `just test` passes: the drift check covers the regenerated schemas, and `test_specification` now validates the §7 example as a whole document.

### X26. Tests tie the spec's examples and §4 ABNF to the contract

- **Resolves:** F131, F153 · **Size:** M · **Decision:** no · **Done:** `94c7b9a`

**The problem.** The specification restates parts of the contract by hand, and nothing ties those restatements to it. `tests/test_specification.py` validates only fenced blocks with a top-level `spec_version`, so the three YAML fragments (§6 conditional elements, §6 universes, §7 undriven declaration) and the abridged `rules.json` in §10 are parsed and then dropped, whatever they contain (F131). No test reads §4's `abnf` fence, so the ABNF and `types.yaml`'s id grammar can drift apart while `just test` passes (F153). That breaks D8's reason for the split: "One statement of each fact, so the two can't drift".

**The fix.**
- `docs/specification.md`: put an HTML comment on the line before each block that isn't a whole document: `<!-- fragment: Analysis -->` before the blocks at :465, :577 and :739, and `<!-- excerpt: rules.json -->` before :1275. The comments don't show in the rendered page.
- `tests/test_specification.py`: read the fences with `markdown-it-py`, already a dev dependency, where the marker is the `html_block` token just before a fence. A yaml/json block is whole (it has `spec_version`, and no marker) or marked; any other block fails the test. Marking a block that has `spec_version` also fails.
- New test: merge each fragment over a stub, `{spec_version: "0.1.0", outputs: {stub: {type: metric, format: json}}}`. The fragment's keys replace the stub's, so its own `outputs` replace the stub output. Then validate the result against `analysis.schema.json`. The check covers the schema layer only.
- New test: the `rules.json` excerpt is a subset of `src/astra/schema/generated/rules.json`, with `$comment` ignored. Each `errors` entry must match exactly. Each rule must equal the generated rule with the same code, and the rules keep catalogue order.
- New `tests/test_grammar.py`: read §4's `abnf` fence and translate each rule to a regular expression. The translator is a small one for the subset §4 uses: concatenation, `/`, `*( )`, `[ ]`, quoted strings (case-insensitive, per RFC 5234 §2.3), `%x` ranges, `DIGIT` and rule names. It fails on anything else. Rules named after a type (`-` becomes `_`) are compared with that type's generated pattern, read at one JSON Pointer per type in `analysis.schema.json`. For `id` that pointer is `/properties/outputs/propertyNames/pattern`. Each comparison is run with `re.search` on every string of length 0–5 over `a z 0 9 _ . # ~ A - \n`, a space, `é` and `!`. The test also fails when the fence is missing, when a structured-pattern type in `types.yaml` has no rule, or when a helper rule (`lower`) isn't used by a compared rule. No new dependency.
- `CLAUDE.md` Tests paragraph and the module docstring: "every whole document among the specification's examples" becomes "every example block: whole documents validated, marked fragments validated over a stub, the `rules.json` excerpt a subset of the generated file, and §4's ABNF matching the named types' patterns".

**Why this route.** These are tests and markers only, so nothing about what is valid changes. They enforce what CLAUDE.md ("every whole document among the specification's examples, must also validate") and D8 already require.

**Done when.** `just test` fails after each drift that F131 and F153 demonstrate in a clone: the broken fragments, an edited excerpt entry, the ABNF edits, and `id: "[a-z][a-zA-Z0-9_]*"`. On the committed tree it passes. If X25, X01 or X24 lands first, its edits to the §7 example or the excerpt have to pass these tests.

### X27. Every `$` in the contract is `$(?!\n)`: partial_match on structured patterns, guard checks every `$`

- **Resolves:** F122, F145 · **Size:** S · **Decision:** no · **Done:** `2c285d9`

**The problem.** The convention says every anchored pattern ends in `$(?!\n)`, but LinkML wraps each `structured_pattern` without `partial_match` as `^(?:…)$`. The five structured patterns are already anchored, so 23 patterns in the three JSON Schemas end in a bare `$`, and the guard in `tests/test_contract.py` was loosened to accept `$(?!\n))$` (F122). The guard, like the rule, checks only how a pattern ends, so a bare `$` in an earlier branch (`^a$|^b$(?!\n)`) passes, and Python and Ajv then disagree on `"a\n"` (F145). Every committed pattern behaves soundly today. Only the rule and its check are wrong.

**The fix.**
- `src/astra/schema/types.yaml`: add `partial_match: true` to the `structured_pattern` of `id`, `address`, `reference`, `option_reference` and `condition`, with one comment saying the syntax is already anchored. Run `just gen-schema`. Only the 23 pattern strings change; `rules.json` and the context stay the same (checked in a clone).
- `tests/test_contract.py` `pattern_problems`: drop the `$(?!\n))$` ending and the ending check. Instead, fail every `$` outside a class or a lookahead that isn't immediately followed by `(?!\n)`, and fail an anchored pattern that doesn't end in `$(?!\n)`. A `$` inside a lookahead is exempt: it can only differ on a value ending in `\n`, which the final `$(?!\n)` rejects in both languages. That keeps `Input.path`'s `(?:/|$)` and `(?![^…]*/$)`.
- Add unit tests of `pattern_problems`. `^(?:a|b$(?!\n))$`, `^a$|^b$(?!\n)` and `^(?:a)$` fail. `^(?:a|b)$(?!\n)`, `Input.path`'s pattern and `[^\x00-\x20\x7f]` pass.
- Restate the convention as "every `$` outside a lookahead is written `$(?!\n)`" in three places: `CLAUDE.md` (Schema conventions), spec §4 "Patterns" (the sentence at lines 245-246) and the design's patterns paragraph (line 210). Keep each one's reason as it stands.

**Why this route.** This is implementation, test and wording work. No value changes verdict in either language, and the rule it restores is already stated in §4, CLAUDE.md and the design. It needs no RFC.

**Done when.** `just test` passes with the narrowed guard and its new unit tests. A grep of the generated schemas finds no `$` that isn't followed by `(?!\n)`, except inside a lookahead. `just check-generated` is clean after regeneration.

### X28. Design §1, §7 and §10-§13 brought in line with the tidy-up draft and Tom's decisions, with change 15's address-key exception

- **Resolves:** F083, F084 (the rest; X23 fixed design §4's `id` row) · **Size:** M · **Depends on:** the tidy-up joint pass (X31-X49, with decisions 1 and 10), X30, X34, X52, X59 · **Decision:** decided · **Done:** `7d29af5`

**The problem.** The design's traceability (§10) and its list for the tidy-up RFC (§11) were written against an older draft and number its changes that way. The draft is now *Settling the schema for v0.1*, with 29 changes. §10's tidy-up table has 11 rows under the old numbers (its "6." is now change 17, its "9." change 12, its "10." change 20). Changes 6-11, 13-16, 19 and 21-29 have no row. The intro says "100 rows", and the tables hold 105. §11's closing line maps its items to the wrong changes (F083). §11 item 12 says "anything local is a `path`", which change 25 contradicts. §11 item 6 says every map key follows the id grammar, but change 15 keys a universe's `decisions` by addresses (F084). §1 (:10, :21) still names the draft *Schema tidy-up*.

Tom's decisions since then also leave the design behind:
- Every open point of §13 is decided. Decision 1 makes agreeing spellings an error. Decision 2 settles "one result, two names". X34 settles the analysis with no decisions, and X50-X51 settle `when` on an inactive decision.
- `PIN_LIKE_COMMENT` is dropped (X49), and §7 (:438) and §10 (:589) still list it.
- Decision 6 settles part of §12's versioning item: patch releases never change validity.
- Decision 4 leaves a re-export for later, and §12 doesn't record it.
- Decision 15 puts only some fixes in the RFC. The rest are spec and schema changes with a design §11 line, which §11's intro ("They belong in the tidy-up RFC") doesn't allow for.
- Two limits that astra-tools found while planning the migration of unions-bmodes-mystra are recorded nowhere in this repository.

The implemented fixes closed part of it. X23 fixed §4's `id` row (:213). X14 removed `Analysis-159`'s second code, and its §12 clause is gone. X15 updated `tools/rules.py`'s docstring, but §12 (:812-813) still calls it stale. X08 added §11 item 18 (`modified` is not a status). Only the design is affected. The spec, the contract and the draft's change 15 and change 25 already agree.

**The fix.** All changes go in `design/2026-11-03-v0.1-schema-design.md`. Do this fix last, after the tidy-up joint pass has written its changes into the draft, so the design is numbered once. The numbers below are the current draft's. If the pass renumbers, use the new numbers, matched by title.
- §1: at :10, name the draft *Settling the schema for v0.1*. At :21-23: "… RFCs 0003-0007 and the draft *Settling the schema for v0.1*, whose changes 4 and 9 were pending at the start of this work".
- §7: drop the `PIN_LIKE_COMMENT` row (:438), with X49.
- §10, the "Tidy-up (draft) and pending items" table (:684-698): rename it "Settling the schema for v0.1 (draft of <date of the pass>)". Give it one row per change, written as the number and the draft's title (for example "9. `Analysis.version` becomes `spec_version`"). The existing rows keep their Lands-in and take their new numbers: 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, then 17 (labels), 8 (`Analysis.label`), 18 (tags), 12 (`excluded`), 20 (doi or artifact). The "Pending" row becomes change 9. The new rows land in:
  - 6 Analysis; 7 Evidence; 10 `ProvenanceRecord.spec_version`; 11 `Insight.description`;
  - 13 the maps (D2); 14 `id`, `AMBIGUOUS_ID` (D5); 15 `INVALID_ID`, the generator's `propertyNames` and its one entry form (D3); 16 the `key` slots (D4);
  - 19 §9, `rules.json` (D9, D13); 21 `INVALID_EVIDENCE_SOURCE`; 22 the closed record schema, `INVALID_RECORD`; 23 `WRONG_TYPE` (`--no-include-null`); 24 `DUPLICATE_ENTRY`;
  - 25 `INVALID_VALUE` (`url`); 26 `INVALID_COMMIT`; 27 `INVALID_PATH`; 28 the time pattern, `INVALID_VALUE`, `xsd:dateTime`; 29 `INVALID_SYNTAX`, `DUPLICATE_KEY`, the harness loader (R19).
  - The changes from 30 on take the rows their fixes write: X34, X35, X36, X39, X40, X44, X48, X49, decision 1's code and decision 10's widened `REMOTE_UNREACHABLE`. This fix checks that each has exactly one row. A fix that decision 15 keeps out of the RFC (X31-X33, X37, X38, X41-X43, X45, X47, and decision 17's worktree line) puts its row in "From `main` and from this design", not here.
- §10, other rows:
  - "From `main` and from this design": a row that a change now covers names it as well. Map keys → 15, ids one field could resolve to → 14, duplicates → 24, YAML 1.2 → 29.
  - RFC 0004, :617 "Two spellings must agree": "`CONFLICTING_SELECTIONS`; agreeing spellings are an error too (decision 1's code, its tidy-up change)".
  - RFC 0004, :626: "(`name` → optional `label`, tidy-up changes 8 and 9)".
  - RFC 0003, :589 "A stray space turns a pin into a comment": "spec §4 (advice); the rule is removed by X49's tidy-up change".
- §10 intro (:573): recount the rows of every table, check each new row, and re-date the check.
- §11: rewrite the intro (:720-721): "Changes this design makes beyond the accepted RFCs. Each names the tidy-up change it became, or says it is in the specification and schema only (Tom, 2026-11-04)." Put the change after each item, by number and title:
  - 1 → 16; 2 and 3 → 14; 4 → 6; 5 → 13; 6 → 15; 7 → 7; 8 → 11; 9 → 21; 10 → 10; 11 → 23, 22 and 24; 12 → 25; 13 → 4 and 9; 14 → 26; 15 → 27; 16 → 28; 17 → 19.
  - Item 6 reads: "Map keys follow the id grammar, except those of `Universe.decisions`, which are decision addresses (RFC 0004's flat keys); map entries take one form …".
  - Item 12 reads: "… an analysis in the root's repository is a `path`, another repository on this machine a `file://` URL".
  - Item 13 reads: "The FragmentSelector swap and `Analysis.version` → `spec_version`, pending in the draft at the start of this work".
  - Item 18 (X08's `modified`) names its change if the RFC pass adds one. Decision 15's list leaves it out, so otherwise it is marked as specification only.
  - §11 also holds each item a later fix adds, in the order the fixes land (X44's item takes the number after 18). Decision 15 sorts them. Those with a change: X34, X35, X36, X39, X40, X44, X48, X49's removal, decision 1 and decision 10. Those in the spec and schema only: X31, X32, X33, X37, X38, X41, X42, X43, X45, X47 and decision 17.
  - The closing line (:757-758) names every change the items became: today "changes 4, 6, 7, 9-11, 13-16, 19 and 21-28; change 29 records R19", plus the new ones. It names the items that are specification only by number. The `rules.json` sentence stays.
- §12, items the decided fixes settle:
  - Drop the stale-docstring clause at :812-813: X15 has updated the docstring.
  - Drop the *Versioning* item on tagless clones (:792-793) when X30 lands: X30 stops stamping the latest tag between releases. The redirect item (:790-791, in the release blockers) serves X30's `$id`s.
  - The `""` item (:800-802) drops `FragmentSelector.value` (X39), and its `excluded` clause becomes X41's `iri` edge.
  - The **Versioning policy** bullet (:778-779) records decision 6. "Patch releases never change validity. There is one analysis schema and one record schema per minor version, and a 0.1 validator accepts every 0.1.x file. A checker keeps the record schema of every minor from 0.1 on (X59), and a record whose `spec_version` has no record schema fails `INVALID_RECORD`. Moving 0.0.x files to 0.1 is migration." What a minor or a major release may change stays for the policy.
  - The **Conformance suite** bullet (:770-775) names `conformance/` in this repository, outside the wheel (D7). It says runner rules are tested through a runner harness, future work in astra-tools. That item stays.
- §12, a new bullet, **What v0.1 can't write**, for a later RFC. Its first two items come from astra-tools' design §15-§16 (unions-bmodes-mystra):
  - *Parametric outputs.* The spec has no way to write one output per parameter value, so a project generates its outputs and repeats them. unions-bmodes-mystra has 351 in one generated file.
  - *Inputs of several files.* An input names one file. An input that stands for many files becomes one input per file, or an output whose recipe reads them all. This sits beside the **Folder formats** item (:780-782).
  - *Re-exports* (decision 4). Building a declared analysis's result on request (`materialise u1 sub.out`) is tooling. A later RFC may let an analysis name what it exports. The preferred form is an optional `Analysis.targets` list. The other is an output with no recipe and a single input, read as a re-export. v0.1 gives an output with no recipe no meaning, and the specification stays silent on it.
- §13, every point is settled. Mark each **Settled**, as the driven-analysis point (:832) is:
  - "Agreeing spellings" (:820-821), by decision 1. "Two spellings of one decision in one universe, and two addresses of one element in one list (`Output.inputs`, `Output.decisions`, `when`, `requires`, `incompatible_with`), are errors (decision 1's code, its tidy-up change)." This covers X04's aliased entries, so X04 adds no open point.
  - "`when` on an inactive decision" (:822-824), by the when RFC draft (X50, X51). A condition naming a switched-off decision is an error unless it is guarded. The specification takes it when that RFC lands.
  - "An analysis with no decisions, reading a per-universe output" (:825-831), by X34's change 30. "An analysis with no decisions and no universes of its own counts as driven by every universe of its driver."
  - "One result, two names" (:839-848), by decision 2, in spec §8 now with X52, not at the check phase. "A result's name is its output's id after the driven declarations from the analysis it belongs to. Reached through two such chains, it is built once and placed and recorded under each. Undriven declarations, back-edges included, never add a name." A warning about two spellings is tooling's. Drop the "probably: the fewest declarations" guess.
- §12 and §13, a last pass: a question Tom has decided is not an open point. The check and runner points that X52 leaves to the check phase, and that X53-X57 and X59 record, go in §12 as decided work for that phase, with their answers. None goes in §13. Nothing is recorded on git-annex adjusted branches (X55) or on a list of stand-ins (X46).
- The one-letter-host sentence (:256) is left to X17. The narrowings F022 records are left to X42, which adds its own rows and items.

**Decided.** Tom accepted every recommendation this fix rests on. He dropped `PIN_LIKE_COMMENT` (X49). He settled the no-decisions point in X34, agreeing spellings in decision 1, result names in decision 2, and patch releases in decision 6. He put re-exports in §12 for later (decision 4), sorted the fixes by RFC scope (decision 15), and asked that astra-tools' two limits be recorded in design §12. The fix needed no decision of its own.

**Why this route.** These are design-only corrections. They bring the design in line with the draft and with Tom's decisions. Change 15 already says "its keys are decisions' addresses rather than ids", and change 25 says "another repository on the same machine by a `file://` URL". The settled points are settled by fixes that carry their own spec and RFC edits. This fix only stops the design from listing them as open. The astra-tools notes and the re-export item record limits of v0.1 and add no rule. Nothing that is valid changes, and nothing tools must do changes. The draft needs no edit.

**Done when.**
- Every change of the draft has exactly one §10 row, with the draft's number and title. The stated row count matches a count of the table rows.
- Every §11 item names its change or says it is specification only, and the closing line lists them all.
- A search for "every map key" in the design finds the `Universe.decisions` exception.
- Searches for "anything local", "Schema tidy-up", "clone without tags" and "older set of extraction errors" find nothing. `PIN_LIKE_COMMENT` appears only where its removal is recorded (§10's RFC 0003 row, the tidy-up table, §11).
- §12 carries the parametric-outputs, multi-file-inputs and re-export items, and decision 6 in the versioning bullet.
- §13 has no open point: every item is marked **Settled**.

### X29. Warn that `excluded: no` or `off` is a reason under YAML 1.2

- **Resolves:** F157 · **Size:** S · **Decision:** no · **Done:** `a68ad06`

**The problem.** Tidy-up change 12 turns `excluded` from a boolean into a reason, and change 29 reads files under YAML 1.2's core schema, where `no`, `off`, `n` and their capitalised forms are strings. So `excluded: no` passes `BLANK_EXCLUDED`'s pattern and excludes the option, with the reason "no". In v0.0.x the same line meant "not excluded". Nothing at `excluded` warns of this: §5 names only `excluded: true` as not a reason, the field description says nothing, and change 12's migration step names only `excluded: false` (F157). The contract is right and both validators agree. Only the wording is missing.

**The fix.**
- `docs/specification.md` §5, the `excluded` paragraph: after "`excluded: true` is not a reason ([`WRONG_TYPE`])", add "while `excluded: no` or `off` is the reason `no` or `off` (§3), so the option is excluded".
- `src/astra/schema/analysis.yaml`, `Option.excluded` description: "Why the option is ruled out, as a reason that is not blank; any string is a reason, `no` and `off` included. An option with a reason is excluded: no universe selects it." Then run `just gen-schema` and commit the regenerated `generated/`.
- `docs/llms.txt` (lines 12 and 71) still tells agents to write `excluded: true` with `excluded_reason`, which fails as `WRONG_TYPE`. Rewrite both places as `excluded: <reason>`. No finding names this, but CLAUDE.md requires llms.txt to match the specification.
- Tidy-up draft, change 12's migration step (line 262): "An option with `excluded: false`, or a YAML 1.1 word for false such as `no` or `off`, drops it". This edits an existing step and adds no numbered change.
- New valid fixture `tests/data/valid/Analysis-nnn-excluded-no.yaml`: an option with `excluded: no` that no universe selects. It records that the word is a reason.

**Why this route.** Change 29 already makes `no` and `off` strings ("`yes`, `no`, `on` and `off` are strings"), and change 12 already makes any non-blank string a reason. The fix only states what follows from the two. It changes nothing about what is valid, so it is just do. Change 12's other wording problem ("can't be empty" against "not blank", F081) is a separate fix, X48. Both edit the same §5 sentence and description, so do them in one pass if both land.

**Done when.** §5, the description and llms.txt all carry the warning, and the migration step names `no` and `off`. The new valid fixture passes. `just test` is green, the drift check included.

### X30. Each JSON Schema's `$id` names its minor version

- **Resolves:** F151 · **Size:** M · **Depends on:** none · **Decision:** decided · **Done:** `4b5f8e6`

**The problem.** Each JSON Schema's `$id` is `https://w3id.org/astra/` and its file name (`justfile`, `schema_namespace`). A release changes only `$comment`, so every version of a schema has the same `$id`, and so do all 265 `errors` keys of `rules.json` (F151). A checker still holds several versions at once: `INVALID_RECORD` uses "the record schema of its `spec_version`", and X59 has a checker keep the record schema of every minor version from 0.1 on. JSON Schema 2019-09 §8.2.4.5 allows one schema per URI. Ajv throws on the second version, and Python's `referencing` silently keeps one. §10 doesn't say which version a URI names. Between releases, `_stage-versioned` stamps the latest tag, so main declares a version for content that isn't that version, and a clone without tags stamps `0.0.0` and fails `just test` (design §12, "Versioning"). And astra-tools needs a 0.1.0 pre-release to pin against, which `just release` can't make: it accepts only `X.Y.Z`.

Tom's item 6 (2026-11-04) settles what a version names. A patch release never changes what is valid. So there is one analysis schema and one record schema per minor version, and a 0.1 validator accepts every 0.1.x. One URI per minor version is then one schema, which is what JSON Schema asks.

**The fix.**
- **The promise, in spec §1 Versions.** After "such as `0.1.0` ([`INVALID_VALUE`])", add: "A patch release never changes what is valid: a document or a record is valid under `X.Y.Z` exactly when it is valid under every other `X.Y` version. So one schema of `astra.yaml` and one of a record serve each minor version, and a validator that reads one version of it reads them all ([`UNSUPPORTED_SPEC_VERSION`](#validator))." The last sentence becomes "What a minor or major version promises about another is left to ASTRA's versioning policy." The `spec_version` pattern and the `UNSUPPORTED_SPEC_VERSION` row stay as they are. X30 writes this sentence because it lands first, and X59 builds on it.
- **The id's version.** `tools/jsonschema_generator.py`: `--id` takes a `{series}` placeholder, filled from the staged schema's version. It is `X.Y` for a release `X.Y.Z`. It is the whole version for a pre-release (`0.1.0rc1`). It is `dev` for `dev`. Any other version fails when `{series}` is present. Update `--id`'s help and the module docstring. `$comment` keeps the whole version (`analysis schema version 0.1.2`), so a tool can tell which patch it holds.
- **`justfile`, `gen-schema`.** The three `--id` arguments become `{{schema_namespace}}{series}/<file>`. Update the comment on `schema_namespace` and the one above `gen-schema`. `tools/rules.py` builds its keys from `$id`, so every key carries the series and the script needs no change.
- **`justfile`, `_stage-versioned`.** The version is `$RELEASE_VERSION` when set. Otherwise it is the tag at HEAD without its `v` (`git describe --tags --exact-match --match 'v[0-9]*'`). Otherwise it is `dev`. The recipe fails on a value that isn't `X.Y.Z`, `X.Y.Z` followed by `aN`, `bN` or `rcN`, or `dev`. Update its comment. A clone without tags then stamps `dev`, which is what main holds.
- **`justfile`, `release`.**
  - The version may be a pre-release: `^[0-9]+\.[0-9]+\.[0-9]+((a|b|rc)[0-9]+)?$`. This is PEP 440's normal form. uv-dynamic-versioning (`style = "pep440"`) makes the same string of the tag `v0.1.0rc1`, so the tag, the wheel and the `$id` agree. `0.1.0-rc.1` is refused: PEP 440 would make the wheel `0.1.0rc1`.
  - The patch guard. When the version's minor already has a release (a tag `vX.Y.N`, from 0.1 on), the recipe compares the regenerated files with that minor's greatest release, through `git show`, before it commits. The three JSON Schemas must be equal once every string-valued `description`, `title` and `$comment` is dropped. `rules.json`'s `errors` must be equal, keys and codes. Any other difference fails the release and prints it. A new script, `tools/check_patch.py <tag>`, does the comparison. The guard covers the schema layer; the validator and check layers rest on review.
  - After the tag, the recipe runs `RELEASE_VERSION=dev just gen-schema` and commits "Back to dev after vX.Y.Z", so only the tagged commit holds the version. HEAD is that commit, so the "next steps" push (`git push --atomic origin HEAD vX.Y.Z`) stays as it is.
- **What a pre-release carries.** Its files carry its whole version, in `$id` (`https://w3id.org/astra/0.1.0rc1/analysis.schema.json`), in `rules.json`'s keys and in each `$comment`. A pre-release is a candidate for `X.Y.Z` and may still differ from it, so it doesn't share the minor's ids. The documents it checks still state `spec_version: "0.1.0"`: the `spec_version` pattern has no pre-release form, and none is added. A pre-release isn't a release, so X59's duty doesn't cover it. A record written under 0.1.0rc1 states 0.1.0 and is read by 0.1's schema.
- **`.github/workflows/deploy-docs-on-tag.yaml` and `docs-deploy`.** `docs-deploy` takes zero or more aliases. A release `X.Y.Z` deploys at `/X.Y.Z/` and moves the aliases `X.Y` and `latest`. A pre-release deploys at its own version (`/0.1.0rc1/`) and moves none. The `X.Y` alias serves the latest patch's `schema/` files, which is where `w3id.org/astra/X.Y/` redirects. A GitHub release marked as a pre-release still publishes to PyPI, where pip skips `0.1.0rc1` unless asked. astra-tools pins `astra-spec == 0.1.0rc1` until 0.1.0, then `astra-spec ~= 0.1.0`, which takes every patch of 0.1.
- **Spec §10.**
  - The lead-in ("each is published at `https://w3id.org/astra/` followed by its file name") becomes "at `https://w3id.org/astra/`, its minor version (`0.1` for every 0.1.x) and `/`, followed by its file name". The table's five URIs gain `0.1/`.
  - The `$id` paragraph says, in gist: "Each JSON Schema's `$id` is its URI. So the closed and open record schemas are distinct resources, and so are the schemas of two minor versions, which a checker can hold side by side. The patches of one minor version share each URI: a patch never changes what is valid (§1), so the URI names one schema whichever patch's file a tool holds, and a tool keeps the latest. A pre-release is `X.Y.Z` followed by `aN`, `bN` or `rcN`. It is a candidate for `X.Y.Z`, its URIs carry its whole version, and the documents it checks state `X.Y.Z`. `https://w3id.org/astra/` followed by a file name alone redirects to the latest release's file and is no schema's `$id`. The version of the release a file was generated from is in its `$comment`."
  - The `errors` bullet adds that each key names its minor version, through `$id`. The three keys of the `rules.json` excerpt gain `0.1/`; its `$comment` stays `0.1.0`.
  - The RDF namespace of the record's terms (`https://w3id.org/astra/`, end of §10) is not a schema URI and stays.
- **Tests.**
  - `test_contract.py`: `test_each_schema_is_identified_by_its_own_file_uri` checks that `$id` is the namespace, the series of the version in `$comment`, `/`, then the file name. `test_top_level_holds_only_standard_keywords` accepts a `$comment` ending in `X.Y.Z`, a pre-release or `dev`.
  - `test_rules.py`: `ANALYSIS`, `RECORD` and `OPEN` are built from each committed schema's `$id`, not from `NAMESPACE`. The `$comment` check in `test_rules_json_has_its_three_parts` takes the same three forms.
  - `test_specification.py`, `test_the_rules_excerpt_is_a_subset_of_the_generated_file`: an excerpt key's series is replaced by the committed file's before the lookup, so the spec writes `0.1/` while main holds `dev/`.
  - `test_generator.py`: `{series}` gives `0.1` for `0.1.3`, `0.1.0rc1` for `0.1.0rc1` and `dev` for `dev`, and fails for `0.1` and `0.0.0-x`.
  - A test puts stamped copies at `0.1/` and `0.2/` into one `referencing` Registry and checks that each resolves to itself.
- **Design and CLAUDE.md.**
  - Design §3: the table's `$id` column, the "Versioned at release" bullet (`dev` between releases, the series in `$id`), the `$id` bullet, and the example key.
  - Design §12: the redirect item serves `X.Y/<file>` and `<pre-release>/<file>`, and `dev` is served nowhere. The "Versioning" tagless-clone item goes. The "Versioning policy" item records item 6: one schema per minor version, a patch never changes what is valid, and the release guard checks the schema layer.
  - CLAUDE.md: the stamping sentence ("the tag at HEAD, or `dev`"; the tags are needed only at a tagged commit). The release flow: pre-release versions, the patch guard, the `dev` commit, and the `X.Y` and `latest` aliases moving only on a release. The "pin to" sentence: `astra-spec.org/X.Y/schema/...` for a minor version, `X.Y.Z` for one release.

**Decided.** Option (A), as Tom agreed, reshaped by item 6 (Tom, 2026-11-04). The `$id` carries the minor version (`0.1`) for every release of it, the whole version for a pre-release, and `dev` unless HEAD is at a tag; a `dev` regeneration commit follows each release's tag; and the patch promise goes in §1. Not taken: an `$id` per release (`0.1.2`), which would make each patch a second schema for the same documents; a pre-release at its minor's ids, which would put two schemas at one URI if the candidate changes; unversioned ids that name the latest (B); and stamping the latest tag between releases.

One choice is left to Tom: whether the patch guard holds `rules.json`'s codes fixed as well as its keys. Recommended: yes, as written above. A tool then reports the same code for the same error across a minor version, and a wrong code is fixed in the next minor.

*Confirmed (Tom, 2026-11-05): the patch guard keeps `rules.json`'s error codes fixed within a minor version, as well as its keys. `tools/check_patch.py` does: it compares `errors`, keys and codes (`error_map`), and `tests/test_check_patch.py`'s `test_a_changed_error_map_fails` fails a changed code.*

**Why this route.** No accepted RFC or tidy-up change names a schema URI, and design §11 keeps the generated JSON Schemas out of the RFC: they are "a published artefact of the implementation". Pre-releases, the release recipe and the guard are tooling. The patch promise settles part of what §1 already leaves to "ASTRA's versioning policy", which no RFC writes, so it goes in §1 and design §12. Tidy-up change 22's "later version" is X59's. Nothing changes what is valid now: the `spec_version` pattern is untouched.

**Done when.**
- `just check-generated` passes at a commit with no tag, with every file stamped `dev` and every id under `dev/`.
- The contract test ties `$id` to `$comment`. The registry test passes with two minor versions side by side. The excerpt test passes with the spec's `0.1/` keys.
- In a scratch clone:
  - `just release 0.1.0rc1` makes a tagged commit whose ids carry `0.1.0rc1`, then a `dev` commit. `uv build` at the tag gives `astra_spec-0.1.0rc1`.
  - `just release 0.1.0` gives ids under `0.1/`.
  - After a change to a description only, `just release 0.1.1` passes, with ids still under `0.1/`.
  - After a change to a pattern, `just release 0.1.2` fails before it commits.
  - `just check-generated` passes at every commit.
- Ajv is a one-off manual check: the `0.1/` and `0.2/` copies compile in one instance.

### X60. §5 Recipes: what a placeholder gives, verbatim with the author quoting, and no meaning for an output without a recipe

- **Resolves:** none (from astra-tools' design D15) · **Size:** S · **Depends on:** none (lands in one edit with X02 if both are open) · **Decision:** decided · **Done:** `20df68d`

**The problem.** §5 says what each placeholder "becomes" but not how it goes into the command. A path may hold a space, and `INVALID_PATH` also lets it hold `"`, `'`, `$` and other characters the shell treats specially. So a reader can't tell whether a runner shell-quotes what it substitutes, or whether an author must. Two runners that answer differently break each other's recipes: `"{inputs.data}"` comes out doubly quoted under a quoting runner, and `{inputs.data}` splits on a space under a verbatim one. §5 also doesn't say what path a placeholder gives: relative to what, or absolute. A recipe runs in its analysis's directory, but `{output}` of a driven analysis points into its driver's `results/` (§8). The findings report lists both under "Not verified": "Also whether input paths with spaces are shell-quoted when substituted. Recipes are out of scope; settled by a runner reference implementation or a statement in the spec." Last, `Output.recipe` is optional in the schema, and nothing says what an output without one means. astra-tools wants to build a declared analysis's result on request (`materialise u1 sub.out`), and a recipe-less output with one input reads like a re-export of it. Nothing in v0.1 should commit to that reading yet.

**The fix.**
- `docs/specification.md` §5 Recipes, a new paragraph straight after the placeholder table, before "In `{inputs.<entry>}` and `{decisions.<entry>}`":

  > A placeholder gives a path the recipe can open from the directory it runs in; whether it is relative or absolute is the runner's. A runner substitutes each placeholder verbatim, with no shell quoting, so quoting is the author's job: a path may hold a space or a character the shell treats specially, and a command that may meet one quotes the placeholder, as in `--data '{inputs.data}'`. Single quotes keep every character a path may hold but `'`, and double quotes every one but `$`, the backtick, `"` and `\`.

  *Updated 2026-11-05: Tom changed the example from double quotes to single quotes, since a path may hold `$`, the backtick and `"`, and the last sentence says what each kind of quoting keeps (`6d954e0`); after review the backslash joined the double-quote list, since a runner's absolute prefix or chosen path may hold one.*

  "A placeholder gives a path" covers `{inputs.<entry>}`, `{inputs}` and `{output}`; the table already says `{decisions.<entry>}` gives an option's id.
- `docs/specification.md` §5 Outputs, after the list of the output's fields (after "`recipe` says how the output is made (below)."), one sentence: "An output may leave out `recipe`; this version gives such an output no meaning."

  *Updated 2026-11-05: Tom decided that every output has a recipe with a command, so this sentence is withdrawn. `Output.recipe` and `Recipe.command` are required (`MISSING_FIELD`), since the planned results of an output with no recipe, or with a recipe that has no command, are results that nothing builds, which a checker reports missing for ever; design §11 item 39 records the change, and §12 now says v0.1 requires a recipe, so a later version can make it optional again without invalidating any file.*
- Design §12 'For later phases', one line: "**Building a declared analysis's result on request** (`materialise u1 sub.out`) is the tooling's in v0.1. A later version may write it in the file: an optional `Analysis.targets` list of results to build (preferred), or a recipe-less output with one input as a re-export. v0.1 gives a recipe-less output no meaning, so either stays open."
- Nothing else changes. No rule, code or level is added, so §9 and `rules.json` are untouched. The `Recipe.command` and `Output.recipe` descriptions, the schema and the examples stay as they are: design §1 leaves recipes untouched beyond the placeholders' forms, every example output has a recipe, and the examples' paths hold no space. X02 rewrites the last sentence of the same §5 paragraph ("A recipe reads files and results only through its output's inputs", `UNDECLARED_READ`), so land the two in one edit if both are open.
