**Decided.** Verbatim substitution with the author quoting, as astra-tools' design D15 proposes (Tom, with the runner approach, 2026-11-04). The path's form is the tooling's: the spec says only that the recipe can open it from where it runs (Tom, 2026-11-04, item 3). This closes the half the earlier entry left for design §12, so no §12 line on it is needed. Building a declared analysis's result on request is the tooling's, and v0.1 gives a recipe-less output no meaning; `Analysis.targets` (preferred) or a recipe-less re-export goes to design §12 for later (Tom, 2026-11-04, item 4).

**Why this route.** The sentences state what runners already do, so they change nothing for any document or tool. §5's table says a placeholder "becomes" the path, which is a verbatim reading, and no RFC (0003, 0004, 0006, 0007) mentions quoting or the path's form. astra-tools' `render_command` substitutes verbatim, with paths relative to the recipe's directory or absolute on request (its D15), and unions-bmodes-mystra's recipes already quote their own placeholders (`"{inputs.glass_mock_catalogs}"`; astra-tools design §16), so a quoting runner would break them. Either path form works for a recipe that opens what it is given, so fixing one would bind runners for no gain to authors. `{inputs}` joins its paths with spaces, as the table already says, so quoting can't keep them apart; a command whose input paths may hold a space names them one by one with `{inputs.<entry>}`. No rule is needed for that. Leaving a recipe-less output without meaning keeps both later forms open: a v0.1 file with one doesn't acquire a reading a later version contradicts. A tool may still do something with one, as astra-tools plans (no command to run); nothing in v0.1 relies on it. Recipes are otherwise out of scope (design §1), so the fix stops here.

**Done when.** §5 Recipes carries the paragraph after the placeholder table, §5 Outputs carries the sentence on an output without a `recipe`, and design §12 carries the line on building on request. `just test` is green, with `rules.json` and the generated contract unchanged. astra-tools' design §14 row "Quoting, and the form of a placeholder's path" already reads "settled", and needs no change.

### X52. Planned results, a result's names, and unpinned evidence's status

- **Resolves:** F007, F039, F055 · **Size:** M · **Depends on:** X07 · **Decision:** decided · **Route:** just do (changed from checker and runner phase, Tom 2026-11-04) · **Done:** `4495c81`

**The problem.** Every check rule works on the root's planned results. §8 Results still defines them in one sentence: "The results an analysis's outputs and universes call for are its **planned** results." It doesn't say whether a driven analysis's unread outputs are planned, or whether the results of consumed analyses count (F039). X23 added that an inactive output plans nothing, and X03 that a checker reads records only through planned results, but neither says what is planned. X06 named a result through driven declarations only and made the root never driven. That closes F055's `iris_pipeline` case, whose back-edges are undriven. But an analysis can still be reached through two chains of driven declarations, or through a cycle of them (a key `a.b.a.x`), and §8 gives each result one name and one place (F055). An unpinned citation of a per-universe output is valid (`UNPINNED_EVIDENCE` is a SHOULD), but it names no single result, so `STALE_EVIDENCE` gives it no status (F007). That part still holds: §8 Statuses and §9 say nothing about it.

**The fix.** In `docs/specification.md`:
- §1 Terms, after `result`: "| planned result | a result a check of the root covers: every result of the root's active outputs, every result those read, transitively, and every result the root's evidence cites | §8 |".
- §8 Results, in place of the planned-results sentence (it keeps X23's sentence):
  "The root's **planned** results are:
  - in each of the root's universes, the result of every output of the root active there, and the result of each output of the root built once;
  - every result a planned result reads through its output's `inputs`, through any declaration, pinned or not;
  - every result the root's evidence cites (§5).

  An output plans no result in a universe where it is inactive (§5). An output of a declared analysis that no planned result reads and no evidence of the root cites isn't planned, and building the root doesn't build it. Building it on request is the tooling's choice, and a checker gives its record no status (below)."
- §8 Results, the ownership and name paragraph, in place of X06's "name … pushed through …" text: "A result belongs to the analysis whose universe it was built in. So what a driven analysis builds in its driver's universes belongs to the driver. The result of an output built once belongs to the analysis whose output it is, and a result read through a pin in a declared analysis's own universe belongs to that analysis: a consumed analysis's results stay in its own directory. A result's **name** is its output's id after the ids of the driven declarations from the analysis it belongs to (`subanalysis1.output1`). An undriven declaration, a back-edge such as `iris: {path: ..}` included, never adds a name, since a pin through it names that analysis's own universe (§7). So an output of the analysis a result belongs to has its id as its name, and so does an output built once, which is built in no universe. Reached through two such chains (`inner` and `stage.inner`, when keys go through both), the result is built once and placed and recorded under each name. Its **address** is a name followed, for a result built in a universe, by `#` and the universe's id (`subanalysis1.output1#u1`). Each address is the `address` of the record at its place."
- §7 The analysis stack, X06's paragraph: "That analysis is then its driver" becomes "That analysis is then its driver, and, like the root, is never driven in that universe." Without it, a driver's own outputs could take a second name.
- §8 Where records and bytes go: "Both are worked out from the result's address" becomes "Both are worked out from an address of the result, so a result with two names has two of each." "A record is the record only of the result its `address` names" gains ", under that name".
- §8 What a runner does, and §9 `UNRECORDED_RESULT`: "A runner writes a record for every result it makes, at the place of each of its names in the analysis the result belongs to, and writes the bytes to their default places." X02 keeps this row's wording otherwise.
- §8 Statuses: after the first paragraph, "A result with two names gets its statuses at each place." After X07's evidence sentences (written here too if X07 hasn't landed): "An `artifact` that names an output built per universe without a pin cites that output's results in each of the root's universes where it is active, and its analysis is built. The evidence gets the worst of their record statuses: `missing`, then `stale`. If there are none, it is `missing`." Keep the sentence on an analysis from a commit as X07 words it: its results that are read or cited are planned like any other, and their records are read at the declared commit.
- §5 Insights and evidence, the last sentence: "Evidence citing a result is checked through that result's record, and unpinned evidence through the records of the output's results (§8)."
- §9 check, the Statuses paragraph: add "A result with two names has its statuses at each place. Evidence citing an output built per universe without a pin gets the worst first status of that output's results (`missing`, then `stale`)."
- §9 rows:
  - `MISSING_RECORD`: "Every planned result has a record at `.astra/records/{universe}/{name}.json` (or `{name}.json` for an output built once), for each of its names, in the analysis it belongs to".
  - `MISPLACED_RECORD`: "The record at each place of a planned result has, as its `address`, the address that place is worked out from".
  - `STALE_EVIDENCE`, Status when broken: "the cited result's status; without a pin on an output built per universe, the worst of its results' (`missing`, then `stale`)".
- Run `just gen-schema` and commit `generated/rules.json`.

The names stay finite. Under §7 today a declaration is driven only through a key, and keys are finite. A note for X34: its clause for an analysis with no decisions and no universes drives no declaration that leads back to an analysis already on the chain, or a self-declaration of such an analysis gives endless names.

In `design/2026-11-03-v0.1-schema-design.md`:
- §7: the Statuses paragraph and the `MISSING_RECORD`, `MISPLACED_RECORD`, `STALE_EVIDENCE` and `UNRECORDED_RESULT` rows match §9.
- §10 RFC 0005: a row "Planned results (0005:199 uses the term undefined) | spec 8; §11 item 19", and the "Evidence is checked through its record" row adds "unpinned: the worst of the output's results".
- §11, item 19: "The planned results are the root's active outputs, what they read, transitively, and what its evidence cites. An output nothing reaches isn't built: RFC 0005:215's `iris_pipeline` has 12 results, not 14 (nothing reads `feature_plot`), and RFC 0004 §5's and RFC 0005 §1's layouts lose `subanalysis3.output3`, `subanalysis3.output3_per_universe` and `subsubanalysis1.output1#u2`. A result is named through driven declarations only, and one reached through two chains of them is placed and recorded under each. Unpinned evidence on a per-universe output gets the worst status of its results." The closing line becomes "items 18 and 19 are not yet". No RFC text: item 15 doesn't list X52.
- §12, Tooling: "Building a result on request (`materialise u1 sub.out`) is the tooling's. A later RFC may let an analysis name what to build: an optional `Analysis.targets` list (preferred), or a recipe-less output with one input as a re-export. v0.1 gives a recipe-less output no meaning."
- §13 "One result, two names": mark it **Settled** with the rule above (spec §8). This replaces X06's note and X28's "fewest, then byte order" bullet, which then has nothing left to do for this point.

F039's narrower clause, the status of a result that reads a `missing` result, is X53's (F040), not this fix's.

**Decided** (Tom, 2026-11-04; items 2 and 4 of the later decisions replace the plan's points 1 and 3).
1. Planned set. In each universe, the root's active outputs (all of them), every result those read through `Output.inputs`, transitively, across declarations, and every result its evidence cites. An output of a declared analysis that nothing reaches isn't built. This replaces "every active output of the root and of each analysis it drives": a driven analysis's unread outputs (`feature_plot`) are no longer planned.
2. Consumed analyses. Their results are planned when reached, transitively, and they stay in the consumed analysis's own directory (RFC 0005 §1's `subanalysis1/subsubanalysis1/.astra/records/v1/output1.json`).
3. Names, in astra-tools' wording, which Tom agreed: "A result's name is its output's id after the driven declarations from the analysis it belongs to. Reached through two such chains, it is built once and placed and recorded under each." Undriven declarations, back-edges included, never add a name. No tie-break by fewest declarations or byte order. Each place's record has that place's address. Warning about two spellings is the tooling's.
4. Unpinned evidence. The worst status of the output's results (recommended: it never hides a stale or missing result). Making the pin a MUST would contradict RFC 0003 §3 and RFC 0004:414.
5. Building a declared analysis's result on request is tooling. Design §12 records `Analysis.targets` (preferred) or a recipe-less re-export for later; v0.1 leaves a recipe-less output undefined.

**Why this route.** astra-tools' v0.1 design asks for these rulings now (its §14), and the conformance check cases rest on them. The fix changes no schema: only spec prose, three §9 statements and one status cell, regenerated into `rules.json`. The planned set departs from RFC 0005's example count and from RFC 0004/0005's layouts, so design §11 records it. It needs no RFC text, since item 15 lists X52 among neither the RFC fixes nor the others.

**Done when.**
- The spec has the `planned result` Terms row and §8's text. A grep for "pushed through" in §8 Results, for "fewest" and for "byte order" in the spec and the design finds nothing.
- `just gen-schema` changes only the statements of `MISSING_RECORD`, `MISPLACED_RECORD` and `UNRECORDED_RESULT` and the status of `STALE_EVIDENCE` in `rules.json`. `just test` passes.
- By hand from §8, `examples/iris_pipeline` has 12 planned results with one name each: `iris_data`, `comparison`, and in each of `baseline` and `mlp_svm`, `split`, `summary`, `classification.accuracy`, `classification.predictions` and `feature_extraction.features`. `feature_extraction.feature_plot` is planned nowhere. `classification.feature_extraction.features` and `feature_extraction.iris.split` are not names.
- A stack whose universes set `inner.d1` and `stage.inner.d2` gives `inner.out#u1` two record places, `.astra/records/u1/inner.out.json` and `.astra/records/u1/stage.inner.out.json`.
- F007's minimal analysis: its evidence is `stale` when `accuracy#u2` is stale and `accuracy#u1` unchanged, and `missing` when either has no record.
- Design §7, §10, §11 (item 19), §12 and §13 carry the items above.

## Tidy-up RFC

Changes that go beyond the accepted RFCs, settle what they leave open, or record a narrowing nothing records yet: each becomes a numbered change in the tidy-up RFC.

### X31. Values with no JSON form: a key that isn't a string is `INVALID_ID`, and a non-finite number, an overflowing one or a lone surrogate fails at parse

- **Resolves:** F001, F121, F163, and F140's `.inf`/`.nan` half · **Size:** M · **Depends on:** X38 · **Decision:** decided · **Route:** spec only, no RFC text, a design §11 line (if Tom confirms the RFC rule in "For the tidy-up RFC") · **Done:** `a74f45c`

**The problem.** Change 29 says a file "means the same as the JSON it converts to, which is what the schema checks". Some YAML values have no JSON form, and nothing says what they become. §3's types bullet (X19) says only "An integer or a float is a JSON number, unless JSON has no number for it".
- Keys (F001). X01 gave `propertyNames/type` the key's code, so the harness reports `1:` or `true:` under `outputs` as `INVALID_ID` at `/outputs` (Analysis-187). The `yaml` package's `toJS()` turns `true` into the valid id `"true"`, so Ajv accepts the file. With `"true"` and `true` in one map, `toJS()` keeps one entry and reports nothing. `1:` beside `1.0:` raises `ConstructorError` in the harness (`INVALID_SYNTAX`, X10's limit), and `yaml` reports `DUPLICATE_KEY`, though they are distinct YAML nodes. A non-string key among an entry's fields is `UNKNOWN_FIELD`. `? [a, b]` raises in the harness.
- Numbers (F121). The harness builds `.inf`, `-.inf` and `.nan`, and `test_loader.py` asserts it. Python's `jsonschema` and Ajv then give them different codes. `1e400` and a 400-digit integer get opposite verdicts in astra.yaml and in records.
- Strings (F163). `"\uD800"` loads in both stacks, and a path holding it names different files in Python and Node. `"😀"` is U+1F600 in `yaml` and JSON, but two lone surrogates in the harness (checked against both).

**The fix.**
- **Spec §3, the `astra.yaml` bullets.**
  - The types bullet: "An integer or a float is a JSON number, and a field's type is checked as JSON Schema checks it, so `2.0` is an integer. `.inf`, `-.inf` and `.nan`, and a number beyond a double's range, which rounds to infinity (`1e400`, an integer of 400 digits), have no JSON form and make the file invalid ([`INVALID_SYNTAX`](#parse-and-schema))."
  - A new bullet on keys: "every key is a string. A key the core schema reads as a boolean, a number or a null (`true:`, `1:`, `~:`), or a key that is a list or a map, has no JSON form ([`INVALID_ID`](#parse-and-schema)). It is reported at its map, naming the key as written, and its entry is left out of the JSON form the schema checks (§9, What is checked). A key that fails at parse, such as `.nan`, is `INVALID_SYNTAX`, as any value is."
  - A new bullet on strings: "a `\u` or `\U` escape of a surrogate (U+D800–U+DFFF) is read only as half of a pair, a high surrogate's escape followed at once by a low one's, which together give one character, as in JSON: `"😀"` is U+1F600. Any other surrogate escape makes the file invalid ([`INVALID_SYNTAX`](#parse-and-schema))."
  - The commit-id sentence X19 wrote: "…and one of digits and a single `e` a float, or invalid at parse when its exponent passes a double's range (`1234e` then 35 digits)". The quoting advice after it stands. Today such an id is `WRONG_TYPE`.
- **Spec §3, "Every document"** gains: "Its JSON form is what the schema checks: each string is a sequence of Unicode scalar values, and each number is finite and within a double's range ([`INVALID_SYNTAX`](#parse-and-schema))." X37's Records paragraph says how a record's parser checks it.
- **§9 rows.**
  - `INVALID_ID`: "Every map key is a string, and matches its key's type where the map has one (an `id`, or an `address` in `Universe.decisions`); every `id`-typed value matches the `id` grammar". Source: tidy-up, main, §3, §4.
  - `INVALID_SYNTAX` takes X31's clause, "in every document each string is a sequence of Unicode scalar values and each number is finite and within a double's range". The joint pass composes it with X32's (now only "UTF-8"), X36's and X37's into the one cell.
  - `DUPLICATE_KEY` is unchanged. It compares every key, string or not, and parse comes before schema, so `true:` written twice is `DUPLICATE_KEY`.
  - `INVALID_VALUE` is unchanged: no maximum.
- **§9, What is checked** (X38's closed list) gains: "A key that isn't a string is reported once, as `INVALID_ID` at its map. Its entry is left out of the document's JSON form, and nothing in it is checked. The rest of the file is checked as that form stands, so a map whose only entries have such keys is also `EMPTY_COLLECTION`."
- **§10.**
  - The `errors` bullet, after the parse-layer sentence: "Nor does a key that isn't a string. It has no JSON form, so a tool reports it from the parsed YAML, as `INVALID_ID` at its map (§3)." The defaults paragraph keeps X01's "`type` … except on a map key, which takes the key's code". `propertyNames`' `type` gives the same code at the same place in a tool that hands its validator the key unconverted.
  - "Reading `astra.yaml`" (X19's paragraph) gains: "Before `toJS()`, a tool walks the document's maps. It reports each pair whose key isn't a `Scalar` holding a string as `INVALID_ID` at its map, and deletes the pair. It refuses a scalar holding a number that isn't `Number.isFinite`, or a string that isn't `isWellFormed()` ([`INVALID_SYNTAX`](#parse-and-schema)). `yaml` reads an escaped pair as one character, as §3 does, and an integer beyond 2^53 rounded, which no check depends on. Its `uniqueKeys: true` compares keys by value alone, so a tool passes a function that compares them as §3 does: by tag (resolved from a plain scalar's `source`, since `yaml` keeps none) and canonical form. Then `1` and `1.0` are two keys, and `? [a]` written twice is one."
- **No schema or generator change.** X01's `propertyNames/type` → `INVALID_ID` stays. No `maximum_value` is added, and `tools/rules.py` gets no `maximum` default. Run `just gen-schema` for the changed `INVALID_ID` and `INVALID_SYNTAX` statements in `rules.json`.
- **`tests/conftest.py`.**
  - `construct_mapping` keeps X10's duplicate check over every key. After it, an entry whose key node isn't tagged `!!str` isn't added. The constructor records the key's source text with its map node. It still constructs the value, so a parse error inside it is raised, and anything it records inside a left-out entry is dropped with it. The `ConstructorError`s for keys equal in Python and for unhashable keys go, and so does `_canonical`'s `.nan` case: every key left is a string.
  - `construct_core_float` raises a `ConstructorError` when the value isn't `math.isfinite`. `construct_core_int` raises when `float(value)` overflows.
  - A `construct_core_str` replaces `construct_yaml_str` for `!!str`, keys included. It joins escaped pairs (`value.encode("utf-16-le", "surrogatepass").decode("utf-16-le")`) and raises a `ConstructorError` on a lone surrogate. `parse_code` gives all three `INVALID_SYNTAX`.
  - A new `load_document(source)` composes the node, indexes each map node's JSON Pointer, and constructs it. It returns the JSON form and the left-out keys, as `(pointer of the map, key as written)`. `load_yaml` and `load_yaml_text` return the JSON form, and raise a `ValueError` naming the keys if there are any. So a valid fixture, an example or a spec block with such a key fails. The docstrings say all of this.
- **`tests/test_fixtures.py`.** `check_invalid_fixture` reads a schema fixture with `load_document`. Each left-out key counts as an `INVALID_ID` error at its map's pointer, beside the validator's errors mapped through `rules.json`.
- **`tests/test_loader.py`.**
  - Through `load_document`:
    - `~:`, `? [a, b]` and `? {a: 1}` are left out and reported.
    - `1:` with `1.0:`, `1:` with `true:`, and `0:` with `false:` are each two left-out keys. This replaces `test_distinct_keys_python_cant_hold_apart_are_an_error`.
    - `1: x` with `"1": y` gives `{"1": "y"}` and one key. This rewrites `test_keys_of_another_tag_are_distinct`.
    - A key in a nested map is reported at that map's pointer, and one inside a left-out entry isn't reported.
    - `true: x` written twice is `DuplicateKeyError`.
  - These raise `INVALID_SYNTAX`: `.inf`, `-.Inf` and `.nan` as values and as keys, `1e400`, `-1e400`, an integer of 400 digits, `"\uD800"`, `"\uDC80"`, `"\uDE00\uD83D"`, and a key `"\uD800"`.
  - These load: `1e-400` as 0.0, 10^308, and `"😀"` as U+1F600.
  - The `.inf` and `-.Inf` rows of `test_plain_scalars_resolve_by_the_core_schema`, `test_nan_is_a_float`, and the `.nan` pair of `test_keys_that_are_equal_nodes_are_duplicates` move to the raising cases.
- **Fixtures.**
  - Analysis-187 gets a second output with a string key, so that leaving `1:` out doesn't empty `outputs`. Its header is unchanged.
  - New schema fixtures, both `# rule: INVALID_ID`:
    - Analysis-195-option-key-boolean: options `"true": {}` and `true: {}`. It fails at `/decisions/augment/options`, with no `DUPLICATE_KEY`, and the quoted entry is kept.
    - Analysis-196-field-key-integer: `1: x` among `result`'s fields. It fails at `/outputs/result`.
  - New parse fixtures, `INVALID_SYNTAX`, from Analysis-185: Analysis-197-gpus-infinite (`gpus: .inf`), Analysis-198-gpus-beyond-double (`gpus: 1e400`), and Analysis-199-label-lone-surrogate (`label: "\uD800"` on the output).
  - A new valid fixture, Analysis-009-quoted-keys-and-escapes: options `"true": {}` and `"false": {}`, a label `"😀"`, and `gpus: 9007199254740993` (valid: no maximum).
  - Run `yamllint` and `check-yaml` on each, and add any they reject to `.yamllint.yaml`'s `ignore` and `check-yaml`'s `exclude` (yamllint's `truthy` rule checks keys).
  - Records, with X37's `load_json`: a parse fixture with `"\ud800"` in `generated_by.runner.id`, and a record holding `"😀"` that loads. X37's `parse_float`, `parse_int` and surrogate walk apply this fix's rule. `json.loads` already joins escaped pairs. The `"size": 1e400` fixture is X37's.
- **CLAUDE.md, Tests.** The loader sentence adds that the loader refuses a non-finite or overflowing number and a lone surrogate, and leaves out an entry whose key isn't a string, reporting it. The fixture paragraph says such a key is `INVALID_ID` at its map, from `load_document`, not through `rules.json`.
- **Design.**
  - §7: the `INVALID_ID` and `INVALID_SYNTAX` rows as in §9.
  - §9: the Parse bullet as in CLAUDE.md.
  - §11: one line. "A value with no JSON form makes a file invalid. A key that isn't a string is `INVALID_ID` at its map, and its entry is left out. A non-finite number, one beyond a double's range, and a lone surrogate are `INVALID_SYNTAX`. This states change 29's 'means the same as the JSON it converts to', and needs no text of its own."

**Decided.** Tom, 2026-11-04 (pending item 12, which replaces (1a) and the maximum in (2a)):
- A key that isn't a string is `INVALID_ID`, not a new `NON_STRING_KEY`. It is reported at its map's pointer, naming the key. Its entry is left out, and the rest of the file is validated. §9's What is checked says so.
- Values with no JSON form still fail at parse as `INVALID_SYNTAX`: `.inf`, `.nan`, numbers beyond a double's range, and strings with a lone surrogate. This keeps (2a)'s parse half and (3a).
- The 2^53−1 maximum on integer fields goes. Integers above 2^53 stay valid, read exactly in Python and rounded in JavaScript.
- Item 15: no RFC text. Spec, harness and a design §11 line only.

Two consequences follow from the decision as worded, and this entry writes them down. A map whose only entries have non-string keys is also `EMPTY_COLLECTION`, since the rest is checked as it stands. A key with no JSON form at all, such as `.nan:`, fails at parse first.

**Why this route.**
- Change 29 already says a file means the same as the JSON it converts to, and that the JSON is what the schema checks. A value with no JSON form can't do that. Saying that it fails, and with which code, states change 29 rather than changing it, so a design §11 line is enough.
- `INVALID_ID` needs no new code. Change 15 already checks every map key, and X01 already gives `propertyNames/type` the key's code. A tool that hands its validator the key unconverted gets the same code at the same place.
- Leaving the entry out gives one error per bad key and keeps the rest of the file checkable, as for any other schema error.
- The numbers and surrogates are what RFC 8259 rules out or leaves undefined (`NaN`, `Infinity`, an unpaired surrogate). No file means to hold them. The pair rule is YAML 1.2's own: YAML 1.2 is a superset of JSON, so the JSON text `"😀"` means U+1F600 read as YAML too.
- With overflow failing at parse, Python and JavaScript agree on every verdict without a maximum. Only the value read differs above 2^53, and no check depends on it.

**Done when.**
- The five new fixtures and Analysis-187 pass under X09's exact-code check. A JS check following §10 (the review toolkit's `validate.mjs`, with the key walk added) gives the same codes and pointers. The new valid fixture passes in both.
- The `test_loader.py` cases pass, and `just test` is green with the regenerated `INVALID_ID` and `INVALID_SYNTAX` statements.
- No `maximum` appears in `generated/`.
- The §3 bullets and the `INVALID_SYNTAX` cell are written in one pass with X32, X36 and X37. The record cases land with X37.
- The new fixtures take 195-199, the last numbers of the invalid `Analysis` range, so X32's and X36's fixtures need numbers past it.

### X32. `astra.yaml` is UTF-8, and the harness loader reads YAML 1.2.2's characters, tabs and line breaks

- **Resolves:** F066, F067, F141, F156, F158 (the `yaml` package's halves of F067, F156 and F158 through §10's guidance, for astra-typescript to apply) · **Size:** M · **Depends on:** X10, X19 (both done); §3 and §9's `INVALID_SYNTAX` row in one pass with X31, X36, X37 · **Decision:** decided · **Route:** spec only, no RFC text, a design §11 line (if Tom confirms the RFC rule in "For the tidy-up RFC") · **Done:** `ab416fe`

**The problem.** The specification names no encoding for an `astra.yaml` and never states YAML 1.2.2's rules on characters and line breaks (§5.1-§5.4). §2's parse row says the layer sees "one file's text", which hides the decoding (F141). Both reference loaders depart from YAML 1.2.2 here, so one file gets different verdicts or values in Python and TypeScript. The harness loader in `tests/conftest.py` still has ruamel.yaml's departures after X10 (checked on the current tree):
- it reads a UTF-16 file with a byte order mark as if it were UTF-8 (F141);
- it rejects a tab used as separation: before a comment, after `:`, at a line's end, on a blank line. It rejects 18 of yaml-test-suite's well-formed tab cases (F066). X10 left its tab check out because the old X32 banned every tab;
- it reads NEL, LS and PS as line breaks: `a: json<NEL>` gives `'json'`, and a block scalar holding LS fails to parse (F067);
- it rejects DEL, C1 and U+FFFE/U+FFFF even inside quoted scalars, where YAML 1.2.2 allows every non-C0 character (F156);
- it keeps a U+FEFF inside a plain scalar as content, which YAML 1.2.2 [27] makes ill-formed.

The `yaml` package checks no characters, so it accepts raw C0, DEL and C1 in plain scalars and comments (F067, F156). It keeps a lone CR as content, where YAML 1.2.2 makes it a line break (F158). It takes only a string, so its caller picks the decoding (F141).

**The fix.**
- Spec §3, a new first bullet under `astra.yaml`: "An `astra.yaml` is UTF-8, and may start with a byte order mark ([`INVALID_SYNTAX`](#parse-and-schema)). This is ASTRA's rule: YAML 1.2.2 §5.2 also allows UTF-16 and UTF-32. Its characters and line breaks are YAML 1.2.2's (§5.1, §5.4): a control character other than tab, LF and CR is invalid anywhere; DEL, C1 other than NEL, U+FEFF, U+FFFE and U+FFFF are invalid outside a quoted scalar; NEL, LS and PS are ordinary characters; a tab is white space but never indentation; and a CR on its own ends a line."
- Spec §2: the parse row's Sees cell reads "one file's bytes".
- Spec §4 (now line 335): "After a space or a tab it starts a comment".
- §9 `INVALID_SYNTAX` Rule cell, written once with X31, X36 and X37: "Every `astra.yaml` is UTF-8 and a well-formed YAML 1.2 stream of at most one document under the core schema, with no `%YAML` directive other than 1.2, no anchor or alias and no tag but the core schema's; in every document each string is a sequence of Unicode scalar values and each number is finite and within double range; and every record is RFC 8259 JSON text in UTF-8 with no byte order mark." Add §3 to its Source cell if it isn't there. Run `just gen-schema` and commit `rules.json`.
- Spec §10, in X19's "Reading `astra.yaml`" paragraph. A tool decodes the bytes itself, strictly, as UTF-8: `new TextDecoder('utf-8', {fatal: true})` before `yaml`, and `bytes.decode('utf-8')` before ruamel.yaml, whose own reader takes UTF-16. Stock ruamel.yaml rejects tab separation, reads NEL, LS and PS as line breaks, rejects DEL, C1, U+FFFE and U+FFFF in quoted scalars, and keeps U+FEFF in a plain scalar; `tests/conftest.py` shows one way to correct each. The `yaml` package keeps a lone CR as content, so a tool replaces each CR not followed by LF with LF before parsing (exact, since YAML 1.2.2 §5.4 makes every such CR a break). It checks no characters, so a tool rejects a C0 control other than tab, LF and CR anywhere, and checks the source of each plain scalar, block scalar and comment, from the CST, for the characters §3 bars outside quotes.
- `tests/conftest.py`, the loader:
  - `load_yaml` reads `path.read_bytes()` and decodes it as strict UTF-8, raising a `ReaderError` on failure, then loads the text (a leading U+FEFF stays for the scanner to skip). `load_yaml_text` is unchanged.
  - The scanner accepts a tab wherever YAML 1.2.2 allows white space that isn't indentation (§6.1, §6.2), restoring X10's dropped bullet "a tab only where YAML allows one". It still rejects a tab in indentation, in a block scalar's indentation, and between a block indicator and a nested block collection (`-\t-`, `?\t-`, `:\t-`). A plain skip of tabs between tokens isn't enough: tried on the current tree, it loads 10 of the 18 well-formed tab cases and lets five Y79Y error cases through.
  - NEL, LS and PS are content, not breaks, everywhere the scanner reads text. ruamel.yaml hard-codes its break set in about thirty places, so one way is a reader that maps the three to private-use stand-ins the text doesn't hold, and a constructor that maps them back in every string.
  - The reader checks only C0 (but tab, LF and CR). The scanner checks the text of each plain scalar, block scalar and comment against YAML 1.2.2's printable set without U+FEFF ([1], [27]), and raises a `ScannerError`. Quoted scalars take any non-C0 character.
  - The `load_yaml` docstring, `_BREAKS`'s docstring and `test_loader.py:1` say so.
- `tests/conftest.py`, `fixture_header`: read the first two LF-ended lines from the bytes, as ASCII, so a Latin-1 fixture has a header.
- `tests/test_loader.py`, through a byte-writing `load` helper: UTF-8 with and without a BOM load; UTF-16LE and UTF-16BE with a BOM, UTF-32LE with a BOM and Latin-1 raise. Tab cases from F066 (t1-t4, t6) load, and a tab in indentation raises. `a: json<NEL>` gives `'json\x85'`, and a block scalar with LS or PS keeps it. `"F1<U+0096>score"`, `'x<DEL>y'` and `"x<U+FFFE>y"` load; the same characters plain or in a comment raise. U+FEFF in a plain scalar raises, and in a double-quoted one loads. `# note<CR>b: 1` gives `{'b': 1, …}`.
- `tests/yaml-test-suite/`: vendor the suite's well-formed cases that hold a tab and use nothing X36 refuses, each with its `in.json`. A test loads each and compares it with the JSON.
- Fixtures, at the next free numbers:
  - parse, `INVALID_SYNTAX`: a Latin-1 file (`label: Iris é` as byte `e9`), a raw U+0092 in a plain `description`, and a raw U+FEFF inside a plain `label`;
  - schema: `type: metric<LS>` (`INVALID_VALUE` at `/outputs/result/type`), and `# note<CR>bogus: 1` after the header (`UNKNOWN_FIELD` at `(root)`);
  - valid: a tab before a comment and after `:`; a raw U+0096 in a double-quoted `label` and a raw DEL in a single-quoted `description`; a NEL in a block-scalar `description`.
  - Add each file that check-yaml or yamllint rejects to `check-yaml`'s exclude and `.yamllint.yaml`'s ignore. Add the tab and CR files to `trailing-whitespace`'s and `end-of-file-fixer`'s excludes. A new `.gitattributes` marks the CR and Latin-1 files `-text`.
- CLAUDE.md Tests: the loader reads bytes as strict UTF-8, accepts tab separation, and reads NEL, LS, PS and quoted non-C0 characters as YAML 1.2.2 does; the suite's well-formed tab cases are vendored too. The hand-kept lists name the new hooks. Design §9's Parse bullet says the same.
- Design §11, a new line: "An `astra.yaml` is UTF-8 and may start with a byte order mark (YAML 1.2.2 §5.2 also allows UTF-16 and UTF-32)." Migration: re-save UTF-16 and Latin-1 files as UTF-8.

**Decided** (Tom, 2026-11-04, item 12, overriding the earlier "decided as recommended"). X32 shrinks to "`astra.yaml` is UTF-8" plus fixing the test loader. The code-point pre-scan, the tab ban and the LF-or-CRLF rule go. Item 15 puts X32 among the fixes that need only spec text and a design §11 line; that list is still to confirm with Tom.

What each dropped part leaves:
- **The code-point pre-scan.** NEL, LS, PS, DEL, C1, U+FEFF, U+FFFE and U+FFFF keep YAML 1.2.2's reading: allowed in quoted scalars (NEL, LS and PS everywhere), ill-formed raw elsewhere. The check is context-aware, so no validator gets it from one regular expression. The harness does it in its scanner. For astra-typescript it is a CST walk that §10 describes, so F067's and F156's `yaml` halves stay open until astra-typescript does it. Mojibake in a quoted `description` (a raw U+0092) stays valid, and a quoted path may hold C1, LS or PS, since `INVALID_PATH` bars only ASCII controls.
- **The tab ban.** Tabs separate tokens where YAML allows, and a block scalar can hold a TSV, a Makefile or code. The cost moves into the harness: most of this fix's work is the scanner's tab check. `PIN_LIKE_COMMENT` must see `- result<TAB>#svm` as a comment; that is astra-tools' to do, and §4 now says so.
- **The line-ending rule.** A lone CR stays a line break. The harness already reads it so. A `yaml`-based tool needs §10's one replacement, so F158's `yaml` half rests on astra-typescript applying it.

**Why this route.** YAML 1.2.2 already decides every character, tab and line-break case. Stating its rules in §3 and bringing the harness to them changes no verdict, so it needs no numbered change. Only UTF-8 narrows YAML: it makes UTF-16 and UTF-32 files invalid, so every validator decodes one way. That one rule goes in design §11, per item 15.

**Done when.** The three parse fixtures fail as `INVALID_SYNTAX`, the two schema fixtures fail at their pointers, and the valid fixtures pass. The loader cases pass. Every vendored well-formed tab case loads to its `in.json`, and every yaml-test-suite error case still raises. `rules.json` carries the new `INVALID_SYNTAX` statement. `just test` is green, with every example and spec block still valid, and `pre-commit run --all-files` leaves the fixtures' bytes unchanged.

### X33. Git is read one way: repository, state, gitlinks, submodule URLs, blobs and symlinks for validator rules

- **Resolves:** F014, F026, F027, F088, F103 · **Size:** M · **Depends on:** X35, X45 (X04 lands with it: each cites the other) · **Decision:** decided · **Route:** spec only, no RFC text, a design §11 line (if Tom confirms the RFC rule in "For the tidy-up RFC") · **Done:** `6296834`

**The problem.** The specification lets a validator read "git" (§2) without saying which repository or which state it reads. A symlink, a path under `.git/` or a path through a submodule therefore gets different verdicts from different git calls (F014). `COMMIT_NOT_FOUND` drops RFC 0007:195's "the declaring analysis's repository" (§7 "which the repository has", §9 "a commit the repository has"). Nothing says what a path read at a commit does at a gitlink (F026), or where a submodule's commit comes from when the superproject's commit is read from a remote. `INPUT_NOT_TRACKED` names no repository (F027). An input is "committed" in §5 and in `Input`'s description but "tracked" in the rule (F103). `COMMIT_NOT_ANCESTOR` compares with "the commit the declaration is in", which a working-tree file doesn't have (F088). And §7 "Validity" says "A commit's files never change, so neither does the answer", but nothing says whether an `astra.yaml` at a commit is parsed as git stores it or as a checkout writes it. A smudge filter, an `eol` attribute or a `working-tree-encoding` makes the two differ, so the answer can depend on the reader's git config.

X46 has already made stand-ins the tooling's: §5's third bullet and the `MISSING_INPUT_FILE` row now read "names a file, or something the validator recognises as standing in for one (§5)". X12 has made §2's validator and checker bullets read "every `astra.yaml` of its stack and git". Neither says how git is read.

**The fix.**
- Spec §3, after X35's sentences, a paragraph **Reading git**:
  - A path is joined to the folder of the `astra.yaml` that writes it, and its `..` segments are removed as text, never through the file system.
  - In the working tree, a path names an entry of the work tree: every entry on disk below the repository's top level, tracked, untracked or ignored, except `.git/` and everything below it. At a commit, it names an entry of that commit's tree.
  - A symlink is an entry, and is never followed. A symlink at a path is neither a file nor a directory, and a path through a symlinked folder names nothing.
  - A checked-out submodule's entries are in the work tree, and are read in the submodule's own repository. A submodule that isn't checked out is an empty folder, and nothing is fetched for it.
  - At a commit, a path that is looking for a file or an analysis continues through a gitlink into the submodule, at the commit the gitlink pins. Whether an input is tracked never crosses a gitlink. An intent-to-add entry (`git add -N`) isn't tracked, since a commit made now wouldn't hold it; an unmerged entry during a merge is tracked, since it is in the index. §5 Inputs says, as advice and with no new rule (Tom, 2026-11-04): data kept in another repository, a git submodule say, isn't an input; it belongs in the analysis's own repository or in another analysis, or comes in through an output whose recipe gets it.
  - The repository that holds a file is the innermost one: for a file in a submodule, the submodule's own. `COMMIT_NOT_FOUND` and `COMMIT_NOT_ANCESTOR` read that repository. `INPUT_NOT_TRACKED` doesn't: it reads the repository that holds the input's analysis and never crosses a gitlink, so an input in a plain data submodule isn't tracked (decision 1).
  - A submodule's pinned commit is got from any repository that has it: its local repository, or a copy kept from an earlier fetch (X45). Otherwise it is fetched from the URL that `.gitmodules` gives for it: the entry whose `path` is the gitlink's, in the `.gitmodules` blob of the commit that holds the gitlink, never `.git/config`. A relative URL (`../lib.git`) is resolved against the URL that commit was got from: the declaration's `url` for an analysis read from a remote, and in the local repository the superproject's default remote, as `git submodule` resolves it. A submodule that can't be got is unreachable ([`REMOTE_UNREACHABLE`](#validator), §7).
  - At a commit, a file is the blob git stores, byte for byte, with no filter, `.gitattributes` conversion or end-of-line change. So an `astra.yaml` at a commit is parsed from its blob. In the working tree, it is parsed from the file on disk.
- Spec §2: the layer table's validator and check rows and the validator and checker bullets say "git (§3)".
- Spec §5 Inputs: the opening sentence reads "a file tracked in git with the analysis", and "Data that isn't committed with the analysis" reads "Data that isn't tracked in git with the analysis". In the third bullet, "The path names the file itself" adds "(not a symlink, §3)", and "is tracked in git" adds ", in the repository that holds the analysis (§3)". The rest of X46's bullet stays.
- `analysis.yaml`, `Input`'s description: "A file the analysis reads, tracked in git with it and named by its path."
- Spec §7 Declarations, "A declared analysis can be a git submodule": add "read as §3 says".
- Spec §7 Analyses from a commit, the `path` sentence: "which the declaring analysis's repository has (`COMMIT_NOT_FOUND`) and which should be an ancestor of the commit the declaration is in or, for a declaration read in the working tree, of HEAD or, during a merge, MERGE_HEAD (`COMMIT_NOT_ANCESTOR`)". The next sentence ends "…from that remote, and each submodule it reaches through a gitlink as §3 says."
- Spec §7 "What is read": its first sentence adds "each file as the blob git stores (§3)". A new bullet: "the `.gitmodules` of each commit whose gitlink a path crosses, and the submodule at the commit the gitlink pins (§3)".
- Spec §7 "Results": "in that analysis's repository" reads "in the repository that holds that analysis at the commit it is read at (§3)". The last sentence's input "is got from its `path` at the declared commit, in the repository that holds the declared analysis (§3)".
- Spec §9 validator rows, final text (X04 and X45 edit the same rows; keep their parts):
  - `MISSING_INPUT_FILE`: "An input's `path` names a file (not a symlink, directory or gitlink), or something the validator recognises as standing in for one (§5)" · `0006, §3`.
  - `INPUT_NOT_TRACKED`: "An input's file, or what stands in for it (§5), is in the index of the repository that holds the input's analysis or, read at a commit, in that commit's tree there, reached through no gitlink" · `0006, §3`.
  - `COMMIT_NOT_FOUND`, with X45's clause: "A declaration with a `path` and a `commit`, read from the local repository, names a commit the declaring analysis's repository has" · `0007, tidy-up, §3`.
  - `COMMIT_NOT_ANCESTOR`: "A declaration with a `path` and a `commit` names an ancestor of the commit the declaration is in or, for a declaration read in the working tree, of HEAD or (during a merge) MERGE_HEAD of the repository that holds the declaring file" · `0007, §3`.
  - `MISSING_DECLARED_ANALYSIS`: "A declaration's `path` (at its `commit`, if it has one), or the top level of its `url`'s repository at its `commit`, holds an `astra.yaml` file, with no symlink on the way (§3)" · `0004, 0007, §3`.
  - `REMOTE_UNREACHABLE`, X45's row widened: "Every commit the stack reads from a remote (a declaration's `url`, or the URL `.gitmodules` gives for a submodule that a path crosses at a commit) can be fetched from it, or was kept from an earlier fetch" · `0007, tidy-up`. X45's rule on where it is reported applies unchanged: on the declaration of the outermost analysis from a commit on the way.
- Tidy-up change 30 (X45's, "Reaching a remote is checked each time") gains one bullet: "A submodule that a path crosses at a commit is got from the URL `.gitmodules` gives for it at that commit, a relative URL resolved against the declaration's `url`. Not getting it is not reaching a remote (`REMOTE_UNREACHABLE`)."
- Design §7: the validator rows match §9. Design §10: the RFC 0004 row "Submodules allowed…" adds "read as spec 3 says"; the RFC 0006 row "Tracked in git" reads "Tracked in git, in the repository that holds the analysis; a symlink only as a stand-in | spec 3; `INPUT_NOT_TRACKED`, `MISSING_INPUT_FILE`"; the RFC 0007 row "`path` with `commit`…" adds "spec 3", and "Bytes from what git holds at the commit" adds "(the blob, unfiltered; spec 3)". Design §11: a new item, "Git is read one way (spec §3, Reading git): the work tree without `.git/`; symlinks never followed; gitlinks crossed at a commit, never for tracking; the innermost repository; the index for tracked; HEAD or MERGE_HEAD for the working tree's commit; a submodule's URL from `.gitmodules` at the commit; blobs unfiltered. RFCs 0004, 0006 and 0007 leave all of it open." Its closing paragraph says this item stays in the design, except the submodule bullet in change 30.
- Run `just gen-schema` and `just gen-doc`.

**Decided** (Tom, 2026-11-04).
1. Where "tracked" is read: in the index, or at a commit in its tree. A new input then passes before it is committed, and a pre-commit hook can run the validator. An input in a plain data submodule exists but fails `INPUT_NOT_TRACKED`, in the working tree and at a commit alike, since RFC 0006:24 and :65 send another repository's data through a recipe. An untracked or ignored input fails the same way.
2. The commit `COMMIT_NOT_ANCESTOR` compares with in the working tree: HEAD, or MERGE_HEAD during a merge. Being an ancestor of either passes.
3. Gitlinks for declarations are followed everywhere, as `Generation.commit` pins submodules (RFC 0005:119). F026's L2 `commit: ROOTC` passes. X54's decision 1(a) assumes this.
4. A symlink at or above an input path is accepted only where the validator recognises it as a stand-in (X46). Any other symlink, a symlinked folder included, fails `MISSING_INPUT_FILE`.
5. A symlink on a declaration's path (`b: {path: stage_link}`, a symlinked folder) isn't followed, as F014 suggests and git reads it, so the declaration fails `MISSING_DECLARED_ANALYSIS`.
6. A submodule reached through a gitlink in a commit read from a remote is fetched from the URL `.gitmodules` gives at that commit, a relative URL resolved against the declaration's `url`. Failing to fetch it is `REMOTE_UNREACHABLE`, widened to every submodule the declared analysis reaches through a gitlink, at its pinned commit (with X45).
7. An `astra.yaml` at a commit is parsed from the raw blob; in the working tree, from the file on disk.
8. Route: X33 is spec, schema and a design §11 item. Only decision 6 is RFC text, in X45's change 30.

**Why this route.** No RFC, design section or tidy-up change says which repository or git state a validator reads (F027, F103), how a gitlink is crossed (F026), which commit stands for the working tree (F088), whether a symlink is followed (F014), where a submodule's commit is fetched from, or which bytes of a commit are parsed. Settling these binds every validator, and decisions 4 and 5 make files invalid that pass today in a validator that reads the disk. They fill gaps the RFCs leave rather than change what an RFC says, so design §11 records them and the RFC doesn't (decision 8). Decision 6 widens `REMOTE_UNREACHABLE`, which X45's change 30 already amends, so it goes into that change. Parsing the blob keeps §7's "A commit's files never change, so neither does the answer" true whatever the reader's git config, and matches X54, where content and size are the bytes git stores. Restoring RFC 0007:195's qualifier is only drift, so it rides along.

**Done when.** §3's **Reading git** paragraph exists, and §2, §5, §7 and `Input`'s description read as above. No sentence calls an input "committed". `rules.json` carries the six rows above and `just test` passes, with no fixture change (all six rules are validator-layer). Change 30 has the submodule bullet. Design §7, §10 and §11 read as above. Design §12's conformance-suite item lists these validator cases: F026's L1 (passes) and L2 (`LIBC2` gives `COMMIT_NOT_FOUND`; `ROOTC` passes); a staged input (passes); an ignored input; a data-submodule input at HEAD and at a commit; a symlink input the validator doesn't recognise; a symlinked declaration; an uncommitted declaration during a merge; a submodule not checked out in the working tree (`MISSING_DECLARED_ANALYSIS`); a submodule crossed at a commit read from a remote, with a relative `.gitmodules` URL (passes); the same with the submodule's remote gone and nothing kept (one `REMOTE_UNREACHABLE` on the outer declaration); and an `astra.yaml` at a commit whose `.gitattributes` sets a filter or `working-tree-encoding` (judged by its blob).

### X34. Change 30: driving and pins defined once

- **Resolves:** F003, F004, F033, F034 · **Size:** M · **Depends on:** X04 · **Decision:** decided · **Done:** `1a8e424`

**The problem.** §7 defines driving in one sentence, and §6 builds the effective selection by "writing over" a map of flat keys. A decision has several spellings, so the merge can work by key or by decision (F004). The text never says which declarations a key drives, whether a universe's own keys or its effective selection count, or whether driving is counted per chain or per declaration entry (F033). It doesn't say which declaration of a chain decides whose universe a pin names (F003), or in which file's frame a pin resolves when its analysis is driven from above (F034). RFC 0004 has the same gaps (:137, :334, :357, :413). Each answer rests on the one before it. An analysis with no decisions has no key to drive it. Yet RFC 0004 builds `subanalysis3.output3_per_universe` in the root's universes (:293-305), and RFC 0005 records it there (:62, :68). As §7 is worded, `subanalysis3` is never driven, so that output has no universe to be built in. Design §13 leaves this point open.

**The fix.**
- Tidy-up RFC: add change 30, "Driving and pins are defined once", under Tightening. The joint pass fixes its number, since X37 and X45 also claim 30. Its example covers root → `fit` → `pre` (a depth-2 key, a derived universe, a depth-2 pin, a pin in `fit`'s own file). It also covers a stack where the root drives `stage` but not `stage.lib`, and `stage`'s universes drive `lib`: `stage.lib.out#v1` in the root's file names `stage`'s `v1`. A third part is RFC 0004's `subanalysis3` (:223-241, :293-305): it has no decisions and no universes, so it is driven by every universe of the root. `output3_per_universe` is built in each of the root's universes and belongs to the root. `output3` reads only `root.data`, so it is still built once and belongs to `subanalysis3`. Its sources are RFC 0003:66 and RFC 0004:223-241 and :293-305.
- §6: a key resolves through its declarations to a decision (the analysis's identity, X04, and the decision's id). Step 2: "each of the universe's own selections is set on its decision: every inherited key for that decision takes the new option, and the key is added." Add `CONFLICTING_SELECTIONS` and `PARTIALLY_DRIVEN` to the list at :562-571.
- §7 Driving: "A universe **drives** a declaration when a key in its effective selection goes through every declaration from its analysis's file to that one. A declaration of an analysis with no decisions and no universes of its own is driven by every universe that drives the chain before it (every universe of the driver, when it is declared in the driver's file). Each chain (`stage`, `stage.inner`, `inner`) is driven by all of the driver's universes or by none." The clause covers only an analysis whose file has no `decisions` and no `universes`. An analysis with universes of its own keeps them unless a key drives it. X51 adds the universes that `when` leaves with nothing to set.
- §7 The universe being built: "In the file of the analysis whose universe is being built, an unpinned read, or a consumed decision, needs the address's last declaration driven by the file's universes (X13). A pin resolves in the file it is written in. It names a universe of the first analysis along the reference, starting from the file's own, whose universes drive the reference's last declaration. If there is none, it names a universe of the analysis whose output it is." §8 Results (:852-854) then reads one universe under every root. A read written in a driven analysis's own file comes under `NOT_BUILT_IN_UNIVERSE` alone (X13). So `subanalysis3`'s `subanalysis2.output2` is allowed: the root drives `subanalysis2` through its own declaration, and the same directory is the same analysis.
- §9 rows, shared with X38: `MISSING_DECISION` "Every universe's effective selection sets every active decision of its analysis and of every analysis reached through a declaration that a universe of its analysis drives". Reword `PARTIALLY_DRIVEN` (per chain), `CONFLICTING_SELECTIONS` ("in a universe's effective selection") and `UNKNOWN_PIN_UNIVERSE` to match. X13's `UNDRIVEN_DECLARATION` row becomes "…ends in a declaration that the file's universes drive (a key in their effective selection goes through every declaration of the address, or the clause for an analysis with no decisions and no universes applies)". Then run `just gen-schema`.
- `analysis.yaml` Universe description: "written over decision by decision". Design §10:577-586 cites change 30. Its row at :581 adds "and one with no decisions and no universes is driven by every universe of its driver".
- Design §13: mark "An analysis with no decisions, reading a per-universe output" settled by change 30.
- X06's bullet "A pin in its own file … still reads one of them" stands as written, since the pin resolves in the file's frame.

**Decided.** All four recommendations: (1) merge selections by decision, not key by key; (2) judge driving on the effective selection and per chain, not on a universe's own keys or per declaration entry; (3) the reference's last declaration decides pins and unpinned reads, not any declaration on the path; (4) a pin resolves in the frame of the file it is written in, not the root's. From X51 part 2, option (iii): an analysis with no decisions and no universes of its own counts as driven by every universe of its driver. Option (i) would override an analysis's own universes, and (ii) would reject RFC 0004's `subanalysis3`.

**Why this route.** The accepted RFCs leave each question open, and design §2 and §13 don't settle them. Changes 1-29 don't touch driving or pins. Each answer changes the verdicts validators give on stacks that are valid at the schema layer. The no-decisions clause belongs here, not in the when RFC, because it doesn't involve `when` and it completes the definition of "drives". Per chain is the stricter reading, so it can be loosened later without breaking files.

**Done when.** Change 30's example records one verdict for each repro stack (F003, F004, F033, F034, and the `a`/`b` and `stage.lib` stacks):
- F004's derived universe that changes a decision under another spelling passes, with no `CONFLICTING_SELECTIONS`.
- F033's `a`/`b` stack (`u1` sets `a.c.k`, `u2` sets `b.c.k`) fails `PARTIALLY_DRIVEN`.
- `stage.inner_undriven.result` in the root's file fails `UNDRIVEN_DECLARATION`.
- `stage.lib.out#v1` names `stage`'s `v1`.
- RFC 0004's `subanalysis3` is valid. `output3_per_universe` is planned in each root universe and named `subanalysis3.output3_per_universe` (RFC 0005:62), and `output3` is built once.
- X51's `sub3` stack keeps its own universes: `sub3` has universes `s1` and `s2` and no decisions, and the root declares it without keys. `sub3.out#s1` in the root's file names `sub3`'s `s1`, and an unpinned `sub3.out` fails `UNDRIVEN_DECLARATION`.
- F091 case 3 gives `MISSING_DECISION` and `PARTIALLY_DRIVEN`.

`just test` passes with the regenerated `rules.json`.

### X35. Paths are git's paths: case- and Unicode-exact, no Windows or length rule

- **Resolves:** F010, F160, F162 · **Partly:** F014, F011 · **Size:** S · **Decision:** decided · **Done:** `5368cc0`

**The problem.** The specification says how a path is written but not what it names: a string matched against git, or a name a file system resolves. So a Windows drive path passes as a relative path that each OS reads differently (F010). The places §8 builds from ids and formats can be names Windows reserves (`con`, `aux`, `csv.`) or longer than a file system holds (F160). Paths that differ only in case or Unicode form are valid and become one file on macOS or Windows (F162, and F014's case facet).

**The fix.**
- Tidy-up change 27, one sentence after "as git does": "A path names the entry git holds at exactly that path, byte for byte, so case and Unicode form count."
- §3, after the first paragraph: a path in an analysis, and each place §8 builds, is written as git writes a path in a commit, the same on every machine; a tool on Windows maps it onto its own paths, as git does. It names the entry git holds at exactly that path (read as §3's **Reading git** says, X33), case and Unicode form included; a match that ignores either names nothing (`MISSING_INPUT_FILE`, `MISSING_DECLARED_ANALYSIS`). Two paths that differ only in case or Unicode form name two different files or folders, as in git, so `Stage` and `stage` declare two analyses (§7). `astra.yaml` needn't be Windows-compatible: `C:/x` is a folder `C:`, and a device name such as `con` or a name ending in `.` is valid. No ASTRA rule bounds the length of an id, a format or a path: a path git can't check out on a machine, such as a name over the file system's limit (255 bytes on ext4), can't be used there.
- §5 Inputs and §7 Declarations: each `path` paragraph adds "(§3)". §4's "no id is reserved" adds "(not even a name Windows reserves, §3)".
- New `tests/data/valid/Analysis-008-git-paths.yaml`: inputs `C:/data.csv`, `data/A.csv` and `data/a.csv`; a declaration at `C:repo`; outputs `aux` and one with `format: "csv."`; a decision `prior` with options `pro` and `con`, and a universe `con` with `decisions: {prior: con}`.

**Decision** (Tom, 2026-11-04: decided as recommended). Decisions 1-3 settle what is valid. Tom still confirms stating decision 1 in §3 (recommended: readers of the specification don't see the RFC) rather than leaving it to change 27 alone.

**Why this route.** Change 27 says how a path is written, not how it is looked up (F014), so decision 2 binds every validator: a macOS or Windows validator that opens files through the file system accepts `data/Iris.csv` today and must reject it afterwards. That needs a tidy-up sentence; the rest of §3 restates change 27 and decisions 1 and 3, and changes no pattern or rule.

**Done when.** Change 27 has the sentence, and §3 says the above. The new valid fixture passes, and `just test` passes with `generated/` unchanged. With X17, which drops §7's "`C:/repo` is a local path" sentence.

### X36. Change 29: core-schema tags only, no anchors or aliases, at most one document

- **Resolves:** F056, F068, F069 · **Size:** M · **Depends on:** X10, X19 · **Decision:** decided · **Done:** `776895c`

**The problem.** §3 says `astra.yaml` is read "strictly, under the core schema only" and then lists what that means. Three YAML features aren't on the list, and a loader that follows the spec's words reads them differently. Tags: YAML 1.2.2 §10.4 lets the core schema carry other tags. The harness rejects `!not` and `!!omap`, and the `yaml` package builds them or only warns (F056). Anchors and aliases: §3 doesn't mention them, loaders set different limits, and a refusal has no code. The harness loads them today, and Analysis-181 and `test_loader.py:108` rely on them (F068). Documents: no rule says a file is one document, so Go and Ruby read the first and drop the rest, and §3's "read as one document, as `astra.yaml` is" (:185-186) is ambiguous (F069).

**The fix.**
- Tidy-up change 29 adds: "A node's tag, once resolved, is one of the core schema's (`tag:yaml.org,2002:` `str`, `int`, `float`, `bool`, `null`, `seq` or `map`, written `!!str` … by default), on a node of its kind; a node tagged `!` is a string, a list or a map (YAML 1.2.2 §6.9.1). A file uses no anchor and no alias, so an entry is written out in full. A file holds at most one document; a file with none (empty, or only comments) is null, and fails as `WRONG_TYPE`." The migration list adds three steps: remove other tags, write aliased entries out in full, and remove a second document.
- Spec §3 gets one bullet for each rule, under `INVALID_SYNTAX`. §3:185-186 becomes "A record is one JSON value, checked by the same schema rules", and X37 builds its Records paragraph on that wording. §9's `INVALID_SYNTAX` row: "Every `astra.yaml` is a YAML 1.2 stream of at most one document, well-formed, with no `%YAML` directive other than 1.2, no anchor or alias, and no tag that resolves outside the core schema …". Make these edits in one pass with X31 and X32, then run `just gen-schema`.
- §10, in the "Reading `astra.yaml`" paragraph that X19 creates: add `resolveKnownTags: false` to its options, and say that a JS loader refuses every anchor, every alias and every tag outside the core schema.
- `tests/conftest.py`: a Composer raises `ComposerError` on an anchor or an alias, and the docstring says so. The loader already rejects other tags and a second document. `tests/test_loader.py` adds these cases: an anchor, an alias, `a: 1\n---\n`, `%TAG ! tag:yaml.org,2002:` with `!str x` (loads), `!<tag:yaml.org,2002:str> x` (loads) and `%TAG !! tag:example.com,2000:` with `!!str x` (raises). Rewrite `test_merge_key_is_an_ordinary_key` as `other: {<<: {x: 1}}`, which gives `{'other': {'<<': {'x': 1}}}`.
- Fixtures, from Analysis-187: a local tag, `!!omap`, an anchor with an alias, and a trailing `---`. Add each one the linters reject to `.pre-commit-config.yaml` and `.yamllint.yaml`. Rewrite Analysis-181 with `<<: {type: metric}`. CLAUDE.md Tests adds "no anchors or aliases, at most one document".

**Decision** (Tom, 2026-11-04: decided as recommended).
1. Tags. (a) Only the core tags, with `!` read as YAML reads it. (b) No explicit tags at all, so `!` is refused too. That replaces X10's composer change (`!` resolves to a string) with a rejection. (c) The open text as it stands. Recommended: (a). It is what the harness does, and under (c) `!not` could reverse a `when`.
2. Anchors and aliases. (a) Forbid them. (b) Set a floor that every validator accepts, with a new code for going beyond it. (c) No bound. Recommended: (a). It needs no number and no new code, and it matches the rule for merge keys. It tightens the harness as well as the specification.
3. Documents. (a) At most one, so an empty file is `WRONG_TYPE`. (b) Exactly one, so an empty file is `INVALID_SYNTAX`. Recommended: (a). It is what the harness reports. Loaders differ here (Ruby's `safe_load('')` gives `false`, Go gives a zero value), so change 29 binds it.

**Why this route.** Change 29 as drafted covers only the `%YAML` directive, repeated keys and merge keys. Each rule here makes files invalid that YAML 1.2 accepts, or binds a reading on which loaders differ, so it needs a numbered change.

**Done when.** The four new parse fixtures pass, the rewritten Analysis-181 still fails as `UNKNOWN_FIELD`, and the rewritten `test_merge_key_is_an_ordinary_key` passes. The new `test_loader.py` cases pass, and a grep finds no anchor or alias left in `examples/`, `tests/` or `docs/`. `just test` is green with the new `INVALID_SYNTAX` statement.

### X37. Records are strict JSON documents; a broken record is `missing`, reported as INVALID_RECORD

- **Resolves:** F020, F050, F100 · **Size:** M · **Depends on:** X31, X03, X09 · **Decision:** decided · **Done:** `5a97779`

**The problem.** RFC 0005 judges a record only in the check layer: a file that breaks a rule of its §1 or §2 leaves its result `missing` (0005:142, :199). The specification also puts records in the parse and schema layers as documents, and nothing records that departure. It never names the parser a record is read with: §2 says a YAML 1.2 loader, and §3 and §9 say well-formed JSON. Stock JSON parsers keep the last of two repeated names, and the harness reads records only as YAML (F020). Nothing says which code a checker reports when a record breaks a schema rule (F050), and the `missing` row leaves out a record that breaks a parse rule (F100).

**The fix.**
- Tidy-up RFC, a new change 30 under Tightening, "A record is strict JSON, judged as a document". A record is RFC 8259 JSON text in UTF-8 with no byte order mark. It uses only JSON's forms: no comment, trailing comma, `NaN` or `Infinity` (`INVALID_SYNTAX`). A repeated name in an object is `DUPLICATE_KEY`. Its strings and numbers follow change 29's "Every document" paragraph from X31. A record that breaks a parse or schema rule leaves its result `missing`, reported as `INVALID_RECORD`. Add it to the migration list and to design §11.
- Spec §2: the parse row reads "a strict YAML 1.2 loader (`astra.yaml`) or a strict JSON parser (records)". The checker bullet says that a record which breaks a parse or schema rule leaves its result `missing`.
- Spec §3 Records: state the rule above. Add that `json.loads` and `JSON.parse` keep the last repeated name, so a checker needs a parser that reports repeated names.
- §9, one wording shared with X31. `INVALID_SYNTAX` reads: "Every `astra.yaml` is well-formed YAML 1.2 under the core schema (with no `%YAML` directive other than 1.2), every record is RFC 8259 JSON text in UTF-8 with no byte order mark, and in every document each string is a sequence of Unicode scalar values and each number is finite and within double range." `DUPLICATE_KEY` adds "and every name of a record's object". Build `INVALID_RECORD` on X03's wording: "The record at each planned result's place breaks no parse rule, and no schema rule against …". The `missing` status row adds "(including a record that breaks a parse or schema rule)". The check introduction and §10 say that a checker reports `INVALID_RECORD`, with each underlying code as its reason: the parser's code, or the one `rules.json` gives the error. Regenerate `rules.json`.
- `tests/conftest.py`: add `load_json`. It decodes the bytes as strict UTF-8 and rejects a leading U+FEFF. It calls `json.loads` with four hooks. `object_pairs_hook` raises on a repeated name. `parse_constant` raises on `NaN`/`Infinity`. `parse_float` raises when `float()` gives inf (`1e400`). `parse_int` raises past double range. A post-load walk rejects lone surrogates (X31's rule). Extend X09's `parse_code` to these errors, and make `fixtures()` take `*.json` too.
- Fixtures: convert the 16 record fixtures to `.json`. Each invalid one keeps its two header lines in a sidecar `<stem>.header`. Add parse fixtures for a repeated `size`, a trailing comma, `NaN`, a comment, a BOM, `"size": 1e400`, a bad UTF-8 byte and YAML block form. Switch `test_provenance.py` and `test_specification.py:103` to `load_json`. Update CLAUDE.md's fixture convention.
- Related, not resolved here: F046 (the bytes status of an invalid record, X57) and F098 (records older than any schema, X59).

**Decision** (Tom, 2026-11-04: decided as recommended). (1) The leading BOM, to be chosen together with X32, which recommends the opposite for `astra.yaml` ("An `astra.yaml` is UTF-8 and may start with a BOM"). For records, rejecting it is recommended. People write `astra.yaml` in editors, and some add a BOM. Runners write records, and RFC 8259 §8.1 says implementations MUST NOT add one. `JSON.parse` already rejects one, so allowing it makes each checker strip it in its own way. (2) The code. Option A, recommended: `INVALID_RECORD`, with the parse or schema code as its reason. Every result then has one check code, `rules.json` and the existing fixtures keep their codes, and RFC 0005's layering holds. Option B: report the underlying code alone. That mixes parse and schema codes into check reports and leaves `INVALID_RECORD` empty. Option C: take records out of the parse and schema layers. That loses the reason, and `rules.json` and every record fixture have to be recoded.

**Why this route.** RFC 0005 says only that records are `.json` files, judged in the check layer. Tidy-up change 22 covers only unknown fields, and change 29 covers only YAML. Neither says how a record is parsed or which code a checker reports. Both answers bind tools, and the parse layer is a departure that is implemented but recorded nowhere.

**Done when.** `just test` passes, every record fixture is `.json` and loads through `load_json`, and each new parse fixture (the `1e400` one included) raises its exact code. The regenerated `rules.json` carries the changed `INVALID_SYNTAX`, `DUPLICATE_KEY` and `INVALID_RECORD` statements.

### X38. No cascades: a closed list of the rules left unchecked where another broken rule leaves their subject undetermined

- **Resolves:** F052, F091, F107 · **Size:** M · **Depends on:** X34, X31 · **Decision:** decided · **Route:** spec only, no RFC text, a design §11 line (if Tom confirms the RFC rule in "For the tidy-up RFC") · **Done:** `52aa2c2`

**The problem.** A validator "reports every parse, schema and validator rule the analysis breaks" (spec §2). The catalogue never says whether a rule counts as broken when another broken rule leaves its subject undetermined. So for one fault a tool can report one code or a cascade of them. A missing or malformed `spec_version` can also break `UNSUPPORTED_SPEC_VERSION`, and then there is no schema to read the file by (F052). An unreadable commit or remote also breaks `MISSING_DECLARED_ANALYSIS`, and every address through it breaks `UNRESOLVED_ADDRESS` (F107). A universe whose `derived_from` doesn't resolve, or whose chain loops, has no effective selection. As worded ("No universe is derived from itself, directly or through others"), `from_loop` escapes `DERIVED_FROM_CYCLE`, and "every analysis it drives" reads two ways (F091).

**The fix.**
- Spec §9 preface: a paragraph **What is checked**, after the layer table and the paragraph on `MAY` rows, before **The catalogue as data**. It is a bold-led paragraph with a numbered list, not a `###` subsection and not a table with a Code column, so the extractor ignores it. `rules.json` doesn't carry it yet (item 5), and the §9 rows keep their statements: the list, not the rows, says what is skipped. Each code links to its table, as elsewhere in §9. The text:

  > **What is checked.** A tool reports every rule broken, except in the cases below, and the list is closed. In each case another broken rule leaves what a rule is about undetermined, so the rule isn't checked and isn't reported. Each case starts from a broken `MUST`. So a rule left unchecked never makes an invalid analysis valid, or a `missing` result anything else: it changes only which codes are reported. Two rules whose subjects are both determined are both reported, even when one edit fixes both.
  >
  > 1. **A key that isn't a string.** A map entry whose key isn't a string (`1:`, `true:`, `~:`) is reported once, as `INVALID_ID` at its map, naming the key. The entry is left out, and the rest of the file is checked without it.
  > 2. **A file that can't be read.** A file that breaks a parse rule is checked no further. A file whose `spec_version` is missing or malformed isn't checked by `UNSUPPORTED_SPEC_VERSION`: it is checked against the validator's own schema (§1). A file whose well-formed `spec_version` breaks `UNSUPPORTED_SPEC_VERSION` isn't checked by the schema layer.
  > 3. **A file that isn't a valid document.** A file that breaks a parse or schema rule, or `UNSUPPORTED_SPEC_VERSION`, isn't checked by the validator layer, and its declarations aren't followed. Its analysis is undetermined (5).
  > 4. **A declaration that can't be read.** On a declaration whose own analysis can't be got, because it breaks `DECLARATION_OUTSIDE_REPOSITORY`, `COMMIT_NOT_FOUND`, or `REMOTE_UNREACHABLE` for its own `url`, neither `MISSING_DECLARED_ANALYSIS` nor `DECLARED_ANALYSIS_INVALID` is checked. On one that breaks `COMMIT_NOT_FOUND`, `COMMIT_NOT_ANCESTOR` isn't checked either. Its analysis is undetermined (5), and so is the analysis of a declaration that breaks `MISSING_DECLARED_ANALYSIS`. `DECLARED_ANALYSIS_INVALID` isn't checked on an analysis from a commit whose file breaks `UNSUPPORTED_SPEC_VERSION`. Where `REMOTE_UNREACHABLE` is reported on a declaration for a commit further in (§7), `DECLARED_ANALYSIS_INVALID` isn't checked on that declaration, and the analysis that couldn't be got is undetermined. The analyses read on the way to it are not: their declarations' `MISSING_DECLARED_ANALYSIS`, and addresses into them such as `upstream.summary#u1`, are still checked.
  > 5. **An undetermined analysis.** No validator rule is checked in its file. In the files that declare it, nothing reached through that declaration is checked: no address through it, by `UNRESOLVED_ADDRESS` or by any rule on what the address would name, no `MISSING_DECISION` for its decisions, and no `PARTIALLY_DRIVEN` for a chain through it.
  > 6. **A name that names nothing.** An address that breaks `UNRESOLVED_ADDRESS`, and an option that breaks `UNKNOWN_OPTION`, name nothing, and no rule is checked on what they would name. That leaves out `UNKNOWN_OPTION` under such an address, the pin rules (`PIN_ON_INPUT`, `UNKNOWN_PIN_UNIVERSE`, `PIN_INACTIVE`, `REDUNDANT_PIN`, `UNPINNED_EVIDENCE`), and `NOT_BUILT_IN_UNIVERSE`, `UNDRIVEN_DECLARATION`, `INPUT_NOT_BUILT`, `OUTPUT_CYCLE` and <item 1's code> through it. A selection that names nothing isn't checked by `INACTIVE_DECISION`, `CONFLICTING_SELECTIONS`, `MISSING_REQUIRED_OPTION`, `INCOMPATIBLE_OPTIONS` or `EXCLUDED_OPTION_SELECTED`. A `requires` or `incompatible_with` entry that names nothing is ignored.
  > 7. **An element whose activity is undetermined.** In a universe, a `when` is undetermined when one of its conditions names nothing (6), or tests a decision that the universe's selection leaves unset (`MISSING_DECISION`) or sets to an option that names nothing. Its element's activity in that universe is then undetermined. `MISSING_DECISION` and `INACTIVE_DECISION` aren't checked for such a decision, `INPUT_NOT_BUILT` and `PIN_INACTIVE` aren't checked for such an output, and step 3 of the effective selection doesn't drop it.
  > 8. **A universe with no effective selection.** A universe has none when its `derived_from` breaks `UNKNOWN_DERIVED_FROM`, or its chain breaks `DERIVED_FROM_CYCLE` or reaches a universe that has none. For it, `MISSING_DECISION`, `INACTIVE_DECISION`, `MISSING_REQUIRED_OPTION`, `INCOMPATIBLE_OPTIONS`, `EXCLUDED_OPTION_SELECTED`, `CONFLICTING_SELECTIONS`, `NOT_BUILT_IN_UNIVERSE`, `UNDRIVEN_DECLARATION` and `INPUT_NOT_BUILT` aren't checked, and `PIN_INACTIVE` isn't checked for a pin naming it. `UNRESOLVED_ADDRESS`, `UNKNOWN_OPTION` and <item 1's code> are still checked on its own `decisions`. `PARTIALLY_DRIVEN` is judged over the driver's other universes.
  > 9. **An input with no file.** An input that breaks `MISSING_INPUT_FILE` isn't checked by `INPUT_NOT_TRACKED`.
  > 10. **A result with no valid record.** A result that breaks `MISSING_RECORD` isn't checked by `INVALID_RECORD`, `MISPLACED_RECORD` or `MISSING_RECORD_INPUTS`. One whose record breaks `INVALID_RECORD` isn't checked by the last two. `STALE_RESULT` isn't checked for a result that is `missing`.

  <item 1's code> is the new validator code of decision item 1 (two spellings of one decision in one universe, two addresses of one element in one list), named when that fix is written; cases 6 and 8 name it once it exists. X55 and X57 add their check-phase cases to item 10 when they land. X59 adds nothing: under item 6, a record with no record schema for its version breaks `INVALID_RECORD`, so it is `missing` and item 10 covers it. X43's restriction of the universe-being-built rules to the universes built is a scope, not a cascade, and stays in §7.
- §2, validator role: it "reports every parse, schema and validator rule the analysis breaks, except where §9's *What is checked* leaves one unchecked". Written in one pass with X43's edit to the same sentence.
- §1 Versions, after "A validator reads each `astra.yaml` by the version it states": "A file whose `spec_version` is missing or malformed is checked against the validator's own schema, and every schema error in it is reported." Under item 6 that schema is the one of the validator's minor version. The order sentence ("Versions are ordered by MAJOR, then MINOR, then PATCH …") goes back to X59, which still needs it for the open variant, and so do the per-minor wording and design §12's versioning item.
- §7 Analyses from a commit, **Validity**: "A rule broken inside an analysis from a commit is reported twice: `DECLARED_ANALYSIS_INVALID` on the declaration, in the file the author can edit, and the rule's own code at its place in the commit's file, which says why." Coordinate with X43 and X45, which edit the same paragraph.
- §6 Declaring universes: "No universe is derived from itself, directly or through others" becomes RFC 0003:227's wording, "No universe's chain of `derived_from` loops" ([`DERIVED_FROM_CYCLE`](#validator)). The §9 row and design §7's copy (design :420) take the same sentence. After §6's effective-selection steps: "A universe whose `derived_from` doesn't resolve, or whose chain loops or reaches a universe that has none, has no effective selection (§9, *What is checked*)."
- Driving takes X34's text, which settles F091's pronoun. The `MISSING_DECISION` row reads "…of its analysis and of every analysis reached through a declaration that a universe of its analysis drives". §6 Declaring universes and its first effective-selection bullet, §7 Driving's first bullet, §7 The universe being built ("pushed through every analysis it drives"), and the `Universe` and `Universe.decisions` descriptions follow X34. A universe that sets none of a chain's decisions while the driver's other universes drive it breaks both `PARTIALLY_DRIVEN` and `MISSING_DECISION`: both subjects are determined, and RFC 0004:407 and :408 are two rules.
- Design §11: one line (item 15): "A rule isn't checked where another broken rule leaves what it is about undetermined. Spec §9's *What is checked* lists the cases, closed. Every case starts from a broken `MUST`, so it changes which codes are reported, never validity."
- Design §12's conformance-suite item gains the cases under Done when.
- The plan's "For astra-tools" bullet on no cascades drops "That matches the validation report's `unchecked[]`" (item 9): skipped checks aren't reported at all, while `unchecked[]` lists codes a tool doesn't implement.
- Run `just gen-schema` and commit `rules.json`. From X38 alone, only `DERIVED_FROM_CYCLE`'s statement changes; X34 brings `MISSING_DECISION`'s and the descriptions.
