**Decided.** Tom chose to report every error, but not the ones that follow from another: a rule whose subject another broken rule leaves undetermined isn't checked, rather than reporting every code. The skipped rules are a closed list in §9's *What is checked*, not in `rules.json` yet (item 5). A non-string key is one of the cases: `INVALID_ID` at the map, naming the key, the entry left out and the rest checked (item 12, which replaces X31's `NON_STRING_KEY`). X38 needs no RFC text, only spec text and a design §11 line (item 15; Tom confirms the RFC scope when the RFC text is written). Every other recommendation stands: a missing or malformed `spec_version` is checked against the validator's own schema with every error reported (not only the `spec_version` one), a broken analysis from a commit gives `DECLARED_ANALYSIS_INVALID` and the inner codes (not only one of them), and `PARTIALLY_DRIVEN` is judged over the driver's other universes (not skipped for that driver).

**Why this route.** RFC 0004 §5, RFC 0007:198 ("Every other rule … applies as it is") and change 19 name the rules. None of them says which rules apply when another one is broken, and settling that decides which codes tools report. It needs no RFC text: every case starts from a broken `MUST`, so no analysis changes validity, and which codes are reported is the catalogue's business. A closed list, rather than the principle alone, lets a conformance suite fix the expected codes. The `DERIVED_FROM_CYCLE` wording only restores RFC 0003:227, so it rides along.

**Done when.**
- §9 has the *What is checked* paragraph with the ten cases, and the extractor and `just test` pass with the regenerated `rules.json`.
- A new invalid fixture `spec_version: 0.1` gives `WRONG_TYPE` at `/spec_version`, beside Analysis-108 (`MISSING_FIELD`) and Analysis-133 (`INVALID_VALUE`). Design §12 records that none of F052's three files gives `UNSUPPORTED_SPEC_VERSION`.
- A test on a pre-0.1 file (`version:` and `name:`, no `spec_version`) asserts exactly `MISSING_FIELD` and `UNKNOWN_FIELD`, both at `(root)`.
- X31's fixture for a non-string key whose value is also broken reports only `INVALID_ID` at the map.
- Design §12 records the expected codes:
  - F091: only `UNKNOWN_DERIVED_FROM` for case 1; three `DERIVED_FROM_CYCLE` for case 2; `MISSING_DECISION` and `PARTIALLY_DRIVEN` for case 3 (as X34).
  - F107: only `COMMIT_NOT_FOUND` and `REMOTE_UNREACHABLE` (no `COMMIT_NOT_ANCESTOR`, `MISSING_DECLARED_ANALYSIS` or `UNRESOLVED_ADDRESS`).
  - An analysis from a commit with an `UNKNOWN_OPTION` inside: `DECLARED_ANALYSIS_INVALID` at the declaration and `UNKNOWN_OPTION` in the commit's file.
  - A selection key that names no decision: only `UNRESOLVED_ADDRESS`. An output whose `when` names an unknown option: only `UNKNOWN_OPTION`, with no `INPUT_NOT_BUILT` for its readers.
  - A planned result with no record: only `MISSING_RECORD`.

### X39. Change 4: the location selector defined fully (`conforms_to` as `dcterms:conformsTo`, PDF when absent, a pattern on value, quote refined by location)

- **Resolves:** F057, F080, F147 · **Partly:** F123, F146 · **Size:** M · **Depends on:** X16, X41 (X20 landed in ff7fc8b) · **Decision:** decided · **Done:** `a2505a1`

**The problem.** X20 made the schema and spec §5 cite RFC 8118 §3 and say "the cited work or output". The location is still defined only as a PDF fragment, and only in prose. `FragmentSelector.value` has no pattern, so any string is valid, `page 6` and `#page=6` included (F057). `location` is allowed on evidence citing an output of any format, where a PDF fragment means nothing, and nothing can say which syntax is meant (F080). Design §5 (now :295-297) says the selector has "no `conformsTo`", which is W3C's way of naming the syntax, and X20 added that Web Annotation names the PDF syntax by the RFC 3778 IRI. Tidy-up change 4 still says "for a PDF `value` is an RFC 3778 fragment" (X20 left that for this fix). Nothing says how a `quote` and a `location` given together relate. `main`'s "authoritative anchor"/"hint" wording was removed with no replacement (F147).

**The fix.**
- Tidy-up change 4 is amended. It already defines `value`, so the location is defined in one place and no new change is numbered.
  - Its title becomes "`FragmentSelector.page` is removed and `conforms_to` names the syntax".
  - In its second paragraph, the sentence "For a PDF `value` is an RFC 3778 fragment such as `page=6`, which can point at more than a page." is replaced by: "`value` is a fragment identifier of the cited work or output, without the `#`. An optional `conforms_to` is the IRI of the specification of the fragment syntax, as Web Annotation §4.2.1 gives it: `http://tools.ietf.org/rfc/rfc7111` for a CSV (`row=5-7`), `http://tools.ietf.org/rfc/rfc5147` for plain text (`char=0,10`), `http://www.w3.org/TR/media-frags/` for an image, audio or video (`xywh=50,50,640,480`, `t=10,20`), `http://tools.ietf.org/rfc/rfc3236` for HTML, `http://www.idpf.org/epub/linking/cfi/epub-cfi.html` for EPUB. Without `conforms_to`, `value` is a PDF fragment identifier as RFC 8118 §3 gives it (`page=6`, pages counted from 1), which can point at more than a page, so a location written today keeps its meaning. Web Annotation names that syntax `http://tools.ietf.org/rfc/rfc3778`, after the RFC that 8118 obsoletes. Writing that IRI means the same as writing none, and it is read by RFC 8118. PDF parameters are joined with `&`, never with `#` as RFC 3778 allowed, and a space or `"` in a value (as in `search=`) is percent-encoded. For a `doi`, the cited resource is the work's full text. `value` is read by the syntax `conforms_to` names. A tool that knows that syntax may parse it; one that doesn't shows it as written. Whether `value` follows the syntax, and whether the syntax fits the cited work or output, is not checked. `value` is non-empty and holds no ASCII space or control character, none of ``"#<>[\]^`{|}``, and `%` only before two hex digits. Any other character is allowed: `INVALID_VALUE`. `conforms_to` is an IRI (RFC 3987): `INVALID_VALUE`. With both a `quote` and a `location`, the quote is sought within the location: the `oa:FragmentSelector` is `oa:refinedBy` the `oa:TextQuoteSelector`."
  - Its last sentence adds `conforms_to` to the mappings: "(`value` onto `rdf:value` and `conforms_to` onto `dcterms:conformsTo`, as a quote's …)". The field is snake_case like every other ASTRA field. The W3C name comes through the mapping, as `rdf:value` does for `value`.
- The RFC's Compatibility "Removing" bullet adds: "Change 4's `conforms_to` is additive." The migration bullet "remove `page` from evidence's `location` …" goes on: "strip a leading `#` from `value` (`#page=6` becomes `page=6`), rewrite `#` between PDF parameters as `&`, and `page N` or `p. N` as `page=N`. A location on evidence citing something other than a PDF gains the `conforms_to` of its syntax. Leave any other value (`Table 2, column 6`) as written. It is then reported as `INVALID_VALUE`, and the author fixes it." Percent-encoding such a value would pass the pattern, but the result would mean nothing. The References gain RFC 8118 and the Web Annotation Data Model (§4.2.1, §4.2.9).
- `src/astra/schema/insight.yaml`:
  - Add the prefix `dcterms: http://purl.org/dc/terms/`.
  - `FragmentSelector.value` gets `pattern: '^([^\x00-\x20\x7f"#%<>\[\\\]^`{|}]|%[0-9A-Fa-f]{2})+$(?!\n)'` and `annotations: {rule: INVALID_VALUE}`.
  - A new attribute `FragmentSelector.conforms_to`: `slot_uri: dcterms:conformsTo`, `range: iri`. There is no linkml-lint exception and no `.linkmllint.yaml`, because the name is snake_case.
  - Descriptions:
    - `FragmentSelector`: "A place in the cited work or output, given as a fragment identifier in the syntax `conforms_to` names, such as `row=5-7` in a CSV (RFC 7111). Without `conforms_to`, it is a PDF fragment identifier (RFC 8118 §3), such as `page=6`, with pages counted from 1."
    - `value`: "The fragment identifier, without the leading `#`, such as `page=6`. It is non-empty and holds no ASCII space or control character, none of ``"#<>[\]^`{|}``, and `%` only before two hex digits."
    - `conforms_to`: "The IRI of the specification of the fragment syntax `value` follows, as W3C Web Annotation gives it, such as `http://tools.ietf.org/rfc/rfc7111` for a CSV. Without it, `value` is a PDF fragment identifier (RFC 8118)."
    - `Evidence.quote`: "The passage of the cited work or output the claim rests on. With a `location`, the passage is sought within that place."
    - `Evidence.location` keeps its current text.
- `src/astra/schema/types.yaml`: X16's IRI pattern is now used twice, so it moves to a new named type `iri` (`typeof: string`, the pattern, `annotations: {rule: INVALID_VALUE}`). `Environment.iri` in `provenance.yaml` takes `range: iri` and drops its own `pattern` and `rule`. If X16 has not landed, the type takes X16's pattern. CLAUDE.md's list of named types adds `iri`.
- This text replaces X20's text for `FragmentSelector`, `value`, `Evidence.quote` and change 4's citation, and the RFC 3778 note X20 added to design §5. X20's "the cited work or output" wording and its RFC 8118 citation stand.
- `docs/specification.md`:
  - §5's evidence paragraph (now :530-538) says the same as change 4 in prose. It replaces "whose `value` is a PDF fragment identifier … pages count from 1" and gives Web Annotation §4.2.1's IRIs as a short table (syntax, IRI, example).
  - §9's `INVALID_VALUE` row adds `FragmentSelector.value` (a fragment identifier's characters) and `FragmentSelector.conforms_to` (an IRI) after `Evidence.doi`. Its Source already has `tidy-up`.
  - §10's **RDF** paragraph reads `conforms_to` as `dcterms:conformsTo`, with the IRI it names as its object. A location without it reads as one with `http://tools.ietf.org/rfc/rfc3778`. With a quote, the location is the `oa:FragmentSelector` `oa:refinedBy` the quote's `oa:TextQuoteSelector`. X16 makes that paragraph's edits in one pass.
  - X41's table of what each class admits gains a `FragmentSelector.value` row, and the pattern's character class takes X41's wording.
- `design/2026-11-03-v0.1-schema-design.md`:
  - §4, Named types: add `iri` (X16's pattern; `Environment.iri`, `FragmentSelector.conforms_to`). Patterns used once: drop the `Environment.iri` row and add `FragmentSelector.value` (the characters of a fragment identifier, `%` only before two hex digits).
  - §5's FragmentSelector row (:292) becomes "`value` (required, `rdf:value`) and `conforms_to` (`dcterms:conformsTo`, an `iri`): a fragment in the syntax `conforms_to` names, a PDF fragment (RFC 8118, `page=6`) when it is absent".
  - :295-297 become "`conforms_to` is optional; without it the syntax is PDF's, so a location written before it keeps its meaning. Web Annotation names that syntax by the RFC 3778 IRI, and writing that IRI means the same as writing none. With both a `quote` and a `location`, the quote is sought within the location (`oa:refinedBy`)."
  - §10's tidy-up row 4 (:691) and §11 item 13 add `conforms_to` and `value`'s pattern.
  - §12's `""` entry (:801) drops `FragmentSelector.value`.
- Fixtures:
  - Invalid `Analysis-NNN-location-not-a-fragment.yaml`, shaped as Analysis-173: `location: {value: "page 6"}`.
  - Invalid `Analysis-NNN-location-conforms-to-not-an-iri.yaml`: `location: {value: "row=5-7", conforms_to: "RFC 7111"}`.
  - Both are numbered when the plan's fixtures are ordered.
  - Valid Analysis-006: the prior insight's location becomes `page=6&search=%22label%20noise%22`, with no `conforms_to`. It gains an output `scores` (`type: table`, `format: csv`), and `rf_accuracy` gains a second evidence item: `artifact: scores`, `location: {value: "row=5-7", conforms_to: "http://tools.ietf.org/rfc/rfc7111"}`.
- Run `just gen-schema` and commit `generated/`.
- `docs/llms.txt` still writes `location: { page: 6 }` (:90, :106). That belongs to its pre-0.1 rewrite, outside this fix, which writes `location: {value: page=6}`.

**Decided.** Option 1, changed: the location takes W3C's optional `dcterms:conformsTo`. The field is written `conforms_to`, with `slot_uri: dcterms:conformsTo`, and has no lint exception (Tom, 2026-11-04). `value` is read by the fragment syntax it names, and is a PDF fragment (RFC 8118) when it is absent. It is not read by the cited media type, and not for PDFs only. The schema keeps the character check (pattern option a, not RFC 3987 `ifragment`, RFC 8118's grammar or no pattern). The quote is refined by the location (not alternatives, not `main`'s authoritative quote).

**Why this route.** Change 4 defines `value` only "for a PDF", and no accepted RFC mentions selectors. A new field, a pattern that changes what is valid, and a reading tools are bound to all belong in the tidy-up RFC. They go in the change that already defines the location, which is on the list of changes that need RFC text (decision 15). The snake_case name keeps every ASTRA field to one naming rule. No RDF reading of `astra.yaml` is published, so the W3C term is carried by `slot_uri`, as it is for `value` and `exact`.

**Done when.**
- The not-a-fragment fixture has the header `# rule: INVALID_VALUE` / `# fails at: /prior_insights/p/evidence/0/location/value`. The not-an-IRI fixture has `# rule: INVALID_VALUE` / `# fails at: /prior_insights/p/evidence/0/location/conforms_to`. Each fails there only, and `rules.json` maps the failure to `INVALID_VALUE`.
- Analysis-006 validates.
- The generated `analysis.schema.json` has a `conforms_to` property on `FragmentSelector`, and a `pattern` on `value`.
- `test_every_pattern_means_the_same_in_python_and_ecma_262` and the pattern guard pass on both patterns, and `just check-ajv` passes.
- `grep -rn conformsTo src/astra/schema/*.yaml` finds only the `slot_uri`. There is no `.linkmllint.yaml`. `grep -n "no \`conformsTo\`"` finds nothing in the design. `3778` appears in change 4 only in the Web Annotation IRI sentence.
- `just test` is green, `linkml-lint` included.

### X40. Runner-owned places kept apart: CLASHING_RESULT_NAME and RESERVED_PATH

- **Resolves:** F037, F053 · **Size:** M · **Depends on:** X07, X56 · **Decision:** decided · **Done:** `ce72d9f`

**The problem.** §3 and §8 lay out where runners write, `results/` and `.astra/`, but no rule protects those places. An output and a declaration of one analysis can share an id, and a format can contain `.`, so the output `stage` in format `summary.json` and the driven result `stage.summary` in format `json` both land at `results/u1/stage.summary.json` (F037). An input's or a declaration's `path` can point into `results/` or `.astra/`, so a result is read outside the result graph and a run can overwrite an input or a declared `astra.yaml` (F053). §8 and the `UNCOMMITTED_CHANGES` row also dropped RFC 0005:149's "itself" (F053).

**The fix.**
- Tidy-up RFC: a new Tightening change, numbered when the plan's new changes are ordered, "Runner-owned places are kept apart", stating both rules below. The RFC's other edits: the Proposal's count ("twenty-nine changes") goes up; the Compatibility "Tightening" bullet lists the new change as one that can make a file invalid; the migration steps gain "rename an output that shares its id with a declaration", "move an input or a declared analysis out of `results/` and `.astra/`" and "read a result by its address".
- §9 validator rows, Source `tidy-up`:
  - `CLASHING_RESULT_NAME` | MUST | No output shares its id with a declaration of the same analysis.
  - `RESERVED_PATH` | MUST | No input's `path` and no declaration's `path`, with `.`, `..` and repeated `/` resolved, is or lies in the `results/` or `.astra/` of an analysis of the stack.
- §3, under the layout: "`results/` and `.astra/` belong to runners. No input's `path` and no declaration's `path` is, or lies in, them ([`RESERVED_PATH`])." §5 Inputs and §7 `path` link the code; §5 adds "A result is read by its address, never by a path."
- §4 ids, rewritten as two exceptions, each with its reason: "Ids of different kinds can be the same, with two exceptions. An input and an output never share an id, since `Output.inputs` takes both ([`AMBIGUOUS_ID`]). An output never shares its id with a declaration, since their results' default places could then be the same (§8, [`CLASHING_RESULT_NAME`])." §8 "Where records and bytes go" adds: "Two default places `{name}.{format}` are the same only when one name is a proper dotted prefix of the other, which needs an output and a declaration with one id in some analysis on the chain. Every analysis of the stack keeps `CLASHING_RESULT_NAME`, so no two results of one analysis share a default place."
- §8's runner paragraph and the `UNCOMMITTED_CHANGES` row restore "the results and records it writes itself". This only repairs drift from RFC 0005:149; X56 settles its reading when it amends RFC 0005 §3 and rewrites the same row, and its option (a) exempts records and results whichever run wrote them, so the two are done together. `RESERVED_PATH` protects inputs and declared analyses, not "itself". It also keeps X07's restored sentence (:975-977, the runner's commit makes nothing stale) true in F053's cases.
- Design §10, §11 and §12: the rows, the change, and F037's and F053's documents as validator conformance cases in §12's list. Run `just gen-schema`.

**Decision** (Tom, 2026-11-04: decided as recommended).
- F037's rule: (a) the id rule above (recommended); (b) "no two planned results share a default place", which needs the planned results X52 defers; (c) no `.` in `format`, which breaks `tar.gz`.
- Code: new codes (recommended). Widening `AMBIGUOUS_ID` misnames the rule, since no single field resolves to both; `INVALID_PATH` can't resolve `..` or see which directories are analyses.
- Level: MUST (recommended). Under a SHOULD a runner can't keep `UNRECORDED_RESULT`.
- Scope of `CLASHING_RESULT_NAME`: every declaration, driven or consumed, by `path` or by `url` (recommended), or only a driven one. Whether a declaration is driven depends on the root, so the narrower rule can't be judged from one file.
- Scope of `RESERVED_PATH`: all of `results/` and `.astra/` (recommended), or only `.astra/records/` and the places §8 builds. A per-place rule needs X52's planned results, and tools may keep other things under `.astra/`. Optionally also a `Recipe.container` that names a file (a Containerfile); recommend not, since a recipe's files are pinned by the commit and `command` paths stay unchecked anyway.
- An input naming an `astra.yaml`: allow it (recommended); no runner writes that file.

**Why this route.** Tidy-up change 14 makes only an input and an output distinct, and no source reserves `results/` or `.astra/`; both rules make valid files invalid, so they need a numbered change. Restoring "itself" is just do, from RFC 0005:149.

**Done when.** `rules.json` gains the two validator rows with Source `tidy-up`, and its `UNCOMMITTED_CHANGES` statement carries "itself"; `just test` passes. The new change is in the draft. Design §12 lists F037's and F053's documents as validator conformance cases.

### X41. Say exactly which characters excluded, url, doi and iri take, and record a url's no-space narrowing

- **Resolves:** F012, F161 · **Size:** M · **Depends on:** X17, X42, X16, X48 · **Decision:** decided · **Done:** `46bc1b5`

**The problem.** `excluded`, `url`, `doi` and `iri` use `[^\x00-\x20\x7f]`. Spec §4 (:252), §5 (:448, :502) and design §4 (:217) call it "a visible character". Design §4 also calls it "a non-space character" (:228) and writes it as `\S` (:229, :230, :235). It means "not an ASCII space or control". It admits every code point above U+007F, including non-ASCII spaces, format characters, U+2028/U+2029 and C1 controls. Design §12 records this only for `excluded` (F161). The class also keeps out of a `url` an ASCII space that git fetches, and no text says so (F012). X42 and X16 give `doi` and `iri` new classes, and those classes have edges too.

**The fix.**
- What each field's class admits above U+007F. This goes in spec §4 next to each class, and in design §4 and §11:

  | Field | Class | Admits |
  |---|---|---|
  | `excluded`, `url` | (A) `[^\x00-\x20\x7f]`; (B) `[^\x00-\x20\x7f-\x9f]` | non-ASCII spaces (NBSP, U+3000), format characters (ZWSP, BOM, U+202E), U+2028/U+2029; under (A) C1 controls too |
  | `doi`, X42(A) | `[^\x00-\x20\x7f-\x9f]` at the ends, `[^\x00-\x1f\x7f-\x9f]` inside | NBSP and U+3000 anywhere in the suffix (legal, since they are Graphic), plus ZWSP, BOM, U+202E and U+2028/U+2029 (not legal in a DOI) |
  | `iri`, X16(a) | `[^\x00-\x20\x7f-\x9f<>"{}\|\\^`%#]` | format characters, including the bidi controls that RFC 3987 §4.1 says an IRI MUST NOT contain |

  If X42 or X16 keeps the current pattern, that field gets the `excluded` row under (A).
- Spec §4 (:250-253) names each class with its label: "a character other than an ASCII space or control", the two classes that "also leave out C1 controls", and "any character but an ASCII control". Then it gives the table's Admits column. Spec §5 :448 uses the new label and gives the range. Design §4: :217 follows spec §4. :228 becomes "not blank: holds a character of the class". :229, :230 and :235 get the explicit class wherever X42, X16 or X17 leave them unchanged.
- Under (A), CLAUDE.md's convention list gains `[^\x00-\x20\x7f-\x9f]` and `[^\x00-\x1f\x7f-\x9f]`. Under (B), the first of these replaces `[^\x00-\x20\x7f]` and the second is added.
- Tidy-up change 25 gains: "A `url` holds no ASCII space or control character [(B): or C1 control]; any other character is allowed. A space is written `%20` in a `scheme://` URL, which git decodes. Git doesn't decode the scp-like form, so rewrite an scp-like remote `[user@]host:my dir/x` as `ssh://[user@]host/~/my%20dir/x`." The same advice goes in as a migration step. The `%20` sentence also goes in spec §7. X17's `url` description starts "Where git fetches another repository from, as a remote with no space or ASCII control character: …".
- Change 12, after X48's sentence. Under (A): "A reason made only of non-ASCII spaces, format characters or C1 controls still counts." Under (B): X48's range ends at U+009F, and the sentence ends at "format characters".
- X42's change 31 gains: "The suffix may also hold format characters and U+2028/U+2029, which ISO 26324 excludes and no rule checks."
- Design §11: the `url` item adds "no ASCII space (`%20`)". A new item lists `excluded`, `url` and `doi` with their classes. Design §12 replaces its `excluded` clause with the one edge that no RFC records: format characters in `iri`.
- Fixtures: invalid `Analysis-NNN-url-with-space` (`file:///home/me/My Repos/upstream`, INVALID_VALUE, `/analyses/up/url`). `Analysis-005` gains `file:///home/me/My%20Repos/upstream`, `ssh://git@nas/~/shares/My%20Repos/upstream` and `https://example.org/café/repo`. Under (B), `just gen-schema` and two more invalid fixtures: `url-c1-control` (`"https://example.org/r\u0085"`) and `excluded-c1-only` (`"\u0085"`, BLANK_EXCLUDED, `/decisions/d/options/a/excluded`).

**Decision** (Tom, 2026-11-04: decided as recommended). (1) After X42(A) and X16(a), which already exclude C1:
- (A) Leave `excluded` and `url` as they are and record what they admit (recommended for v0.1). The contract keeps two "not a space or control" classes, and CLAUDE.md lists both.
- (B) Give `excluded` and `url` the C1-excluding class as well. One such class is left. This narrows what is valid further and needs regeneration and fixtures.

(2) Spaces in a `url`:
- Record the narrowing and advise `%20` (recommended). Every remote has that spelling.
- Or allow spaces. The pattern then admits typos with trailing spaces.

**Why this route.** RFC 0007's `url` is "where git can fetch another repository from". Change 25 allows "one of git's two remote forms" and change 12 says the reason "can't be empty", and neither says which characters count. Rejecting a space that git fetches is therefore a narrowing that no source records, and design §11 doesn't list it.

**Done when.** The space fixture fails at `/analyses/up/url`, and the `%20` and `café` URLs pass. Grepping for `visible character`, `non-space` and `\\[sS]` in spec and design §4 finds only the paragraph that forbids the shorthands. `just test` is green.

### X42. Record lowercase hashes, a three-part spec_version and an ISO 26324 DOI pattern in the tidy-up

- **Resolves:** F022, F023 · **Partly:** F082, F083 · **Size:** M · **Decision:** decided · **Done:** `71b55ec`

**The problem.** Design §4's type and pattern tables make three narrowings that never reached its §11 list. So the tidy-up RFC records none of them and gives no migration step. The three are lowercase content hashes (RFC 0005 doesn't set the case) and a three-part `spec_version`, for an analysis and a record (`main` took `1.0`) (F022). The third is `Evidence.doi`'s pattern (F023), which has no source. It is narrower than ISO 26324 and rejects registered DOIs: every ISBN-A (`10.978.…`) and the DOIs with a space in the suffix.

**The fix.**
- Tidy-up RFC, Tightening. Change 26 becomes "Commit ids and hashes are lowercase". It adds that a `content_hash` is `sha256:` followed by 64 lowercase hex digits, as Git LFS pointers write it. A new change, "`spec_version` has three parts", says that an analysis's and a record's `spec_version` (changes 9 and 10) is `MAJOR.MINOR.PATCH`, with no leading zeros. A second new change, "A `doi` is a DOI name", gives the pattern chosen in the Decision and its reason. The final change numbers are assigned once, across all tidy-up RFC fixes, when the RFC is edited.
- Compatibility: add both new changes to the Tightening line's list of changes that can make a file invalid. Also say that "`spec_version` has three parts", like change 26's hash clause, changes RFC-0005's records. Add three migration steps: "write content hashes in lowercase"; "write `spec_version` with three parts, such as `0.1.0`"; "give a `doi` a non-empty suffix with no control character and no space at either end". Add ISO 26324 (the DOI Handbook) to the References.
- `insight.yaml` `Evidence.doi`: write the chosen pattern on it. Under (A), the description becomes "The DOI name of the cited work, under the `10` directory: `10.`, the registrant code, `/` and the suffix, such as `10.48550/arXiv.1706.03762`." Run `just gen-schema`.
- Spec §6 (l.501-503) gives the same parenthetical: "`10.`, digit runs joined by `.`, `/`, then a suffix with no control character and no space at either end".
- Under (A), name the new classes `[^\x00-\x20\x7f-\x9f]` and `[^\x00-\x1f\x7f-\x9f]` in spec §4's Patterns paragraph (l.251-253) and in CLAUDE.md's pattern-convention bullet. X41 rewrites the same §4 sentence, so make the two edits together.
- Spec §9 `INVALID_VALUE` Source: add `tidy-up` and `main`. X24 writes this cell.
- Design §4 l.229 gets the new pattern. In §11, item 14 covers content hashes too, and new items cover `spec_version` and `doi`. In §10, l.601's hash case moves to rows citing the tidy-up changes by title. Coordinate with X28.
- Fixtures:
  - Rename Analysis-166 to `doi-directory-not-10`.
  - Add invalid fixtures `ProvenanceRecord-NNN-content-hash-uppercase`, `Analysis-NNN-doi-control-character` (`"10.1234/a\tb"`) and `Analysis-NNN-doi-trailing-space`, each taking the next free number.
  - Add `10.978.8889637/173` and `"10.3929/ethz-a- 007549606"` to `tests/data/valid/Analysis-006-insights.yaml`.

**Decision** (Tom, 2026-11-04: decided as recommended). The DOI language:
- **(A) The `10` directory with a registrant code:** `^10\.[0-9]+(\.[0-9]+)*/[^\x00-\x20\x7f-\x9f]([^\x00-\x1f\x7f-\x9f]*[^\x00-\x20\x7f-\x9f])?$(?!\n)`. It accepts ISBN-As and inner spaces. It rejects control characters (C0, DEL, C1), an empty suffix, and a space at either end. It also rejects two forms that ISO 26324:2022 allows: a shortDOI (`10/aabbe`, which doi.org resolves) and any other directory indicator.
- **(A′) As (A), with an optional registrant code:** prefix `^10(\.[0-9]+(\.[0-9]+)*)?/`. This also accepts shortDOIs.
- **(B) Keep the current pattern and record it:** a work with an ISBN-A, or a DOI with a space, can't be cited by `doi`.
- **(C) ISO 26324:2022 in full:** this also accepts `11.1/x`, which no resolver serves.

Recommend (A), with this reason written into the RFC change. A shortDOI is an alias for a full DOI, so it is the full DOI that gets cited. Accepting both forms would let one work be cited under two names, and tools compare DOIs as strings. X41 then states what (A)'s classes still admit.

**Why this route.** No accepted RFC and no tidy-up change sets the case of a hash, the form of `spec_version` or DOI syntax. Change 26 covers only commit ids, changes 9 and 10 cover only the rename, and RFC 0005 gives the record's version only the example `0.1.0`. Each narrowing can make a file or a record invalid. Design §11 is the list of departures that the RFC must record.

**Done when.** The tidy-up says all of this in "Commit ids and hashes are lowercase", "`spec_version` has three parts" and "A `doi` is a DOI name", in its Tightening line and in its migration steps. `just test` passes with the regenerated contract, and the new fixtures fail or pass as named.

### X43. Say which universes of the stack a validator checks

- **Resolves:** F047 · **Size:** M · **Depends on:** X06, X34 · **Decision:** decided · **Done:** `69982d6`, `bf0c5dc`

**The problem.** A validator reads every `astra.yaml` of the stack, and §9 states the universe rules for "every universe". Nothing says whether another analysis's own universes are checked, or by which rules (F047). §7 says a driven analysis's universes "aren't used", and RFC 0004:149 that they "are considered to not exist". Only an analysis from a commit is settled, by `DECLARED_ANALYSIS_INVALID`. So F047's stacks `a` and `b` pass one validator and fail another.

**The fix.** Written for Option C, the recommendation.
- §7 Driving, after X06's new last bullet ("… Each universe being built has one driver, the analysis whose universe it is."), a paragraph: "A validator still checks every universe of every analysis of the stack. Each universe's selections are held to §6 and to driving (`MISSING_DECISION`, `UNKNOWN_OPTION`, `INACTIVE_DECISION`, `MISSING_REQUIRED_OPTION`, `INCOMPATIBLE_OPTIONS`, `EXCLUDED_OPTION_SELECTED`, `UNKNOWN_DERIVED_FROM`, `DERIVED_FROM_CYCLE`, `PARTIALLY_DRIVEN`, `CONFLICTING_SELECTIONS`). The declarations it drives are those its effective selection names (X34), a declaration back to the root included: nothing is built there, so the root stays undriven in the universes built. The rules on the universe being built (`NOT_BUILT_IN_UNIVERSE`, `UNDRIVEN_DECLARATION`, `INPUT_NOT_BUILT`) hold only in the universes built. The root and its repository never change, so each declaration is judged once, against the root's repository (`DECLARATION_OUTSIDE_REPOSITORY`)."
- §7 The analysis stack: X06's "The universes being built are the root's" becomes "The universes built are the root's and those a pin names. A validator also checks every other universe of the stack (below)."
- §7 Analyses from a commit, **Validity**: "An analysis declared without a `commit` breaks the rules under their own codes. One from a commit breaks `DECLARED_ANALYSIS_INVALID`."
- §2, validator role: "… reports every parse, schema and validator rule that the analysis, or any analysis of its stack not from a commit, breaks. An analysis from a commit that breaks one breaks `DECLARED_ANALYSIS_INVALID`." This keeps §7's remembered answer (l.794-798).
- §9 validator subsection, before the table: "Every universe of every analysis of the stack is checked as §7 says." No new code. `just gen-schema`.
- Tidy-up RFC: a new change under Tightening, "A validator checks every universe of the stack". The plan numbers new changes in one pass. Its example is F047's stack `a` (`MISSING_DECISION`) and stack `b` (`UNKNOWN_OPTION`).
- Design: the RFC 0004 row "Driven analyses' own universes aren't used; one driver per universe" (l.578) cites the new change. §12's conformance-suite bullet lists stacks `a` and `b` with their verdicts. X28's sync includes the change.

**Decision** (Tom, 2026-11-04: decided as recommended).
- (A) Check every universe by every universe rule. Each one is pushed through what its analysis drives, the root staying the same. A back-edge to the root counts as driven when that universe names it. Consequence: any stage with universes of its own must drive every analysis it consumes a decision from, or reads an unpinned per-universe output of. Otherwise `NOT_BUILT_IN_UNIVERSE`, `UNDRIVEN_DECLARATION` or `INPUT_NOT_BUILT` makes the whole stack invalid. In `examples/iris_pipeline`, `feature_extraction` reads `iris.random_seed` and `classification` reads `iris.split`, so neither stage could keep universes of its own.
- (B) Check only the universes built: the root's, and every universe a pin names, whether the pin is in the analysis's own file or goes through an undriven declaration. Consequence: `a` and `b` stay valid, and a stale stage universe goes unnoticed until the stage is validated as the root.
- (C) Check the selection rules in every universe, and the universe-being-built rules only in the universes built. It catches `a` and `b` and leaves stages free to keep universes. This fits RFC 0004:63 and :245 (spec l.639-641, l.708-709), which allow stages that can be validated only from the root.

Recommended: C.

**Why this route.** RFC 0004:149 leaves the question open, and none of the draft's 29 changes or the design's D1-D13 settles it. Each option gives `a` and `b` a different verdict, so the answer needs a numbered change.

**Done when.** The new change's example gives `a` `MISSING_DECISION` and `b` `UNKNOWN_OPTION`, and design §12 lists both. §2, §7 and §9 carry the sentences, and `just test` passes with `rules.json` regenerated.

### X44. No optional list of strings is empty (change 24 widened)

- **Resolves:** F006 · **Size:** S · **Depends on:** X28 · **Decision:** decided · **Done:** `87d4e20`

**The problem.** §6 decides whether an output is built per universe by whether it "lists `decisions`" or "has a `when`", and §5 makes an element with a `when` conditional. The schema accepts `decisions: []` and `when: []`, a third way of writing "none" that the text gives no meaning. `when: []` "has a `when`", yet all of its zero conditions hold. The two readings put the record in different places and give different pin warnings, and JavaScript's `Boolean([])` and Python's `bool([])` disagree (F006).

**The fix.**
- `analysis.yaml` and `insight.yaml`: `minimum_cardinality: 1` on every optional list of strings in `astra.yaml`, the lists change 24 names: `Output.inputs`, `Output.decisions`, `Output.when`, `Decision.when`, `Option.requires`, `Option.incompatible_with`, `Option.insights`, and `tags` on `Analysis`, `Input`, `Output`, `Decision`, `Option`, `Universe` and `Insight`. `just gen-schema` gives each `minItems: 1`, coded `EMPTY_COLLECTION` by default.
- Spec §4, after "so an output lists each of its inputs, decisions and conditions once": "No list of strings is empty: a field with nothing to list is left out ([`EMPTY_COLLECTION`](#parse-and-schema)), so an output lists `decisions` or has a `when` only when it names at least one."
- Spec §9, the `EMPTY_COLLECTION` row: "`outputs`, `Decision.options` and `Insight.evidence` each have at least one entry, and no list of strings in an `astra.yaml` is empty". Add the tidy-up RFC to its Source cell.
- Tidy-up RFC, change 24 becomes "No list of strings repeats an entry or is empty". Its rationale adds: "An empty list is a second way of leaving a field out, and for `decisions` and `when` it has no defined meaning." Its rule sentence: "The schema rejects a repeated entry, and an empty list, in `Output.inputs`, `Output.decisions`, `when`, `requires`, `incompatible_with`, `insights` and `tags`." The migration step becomes "remove repeated entries and empty lists of strings". The compatibility line already counts change 24.
- Design, after or inside X28's rewrite of §11: add item 18 to §11's list ("No list of strings is empty (D12)") and update its closing paragraph ("All seventeen are in the tidy-up RFC draft as its changes …") to eighteen, now including change 24. Update both copies of the `EMPTY_COLLECTION` row: §7's catalogue at line 353, and §10's row at line 670, moved from "From `main` and from this design" to "Tidy-up (draft) and pending items" as change 24.
- New invalid fixtures `Analysis-<next>-output-decisions-empty.yaml`, `…-output-when-empty.yaml` and `…-decision-when-empty.yaml`, each `# rule: EMPTY_COLLECTION`. X21 covers the other lists.

**Decision** (Tom, 2026-11-04: decided as recommended). (A) Only `Output.decisions`, `Output.when` and `Decision.when`, the lists whose presence the spec tests: smallest, but the other lists keep two ways of writing "none". (B) Every optional list of strings, as above. (C) B, plus every optional map (`inputs: {}`, `universes: {}`, `Universe.decisions: {}`) and a record's `inputs: []`; more files become invalid for no defined gain. (D) Keep empty lists valid and define them by content: §6 says "lists at least one decision, has at least one condition in `when`", and §5 says "an element whose `when` is absent or empty is always active and not conditional". D invalidates no file, but keeps two ways of writing "none", and astra-tools and astra-typescript must each override their language's truthiness. Recommend B: D12 and the RFC's principle "one thing is written one way" settle such cases by making absence the only form, and B is one rule over the set change 24 already names. Records are outside F006, which concerns `astra.yaml` lists whose presence the spec tests; a record's `inputs` is a list of objects, left to X03 unless C is chosen.

**Why this route.** Every option settles an open question that binds where runners and checkers put a record; A to C also make files invalid that the sources leave valid. Change 24 rejects repeats but not empty lists, and change 23 rejects only `null`, so the fix needs a numbered change in the tidy-up RFC.

**Done when.** `just test` passes with the three new fixtures, each failing only at its list with `EMPTY_COLLECTION`. Every list of strings in `analysis.schema.json` has `minItems: 1`, and F006's evidence document fails in jsonschema and Ajv.

### X45. Getting a commit from a remote, a submodule's included, is checked each time, not remembered

- **Resolves:** F028 · **Partly:** F107 · **Size:** M · **Depends on:** X38, X33 · **Decision:** decided · **Done:** `eda8cd7`

*Updated 2026-11-04 (pending item 10): a submodule's commit, reached through a gitlink at a commit, is got like a declaration's own remote. Failing to get it is `REMOTE_UNREACHABLE`, checked each time. That part is one tidy-up change shared with X33. Under item 15 (proposed, to confirm when writing), the rest is spec text plus a design §11 line, and no longer a numbered change amending RFC 0007 §6.*

**The problem.** RFC 0007 §6 (0007:138) and spec §7 "Validity" (now :846-855) say the validity of an analysis from a commit can't change, so a validator may remember it. But that validity includes getting commits from remotes as they are now. It covers `REMOTE_UNREACHABLE` for every `url` the analysis reaches, and the commit of a `path` + `commit` declared inside an analysis read from a remote (§7 :816-817). A remote can lose a commit to a force-push and gc, since `COMMIT_NOT_ANCESTOR` is only a SHOULD. So a validator that remembers its answer and one that checks again disagree about the same root. Nothing says what a validator working offline does (F028). X33 now follows gitlinks at a commit, so a submodule's pinned commit is got the same way, from the URL the submodule records. The spec doesn't say what failing to get it reports. It is also open which other codes an unreachable remote brings with it (F107).

**The fix.**
- Spec §7 "Analyses from a commit", **Validity** (:846-855). Replace "A commit's files never change, so neither does the answer, and a validator can remember it for a commit it has validated." and the clause "but a remote that can't be fetched, or doesn't have the commit, is an error in the declaring analysis ([`REMOTE_UNREACHABLE`])" with:
  - "Getting a commit from a remote is checked apart from that ([`REMOTE_UNREACHABLE`]). A commit is got from a remote when it is a declaration's `url` and `commit`, a `path` and `commit` declared in a file read from a remote, or the commit a gitlink pins, crossed at a commit (§3). The remote is the `url`, the remote the file was read from, or the submodule's URL (§3)."
  - "Files at a commit never change, but remotes do. So getting a commit is checked each time, and a commit kept from an earlier fetch counts as got. The rest of an analysis from a commit's validity can't change, and a validator can remember it for a commit it has validated, with the schemas it has."
  - "A commit that can't be got is an error on one declaration in a file outside every analysis from a commit: that of the outermost analysis from a commit on the way to it. It is reported once for each such declaration." Example in the text: root -`path`-> `sub` -`url`-> `upstream` -`url`-> `nested`. If `nested`'s commit can't be got, the error is on `sub`'s `upstream`. Two declarations of one commit (RFC 0007 §7's `upstream` and `upstream_driven`) get one report each.
  - The SHOULD sentence on publishing only fetchable remotes stays as it is.
  - X38 and X43 edit the same paragraph. X38 adds the double report (`DECLARED_ANALYSIS_INVALID` on the declaration, the inner code in the commit's file). X43 adds "An analysis declared without a `commit` breaks the rules under their own codes." Land all three in one pass.
- Spec §7 **What is read** (:826-845), after "can work offline from what they kept": "A commit kept from an earlier fetch counts as got ([`REMOTE_UNREACHABLE`])." X33 adds the submodule's repository and `.gitmodules` to the same list.
- Spec §9 validator rows (:1215, :1217, :1218), edited in the same pass as X11 and X33:
  - `REMOTE_UNREACHABLE`: "Every commit the stack gets from a remote, through a `url`, a file read from a remote or a gitlink crossed at a commit, can be fetched from that remote or was kept from an earlier fetch". Source `0007 §6, tidy-up, §7`.
  - `COMMIT_NOT_FOUND`: X33's text with this fix's scope: "A declaration with a `path` and a `commit`, in a file not read from a remote, names a commit the declaring analysis's repository has". Source `0007, §7`. A `path` + `commit` in a file read from a remote is `REMOTE_UNREACHABLE` instead.
  - `DECLARED_ANALYSIS_INVALID`: X38's text, unchanged here: "An analysis from a commit whose `astra.yaml` can be read, in a version the validator has the schema for, is valid at that commit with everything it declares, `REMOTE_UNREACHABLE` aside". Source `0007, §7`.
- What goes unchecked. This is an entry of X38's closed list in §9's "What is checked" (pending item 5). After a `REMOTE_UNREACHABLE` on an outer declaration, `DECLARED_ANALYSIS_INVALID` on that declaration isn't checked, nor is the unreached analysis with every address into it. The outer analysis it read stays determined, so its `MISSING_DECLARED_ANALYSIS` and addresses into it (`upstream.summary#u1`) are still checked.
- Tidy-up RFC: one change shared with X33, cited by title until the new changes are numbered together: "A submodule's commit is got like a remote's". X33 gives where the submodule's URL comes from: `.gitmodules` at the commit read, a relative URL resolved against the declaration's `url` (pending item 10). This fix gives the rest, in one paragraph: "A commit a gitlink pins, crossed at a commit, is got from that URL. One that can't be got is an error in the declaring analysis, as a remote that can't be fetched is (RFC 0007 §6), and it is checked each time." No change amends RFC 0007 §6's "That can't change" (item 15).
- Design (`design/2026-11-03-v0.1-schema-design.md`):
  - §7 rows (:412, :414, :415) and §10 rows (:672, :679, :680) take the §9 texts.
  - §11 gets one line: "Getting a commit from a remote is checked each time, a kept copy counts as got, and a commit that can't be got is reported on the outermost declaration in a file outside every analysis from a commit. RFC 0007 §6 says the answer can't change." X33 adds the shared change's line.
  - §12's conformance-suite item gains four cases. (1) `nested`'s remote gone, a copy kept: valid. (2) `nested`'s remote gone, nothing kept, answer remembered: one `REMOTE_UNREACHABLE` on `sub`'s `upstream`, and nothing else. (3) A `path` + `commit` inside `upstream` whose commit the remote lost: the same. (4) The root declares `old: {path: lib, commit: C}`, and at `C` `lib` is a gitlink whose submodule URL is gone: with nothing in `.git/modules`, one `REMOTE_UNREACHABLE` on `old`; with the submodule kept there, valid.
- Run `just gen-schema` and commit `rules.json`.

**Decided** (Tom, 2026-11-04: as recommended, plus item 10).
1. Where getting a remote's commit counts.
   - (a) Leave it out of the validity of an analysis from a commit, and report it on the outer declaration. Recommended and chosen. The answer for each commit stays fixed and can be remembered, and one precise code lands on a line the author can edit.
   - (b) Keep it in that validity and drop "can't change". The answer can't be remembered, and the reports are `REMOTE_UNREACHABLE` inside the commit plus `DECLARED_ANALYSIS_INVALID` at the root.
   - In both, with nothing kept, the root is invalid, and its author can only re-pin or drop `upstream`.
2. Whether a kept copy counts as fetched.
   - (a) Yes, as RFC 0007 §2 says ("work offline from what they kept"). Recommended and chosen. A submodule's repository under `.git/modules` is such a copy.
   - (b) No. Offline validation stops working.
   - Under (a), `REMOTE_UNREACHABLE` is the one rule whose answer depends on the validator's environment: the network, and what the validator kept. Two conforming validators can still disagree on a root. That is what remains of F028's split.
3. Submodules (item 10). A submodule's pinned commit, reached through a gitlink at a commit, is got like a declaration's own remote: checked each time, never remembered, a kept copy counting. Failing to get it is `REMOTE_UNREACHABLE`, reported on the same outer declaration. Where its URL comes from is X33's.

**Why this route.** RFC 0007:138 says "That can't change", and the fix narrows it, so it changes which codes validators report and where. Under item 15 that narrowing is written in the spec and recorded in design §11, without amending RFC 0007 §6. The submodule half is new: no RFC says a gitlink is crossed at a commit or what failing to fetch its commit reports. Item 10 puts it in the tidy-up RFC, with X33's reading of gitlinks.

**Done when.** Spec §7 "Validity" and "What is read" read as above, together with X38's and X43's sentences. The three §9 rows read as above, and the closed "What is checked" list has the unreached-remote entry. The shared change is in the tidy-up draft with X33's. Design §7, §10 and §11 carry the texts, and §12 lists the four cases. `just test` passes with `rules.json` regenerated.

### X46. Stand-ins are the tooling's: the specification names none, and §5, §7, §8 and §9 say so one way

- **Resolves:** F051 · **Partly:** F154 · **Size:** S · **Depends on:** X08 · **Decision:** decided · **Route:** just do (changed from tidy-up RFC, Tom 2026-11-04) · **Done:** `9b06dcb`

*Resolves F051. Partly F154: X46 has a checker that can't tell give `absent`, and X08 owns `modified`'s SHA-256 and size test. Related: F032 (resolved by X08) and F071 (resolved by X54).*

**The problem.** RFC 0006:55 and RFC 0005:41-46 give stand-ins only as examples ("a git-annex link or a Git LFS pointer at the path, or a DVC file beside it"). The specification copies the examples and never says whether the list is closed (F051). So §5's `MISSING_INPUT_FILE`, §7's "got in the same way … there" and §8's `absent`/`modified` each read the list their own way. §7's "there" leaves out a DVC file beside the path, which §5 accepts. "git-annex link" names only the symlink, not an unlocked file's pointer, so a checker that knows only the link reports `modified` where `absent` is right (F051, F154).

**The fix.** The specification names no stand-in. Which stand-ins a tool recognises, and where it looks for them, is the tool's. How a file's bytes are stored and got is the runner's, and every record names its runner. §5 says this once, and §7, §8 and §9 link to it. git-annex, Git LFS and DVC leave the specification. RFC 0005 and 0006 keep them as their examples.
- §5 Inputs, third bullet (:356-360). With X08's last sentence and X33's "tracked in git", it reads: "The path names the file itself, or something the tooling recognises as standing in for it ([`MISSING_INPUT_FILE`](#validator)). The file, or what stands in for it, is tracked in git ([`INPUT_NOT_TRACKED`](#validator)). Which stand-ins a tool recognises, and where it looks for them, is the tool's: the specification names none, and two tools may judge the same input differently. How a file's bytes are stored and got is the runner's, and every record names its runner (§8). Something standing in for the content isn't the content."
- §7 'Results' of an analysis from a commit (:823-827): "When a recipe reads the result, the runner gets its bytes from what the declared commit holds at the result's default place, or through something there it recognises as standing in for them (§5), and they are checked against the record. An input of the declared analysis read by address is got from its `path` at the declared commit, or through what the runner recognises as standing in for it (§5)." The input no longer has to be "there", so a stand-in beside the path is the runner's to find.
- §8 'Where records and bytes go' (:887-892): "git-annex or Git LFS, for example, keep them in a store and leave a link or a pointer standing in for them" becomes "a runner may keep them elsewhere and leave something standing in for them (§5)". X08 turns the next sentence into the explanation that links to `MODIFIED_RESULT`.
- §8 Statuses (:1011-1014), over X08's wording: `modified` means `MODIFIED_RESULT` doesn't hold. A checker reports it only where it can tell: a stand-in it recognises names a SHA-256 hash or a size other than the record's, or a file it can tell is no stand-in doesn't match. A checker that can't tell gives `absent`. X08's example "(an MD5E git-annex key of the right size, say)" goes.
- §9 validator table:
  - `MISSING_INPUT_FILE`: "An input's `path` names a file, or something the validator recognises as standing in for one (§5)". X33 then adds "(not a symlink, directory or gitlink)" after "a file".
  - `INPUT_NOT_TRACKED`: unchanged now. X33 owns its final text, and "stand-in" there links to §5.
- §9 check table: `ABSENT_RESULT` keeps its rule, with X08's status `absent`. The Statuses table's `modified` row (:1173) is X58's "`MODIFIED_RESULT` doesn't hold" followed by the test above, ending "otherwise `absent`". The `absent` row (:1172) is unchanged.
- Design §7 rows (:380, :423) follow the §9 rows. Design §10 RFC 0006 row (:619) becomes "A stand-in may be committed in its place; which ones count is the tool's | spec 5; `MISSING_INPUT_FILE`". Design §12's conformance item: cases with a stand-in are tool cases, not specification cases.
- Run `just gen-schema` and commit `rules.json`. Only `MISSING_INPUT_FILE`'s statement changes.
- Other fixes this changes:
  - X33: decision 4(a) becomes "a symlink is accepted only where the validator recognises it as a stand-in". Any other symlink still fails `MISSING_INPUT_FILE`. Its `MISSING_INPUT_FILE` row reads "An input's `path` names a file (not a symlink, directory or gitlink), or something the validator recognises as standing in for one (§5)". Its "Depends on" drops X46, and X46 leaves its list of new tidy-up changes.
  - X54: "a stand-in on X46's list" and "where X46's list puts the stand-in, such as `<path>.dvc`" become "a stand-in the checker recognises". Where it recognises none, the input is `unknown`. Two checkers can then give `unchanged` and `unknown` for one input, which this decision accepts.
  - X08 lands in the same pass and keeps "Something standing in for the content isn't the content". Only its §8 Statuses example goes, and "plainly isn't a stand-in" becomes "a file it can tell is no stand-in".
  - X58: no conflict. Its sentence "Only tooling that recognises stand-ins can check `MODIFIED_RESULT`, so a checker MAY leave it out" links "stand-ins" to §5.
- For astra-tools (design §13): D10 stands. astra-tools recognises git-annex, Git LFS and DVC as its own choice. Its stand-in cases move from `spec/` to `tools/`: `MISSING_INPUT_FILE`'s passing cases (an LFS pointer, a broken annex symlink, a DVC file beside the path), `INPUT_NOT_TRACKED`'s tracked stand-in, and `ABSENT_RESULT`'s LFS or annex stand-in with the recorded hash or another one. `spec/` keeps the cases with no stand-in: a missing path, a directory, an untracked or ignored file, no bytes → `absent`, and a plain file with other bytes → `absent` or `modified`.

**Decided.** The specification says nothing about which stand-ins exist. A validator or checker may recognise a stand-in if it wants, and how bytes are stored and got is the runner's, which the record names. There is no closed list, and two tools may differ. Options (A)-(C) and (a)/(b) on which forms count, and how a `<path>.dvc` counts, fall away.

**Why this route.** RFC 0006:55 says a stand-in is something "that tooling recognises", gives the forms as examples, and leaves "how its bytes are kept … up to the tooling". RFC 0005:46 and RFC 0007:132 leave `modified` and getting the bytes to "the tooling that recognises" a stand-in. Saying plainly that the list is open, and the tool's, adds no rule and makes nothing valid or invalid beyond what the RFCs say. So it needs no numbered change. A checker that can't tell gives `absent`, as RFC 0005:244-246 already implies: there, only tooling that recognises a stand-in finds `modified`.

**Done when.**
- `docs/specification.md` names no git-annex, Git LFS or DVC.
- §5 holds the stand-in paragraph, and §7, §8 and the `MISSING_INPUT_FILE` row link to it.
- `rules.json` has the new `MISSING_INPUT_FILE` statement and no other diff beyond X08's.
- `just test` passes, drift check included.
- X33, X54 and design §10 and §12 read as above.

### X47. Tidy-up records what the record schema checks in an environment IRI

- **Resolves:** none · **Partly:** F061 · **Size:** S · **Depends on:** X16, X28, X33 · **Decision:** decided · **Done:** `64bc2b8`

*Updated 2026-11-05 (Tom): X47 stays specification only, as decision 15 and "For the tidy-up RFC" have it, and the draft gets nothing. The first two bullets of the fix (the new change and the Tightening sentence) are withdrawn, and so is the draft's half of Done when.*

**The problem.** RFC 0005 lists "an IRI for the environment" among what the record schema checks (l.142). Under X16's pattern (i), the schema still accepts strings that are not RFC 3987 IRIs, such as `urn:x:a[b`, `http://h:x:y/`, and private-use characters and noncharacters anywhere. It also rejects some IRIs: `x:` and `x:#y` have an empty part after the scheme. `INVALID_RECORD` ("valid against the record schema") follows the pattern, not the RFC (F061). X16 records the gap only in design §13, which ranks below the RFC, so the departure is implemented but nothing binding records it.

**The fix.**
- *Withdrawn (2026-11-05).* Tidy-up RFC: a new change, "A record's environment is an IRI", appended under Tightening after change 29. It is numbered in X33's joint pass for the new changes, so X47 joins X33's list. Its text: "RFC-0005 says the record schema checks an IRI for the environment. The schema rejects a space, C0 and C1 controls, `<>"{}|\^`, the backtick, the bidi formatting characters (U+200E, U+200F, U+202A-U+202E), a `%` not followed by two hex digits, a second `#`, and an empty part after the scheme. It admits every other character, noncharacters and private use included. It doesn't check authority, port, IP literals, `[` or `]` outside an IP literal, or where private-use characters may stand. A record whose `iri` matches the pattern is valid. A runner writes an RFC 3987 IRI."
- *Withdrawn (2026-11-05).* The same RFC, Implementation implications, Tightening line: add "The new change settles how far a checker's `INVALID_RECORD` reads RFC-0005's 'an IRI for the environment': a record whose `iri` matches the pattern is valid. It relaxes RFC-0005's check and invalidates no file. No migration step is needed, because no tool writes records yet."
- Spec §8 `environment` bullet (l.910): after X16's "an IRI (RFC 3987)", add "The record schema checks its scheme, characters and percent-encoding, not its structure." §9 `UNRECREATABLE_ENVIRONMENT` (l.1197), and its copy in design §7 (l.447): "as an RFC 3987 IRI that it can recreate at its version". Run `just gen-schema` and commit `rules.json`.
- Design §4 row (l.230): "an IRI (RFC 3987)", with X16's pattern in place of `\S+`. Design §10's tidy-up table: a row for the new change, landing in `Environment.iri`, `INVALID_VALUE` and `UNRECREATABLE_ENVIRONMENT`. Design §11: an item for this narrowing, numbered with X41's, X42's and X44's items in X28's rewrite. Design §13: drop X16's structural-gap item.

**Decision** (Tom, 2026-11-04: decided as recommended). (a) Record the narrowing as above, together with X16's pattern (i) and context (a). Under that context every `iri` is read as written, never as a CURIE. The pattern is the whole rule, so every checker gives the same verdict. (b) Checkers also parse the `iri` and report `INVALID_RECORD`. That binds every checker to an RFC 3987 parser, and verdicts then depend on the library. (c) Take X16's pattern (ii), the full grammar. This fix then drops out. Recommended: (a). Runners write records and the runner row already binds them, and a verdict that doesn't depend on the library is worth more than the leftover grammar cases.

**Why this route.** RFC 0005 l.142 says the schema checks an IRI, and a pattern that accepts some non-IRIs and rejects some IRIs departs from that. Design §11 lists departures like this one, and they belong in the tidy-up RFC. Neither change 22 nor change 28 says anything about `iri`.

**Done when.** The draft carries the change and the Tightening sentence. Spec §8 and §9 and design §4, §7, §10, §11 and §13 match the change. `rules.json` is regenerated, and `just test` passes after X16.

### X48. Tidy-up changes 11 and 12 say what the schema does: a decision keeps `rationale`, and the reason must not be blank

- **Resolves:** F081, F085 · **Partly:** F157 · **Size:** S · **Decision:** no · **Done:** `9d81b1c`

**The problem.** Two changes in the tidy-up draft state a rule more broadly or more loosely than the schema they describe. Change 11 says every element with free prose calls it `description`, except an insight. But RFC-0002 (`rfcs/0002-decouple-reports.md:80-81`) gave a decision its own `rationale`, and the draft never mentions it (F085). Change 12 says the `excluded` reason "can't be empty". But `BLANK_EXCLUDED`, the pattern `[^\x00-\x20\x7f]`, §5 and design §4 (:228) all require a reason that is not blank. So the RFC that the §9 row cites records a weaker rule than the contract enforces (F081). The specification and the contract are right and stay as they are.

**The fix.**
- Draft change 11 (`NNNN-settling-the-schema-for-v0.1.md:95`): "Every element with general free prose calls it `description`, except an insight, which calls it `notes`. A decision's prose is its `rationale`, the purpose-specific name RFC-0002 chose, and it stays. An insight's `notes` is renamed `description`." Don't add `Decision.description`.
- Draft change 12 (:101): "An option is excluded exactly when it has one. The reason can't be blank: it holds at least one character outside U+0000–U+0020 and U+007F, which the schema checks with a pattern." Use the explicit range, not the label "visible", so the sentence still holds whatever term X41 settles.
- The migration step for `excluded` (:262): add "A reason made only of spaces, tabs or line breaks fails `BLANK_EXCLUDED` and needs a real reason. `excluded: no` or `off` is a reason under YAML 1.2, so drop it as you would `excluded: false`." The rest of F157 (§5 and the LinkML description) is X29.
- `design/2026-11-03-v0.1-schema-changes.md:63`: change "one name for free prose" to "one name for an element's general prose (a decision keeps `rationale`)".

**Why this route.** The text being fixed is the tidy-up draft's own. Change 12's new wording records a narrowing the contract already implements but no source states: design §11 doesn't list it, and only design §4 has it. Change 11's wording is an editorial correction to the same draft. Neither changes what is valid.

**Done when.** Changes 11 and 12 and the migration step read as above, with `rationale` named in the draft. `grep -n "empty" NNNN-settling-the-schema-for-v0.1.md` no longer hits change 12. The specification, `analysis.yaml` and `rules.json` are untouched, and `just test` passes unchanged.

### X49. PIN_LIKE_COMMENT is dropped; §4 keeps the stray-space caveat as advice

- **Resolves:** F093 · **Size:** S · **Decision:** decided · **Done:** `0e882a0`

**The problem.** `PIN_LIKE_COMMENT` warns on an `inputs` entry whose line comment "looks like a pin", and nothing defines the term. RFC 0003:176 and :242 use the same words, and §9 (:1152) and design:402 copy them. The one example, `- f1_score #svm`, settles the obvious case only. `# svm`, `#rbf` (no such universe), `#svm_is_best, see notes` and a flow sequence broken across lines are left open, so two validators warn differently on one file (F093). `Evidence.artifact` loses a pin to a stray space in the same way, and the rule leaves it out. The rule is also the only one that needs a file's comments, which a validator otherwise never reads: §9's row says "(needs the source text)", and astra-tools' design keeps the raw text for it alone (tooling design:219).

**The fix.** Applied in the tidy-up joint pass (Order of work), with the spec edits riding with the RFC change.
- §9 validator table: delete the `PIN_LIKE_COMMENT` row (:1152).
- §4 Pins (:318-320): keep the caveat as advice to authors, with no code and no BCP 14 keyword: "In YAML, `#` inside a token is literal, so `f1_score#svm` needs no quotes. After a space it starts a comment: `- f1_score #svm` is the plain reference `f1_score`, followed by a comment. Write a pin with no space before its `#`." The link to `#validator` goes. §1's list of keyworded statements outside §9 doesn't change.
- X11 and X12 lose their `PIN_LIKE_COMMENT` bullets: X11's "drop '(needs the source text)'", X12's RECOMMENDED sentence in §2 and its §1 entry, and X12's design §10:551 mapping. With no row, §2's "reports every … rule" makes nothing mandatory that RFC 0003 only recommends, which is what F009 asks. X58's §1 bullet drops "next to X12's `PIN_LIKE_COMMENT` recommendation".
- Tidy-up RFC, Removing: one new change, "`PIN_LIKE_COMMENT` is removed", cited by title until the joint pass numbers the plan's new changes. Its body is one sentence: "RFC-0003's recommended warning on an input whose line comment looks like a pin (`- f1_score #svm`) is removed: a validator reads a file's data, not its comments, and the specification keeps the stray-space caveat as advice to authors." Update the Proposal's count ("twenty-nine changes") with the final number. In Implementation implications, the Removing bullet adds "Change N affects no file, only which warnings a validator gives." No migration step.
- Design: delete §7's row (design:402). §10's RFC 0003 row (design:551), "A stray space turns a pin into a comment", lands in "spec 4 (advice); the warning removed by the tidy-up". Add the change to §10's tidy-up table (after X28's renumbering) and to §11's list, with §11's count following.
- `just gen-schema`: `PIN_LIKE_COMMENT` leaves `rules.json`'s `rules`. The error map doesn't change, since the rule is in the validator layer. No fixture or test names it.
- astra-tools' design (:219, :395, :929) names the rule too. Dropping it there is astra-tools' change, outside this plan.

**Decided.** `PIN_LIKE_COMMENT` is dropped, since RFC 0003 only recommends it ("validators should flag", "warning (recommended)"), and validators then need no YAML comments. §4's caveat stays as advice to authors, and the removal is one line in the tidy-up RFC's Removing theme. Defining a pin-like comment (options a-c, with or without the output restriction and `Evidence.artifact`) is not taken.

**Why this route.** Dropping a rule that RFC 0003 states, even as a recommendation, changes an accepted RFC, so the removal is recorded in the tidy-up RFC, and the spec and contract edits land with it. No file's validity changes. The slip RFC 0003 names stays partly caught: an unpinned `Evidence.artifact` that should be pinned already draws `UNPINNED_EVIDENCE`, and §4 warns authors.

**Done when.** `PIN_LIKE_COMMENT` appears nowhere in `docs/`, `src/astra/schema/generated/` or design §7, and design §10 and §11 record its removal. §4 keeps the caveat with no link and no keyword. The tidy-up RFC has the change, its count and its implications line. `just test` passes with the regenerated `rules.json`, which differs only by the removed rule.

### X04. §7: an analysis's identity stated once, the declaring analysis's repository as the bound, declarations back to the analysis itself, and one spelling per entry

- **Resolves:** F013, F077, F111, F112, F113 · **Partly:** F004, F026, F035 · **Size:** M · **Depends on:** X17, X35, X33 · **Decision:** decided · **Route:** tidy-up RFC (changed from just do, Tom 2026-11-04) · **Done:** `6c0099a`

**The problem.** §7 gives an analysis's identity in two places, copied from RFC 0004 and RFC 0007 without RFC 0007's qualifiers. "The same directory is the same analysis" ignores the commit. The **Identity** paragraph under "Analyses from a commit" compares "the same commit and `path`", an unresolved relative path (F077). `DECLARATION_OUTSIDE_REPOSITORY` keeps RFC 0004's provisional "repository of the root". RFC 0007 replaced that with the declaring analysis's repository, so the row rejects every `path` declaration inside an analysis from a `url` (F013). The text also assumes a declaration names "another" analysis and an element has one address. Yet `path: .` and loops through `..` name the analysis itself (F111, F113). Two declarations of one directory give one element two addresses, so `inputs: [a.features, b.features]` passes. §4 still says "an output lists each of its inputs, decisions and conditions once" (F112). A recipe then gets one file twice through `{inputs}`, and `MISSING_RECORD_INPUTS` makes the record list it twice. That is the harm tidy-up change 24 names.

**The fix.**

Restating RFC 0004 and RFC 0007 (spec, schema and design; no RFC text):
- §7 Declarations: one **Identity** paragraph, moved here from "Analyses from a commit", which keeps a pointer to it. Gist: "A declaration names a directory, read at a commit or in the working tree. With a `url`, it is the top level of that repository at the declaration's `commit`. With a `path`, it is the `path` resolved against the folder of the declaring `astra.yaml`. It is read at the declaration's `commit` or, without one, as the declaring analysis is read. The root, and every analysis reached from it by `path` alone, is read in the working tree. The directory is taken from the top of the repository that holds the commit it is read at (in the working tree, the root's repository). Two declarations name the same analysis when their directories are the same path, compared as git compares paths (X35), at the same commit or both in the working tree. A URL says only where to get it. *Another analysis*, everywhere in this specification, is one with a different identity." X33's §3 "Reading git" covers how a path crosses a gitlink at a commit, which repository holds a submodule's commit, how `..` is resolved and what the working tree is. The paragraph cites it.
- §7, the sentence "The same directory is the same analysis, whichever analysis declares it and by whatever id: it is read once, …": rewrite its identity half and keep X23's clause: "One analysis, whichever analysis declares it and by whatever id, is read once, and each of its results is made at most once, however many declarations reach it." Then add: "A declaration by `path` alone that leads back to the declaring analysis's own directory (`path: .`, or a chain through `..`) names the analysis itself. An address through it is another spelling of one of the analysis's own elements (§4), and a universe key through it is another spelling of the analysis's own decision. A key or address that resolves to the analysis's own element drives no declaration on its chain, since driving sets another analysis's decisions. So `PARTIALLY_DRIVEN` and `MISSING_DECISION` don't count it." X34's chain rule applies only to keys that resolve to a decision of another analysis.
- §7 `path`: replace "It stays inside the repository of the root" with "It stays inside the declaring analysis's repository: in the working tree, the root's working tree, submodules included; at a commit, the repository that holds that commit." §7 `url`: "an analysis inside the root's repository is declared by `path`" becomes "an analysis in the declaring analysis's repository is declared by `path`". §7's first sentence, "An analysis uses another by declaring it in `analyses`", becomes "An analysis declares each analysis it uses in `analyses`…".
- §9 rows:
  - `DECLARATION_OUTSIDE_REPOSITORY`: "A declaration's `path` stays inside the declaring analysis's repository", source `0004, 0007 §1`.
  - `INPUT_IN_ANOTHER_ANALYSIS`: "An input's file is outside every analysis within its analysis's directory (a directory below it with an `astra.yaml` of its own)", source `0006`. This follows RFC 0006:54 and §5's bullet, which already reads this way. RFC 0007's `previous: {path: ., commit: X}` is another analysis with the root's directory, so "every other analysis's directory" would put every input inside one.
- analysis.yaml:
  - `AnalysisDeclaration`: "An analysis that this analysis uses, …".
  - `Analysis.analyses`: "The analyses this analysis uses, …".
  - `AnalysisDeclaration.path`: "The directory of the declared analysis's `astra.yaml`, relative to the folder of this `astra.yaml` and inside the repository it is read from (§7). It doesn't start with `/` or `~`, separates folders with `/` (never `\`), and has no ASCII control character (U+0000–U+001F, U+007F); `..` segments are allowed."
  - `url`: "An analysis inside the declaring analysis's repository is declared by `path`", if the sentence survives X17.
- Design §7 and §10: copy the two rows into design §7's catalogue. Keep the RFC 0004 traceability row "`path` relative to the declaring file, inside the root's repository, …" and add "(RFC 0007 §1: the declaring analysis's repository)".

One spelling per entry (new rule, Tom's item 1; needs tidy-up text):
- New validator code **`DUPLICATE_ELEMENT`**. It pairs with `DUPLICATE_KEY` and `DUPLICATE_ENTRY`: those compare what is written, this compares what it names. §9 validator row, placed just before `CONFLICTING_SELECTIONS`:

  ```
  | `DUPLICATE_ELEMENT` | MUST | No list of addresses, references, option references or conditions holds two spellings of one entry, and no universe's own `decisions` has two keys that spell one decision | tidy-up, §4, §6 |
  ```

  It is validator-layer because it needs the declarations resolved. It covers `Output.inputs`, `Output.decisions`, `Output.when`, `Decision.when`, `Option.requires` and `Option.incompatible_with`, and the keys of `Universe.decisions`.
- §4 Addresses, after the paragraph on `UNRESOLVED_ADDRESS`: "Two addresses written differently that resolve to one element are two **spellings** of it. In `examples/iris_pipeline/classification/astra.yaml`, `feature_extraction.features` and `iris.feature_extraction.features` are one output. Two conditions, or two option references, are spellings of one entry when their addresses spell one decision and they name the same option, and two conditions also need both or neither to have `~`. Two references are spellings of one entry when their addresses spell one element and both are unpinned, or both pin one universe (§7). No list holds two spellings of one entry ([`DUPLICATE_ELEMENT`](#validator)), so a recipe never gets one file twice through `{inputs}`." A pinned and an unpinned reference to one output are never spellings of one entry, even in a universe where both read the same result. `REDUNDANT_PIN` already warns when the output is built once.
- §4 Ids and maps: "No list of strings holds the same entry twice ([`DUPLICATE_ENTRY`](#parse-and-schema)), nor two spellings of one entry ([`DUPLICATE_ELEMENT`](#validator)), so an output lists each of its inputs, decisions and conditions once." The sentence's conclusion then holds.
- §6 Declaring universes, the key sentence: add "or another spelling of either through declarations (§7)", then "A universe's own `decisions` give each decision one key ([`DUPLICATE_ELEMENT`](#validator)); a derived universe can still set a decision it inherits under another spelling."
- §7 Driving, the "either spelling" bullet: "An analysis reached through two driven declarations can have a decision set under either spelling (`stage.inner.d` or `inner.d`), and a universe's own `decisions` set it under one ([`DUPLICATE_ELEMENT`](#validator)). Every spelling of one decision in a universe selects the same option ([`CONFLICTING_SELECTIONS`](#validator))." X34 later rewords the `CONFLICTING_SELECTIONS` half for the effective selection. Keep `CONFLICTING_SELECTIONS`, since RFC 0004:409 states it. With X34's merge by decision, it fires only beside `DUPLICATE_ELEMENT`, on two keys that disagree, and a validator reports both.
- analysis.yaml `Universe.decisions`: "The options the universe selects, as `<decision>: <option>`, one key per decision. Each key is the id of a decision of the analysis or, for a decision of an analysis it drives, a flat key: the decision's address through the declarations, `<declaration>.<decision>`, such as `stage.method`, or another spelling of either (§7)."
- Tidy-up RFC: a new change under Tightening, "One spelling of each entry". The joint pass fixes its number, since X34, X37 and X45 also claim 30. Text, in the draft's style: "RFC-0004 lets one analysis be reached through two declarations, so one decision can be set as `stage.inner.d` or `inner.d` and one output read under two addresses. A universe may set a decision under both as long as they agree, and RFC-0004 leaves open whether that should warn. Nothing stops a list naming one element under two spellings either, as in `inputs: [a.features, b.features]` where `a` and `b` declare one directory. A recipe gets the file twice and RFC-0005's record lists it twice, the harm change 24 removes for a repeated string. A universe's own `decisions` set each decision under one key, and a list of addresses, references, option references or conditions names each entry once, whatever its spelling. A derived universe can still set an inherited decision under another spelling. A validator reports either as `DUPLICATE_ELEMENT`." Add the change to the Tightening line of *Compatibility* ("can make a file invalid"). Add a migration step: "keep one spelling of each decision in a universe's `decisions`, and of each entry in a list".
- Design: copy the row into §7's catalogue. In §10's RFC 0004 table, the row "Two spellings must agree" becomes "`CONFLICTING_SELECTIONS`; agreeing spellings in one universe, and two spellings of one entry in a list: `DUPLICATE_ELEMENT` (tidy-up change NN)". Add a §11 item: "A universe's own `decisions` give each decision one key, and no list holds two spellings of one entry (`DUPLICATE_ELEMENT`); RFC 0004:221 allows agreeing spellings." In §13, strike through "Agreeing spellings" and mark it **Settled** in the same form as the other settled point. X28's §13 line ("stays open, widened by X04's aliased entries") follows: after X28, §13 has no "Agreeing spellings" point left open.

Then run `just gen-schema` and `just gen-doc`.

**Decided** (Tom, 2026-11-04). Identity, the repository bound and declarations back to the analysis itself: as recommended. Aliased entries: decided by pending item 1. Two spellings of one decision in one universe's own `decisions`, agreeing or not, are errors. So are two spellings of one entry in one list. Both come under a new validator-layer code, through the tidy-up RFC. This replaces the earlier choice (a), an open point in design §13. It also settles design §13 "Agreeing spellings". A derived universe setting an inherited decision under another spelling stays valid, as X34's merge by decision needs. The code's name, `DUPLICATE_ELEMENT`, is confirmed (Tom, 2026-11-05). The tidy-up text fixes it.

**Why this route.** RFC 0007 §1 decides the identity and the bound. `path` is "the directory of an analysis in the declaring analysis's repository", and a `path` without `commit` "means exactly what it means in RFC 0004". §2 makes "the commit id, with the `path`" the identity. RFC 0004:65 and :221 make the same directory the same analysis under every spelling. RFC 0006:54 bounds an input by the analyses within its folder. These parts restate accepted RFCs, so they need no RFC text (pending item 15). The one-spelling rule is new. RFC 0004:221 allows agreeing spellings, and :688 leaves only a warning open. The rule makes files invalid that RFC 0004 accepts, so it needs a tidy-up change. That change is the only part of X04 that needs RFC text. The rule costs validators little. They already resolve every address for `UNRESOLVED_ADDRESS`, and the rule compares the resolved entries by the identity this entry defines. That is why it sits in X04 rather than a fix of its own.

**Done when.**
- No sentence bounds a declaration's `path` by the root's repository except the working-tree clause.
- The `DECLARATION_OUTSIDE_REPOSITORY` and `INPUT_IN_ANOTHER_ANALYSIS` rows read as reworded, and the `path` and `url` descriptions don't say "root".
- §4, §6 and §7 cite `DUPLICATE_ELEMENT`, and §4 defines a spelling.
- The tidy-up draft carries the new change, its compatibility line and its migration step. Design §7, §10, §11 and §13 match.
- `rules.json` carries `DUPLICATE_ELEMENT` and the reworded rows, and `just test` passes with no fixture change: all three rules are validator-layer.
- `examples/iris_pipeline` stays valid: no universe or list in it holds two spellings.

## When RFC (#93)

Settled by, or belonging to, the conditional-elements RFC draft (`astra-rfcs/when/rfc-draft.md`).

### X50. When RFC: a use counts where it happens, across analyses too

- **Resolves:** F048, F108 · **Size:** M · **Depends on:** X13, X34 · **Decision:** decided

**The problem.** RFC 0004's `NOT_BUILT_IN_UNIVERSE`, `UNDRIVEN_DECLARATION` and `INPUT_NOT_BUILT` (rfcs/0004:410-412) say an element is used or read "in the universe being built", but not where each kind of use counts. The spec copies them (§5 l.482-485, §7 l.683-688 and l.720-730, §9 l.1142-1144). Read statically, `INPUT_NOT_BUILT` rejects two outputs that share a `when` where one reads the other, in every universe where the `when` fails (F108). A constraint's user is an option, which is never built, so nothing says in which universes a decision named in `requires` or `incompatible_with` must exist (F048).

**The fix.**
