- When draft, modification 3 (l.91): add "Across analyses the same holds: in a universe that selects the option, a decision of an analysis not built there doesn't exist, and naming it is an error."
- When draft, modification 4 (l.109): add "A use counts where it happens: an output's reads and listed decisions in each universe it is built in, a condition's decisions in each universe where it is tested (unless another condition of its `when` fails there), a constraint's decisions in each universe that selects its option. Evidence isn't built in a universe, so its citations count in none: these rules don't apply to it, RFC-0004's warning asks it to pin, and its status is the check phase's (X52). RFC-0004's `NOT_BUILT_IN_UNIVERSE` (0004:410), `INPUT_NOT_BUILT` (0004:412) and, under (i) below, `UNDRIVEN_DECLARATION` (0004:411) are checked the same way."
- When draft, validator table, third row (l.117): "The constraints of a selected option name only decisions that exist in the universe being built: their `when` holds there and their analysis is built there (`NOT_BUILT_IN_UNIVERSE` across analyses)".
- When the RFC lands, in the spec (on X13's wording):
  - §7 'Consuming': replace "whatever uses it can be built only where…" with the "use counts where it happens" sentence, evidence clause included.
  - §7 'The universe being built': open with "Wherever such a use counts". End with "every output read without a pin by an output built in the universe being built is active there".
  - §5: "An inactive output isn't built in that universe and reads nothing there. No output built there reads it without a pin."
  - §9: `NOT_BUILT_IN_UNIVERSE` (and `UNDRIVEN_DECLARATION` under (i)) gets ", in each universe where its use counts". `INPUT_NOT_BUILT`: "Every output read without a pin by an output built in a universe is active (its `when` holds) there". Add the when RFC to the Source cells. Run `just gen-schema`.
  - Design §13: mark the `when` point settled.

**Decision** (Tom, 2026-11-04: decided as recommended). The draft already rules out reading (a), "checked in every universe", which only the current §7/§9 text allows: modification 3 says "A constraint is only checked in a universe that selects the option it's written on", and modification 4 says "The rules are the same within one analysis and across analyses". Two questions remain.
1. (b) A decision of an unbuilt analysis doesn't exist where the option is selected (error), or (c) it reads as "nothing selected" (`requires` fails as `MISSING_REQUIRED_OPTION`, `incompatible_with` holds). Recommend (b): it is the draft as written, and its 'Questions' section defers (c) as a relaxation that "can come later without making any file invalid".
2. Is `UNDRIVEN_DECLARATION` also checked only where a use counts? (i) Yes: F048's r1 and r2 are valid; r3 fails `NOT_BUILT_IN_UNIVERSE` and `UNDRIVEN_DECLARATION`. (ii) No, it applies to the whole root file: r1 and r2 fail `UNDRIVEN_DECLARATION` (no key drives `b.c`), and (b) decides only constraints in a driven analysis's file, such as F048's stack with `m2: {requires: [c.d.p]}` moved into B and `u1` setting `b.m: b.m1`. Recommend (i): modification 4 checks "RFC-0004's reads" per universe, and a use that counts in no universe has no frame needing a driven declaration. (ii) is stricter and could be relaxed to (i) later.

**Why this route.** The when draft's modification 1 states F108's rule ("every output it reads without a pin must exist in each universe the output is built in") and widens RFC 0004's row. Modifications 3 and 4, quoted above, settle F048 once the draft says so across analyses.

**Done when.** The when draft carries the sentences and the row. On landing, `just test` passes with the regenerated `rules.json`. By hand, under (b) and (i): F108's document is valid; F048's r1 and r2 are valid and r3 fails `NOT_BUILT_IN_UNIVERSE` and `UNDRIVEN_DECLARATION`; the B-file variant is valid, and fails `NOT_BUILT_IN_UNIVERSE` with `b.m: b.m2` and `incompatible_with`.

### X51. When RFC: driving counts only the universes where something reached through the chain is active

- **Resolves:** F002 · **Size:** M · **Depends on:** X34, X13 · **Decision:** decided

**Superseded in part (2026-11-05).** PARTIALLY_DRIVEN is removed: driving is a property of the driver's universes together, and a key missing in one of them is a MISSING_DECISION. So a universe with nothing active to set is valid. What remains for the when RFC is a chain none of whose decisions is active in any universe of the driver.

**The problem.** Driving means setting keys through a declaration. Take a driver universe in which `when` switches off every decision reached through a driven declaration. That universe can't set a key (INACTIVE_DECISION). If it sets none, PARTIALLY_DRIVEN fails, and if no universe sets one, UNDRIVEN_DECLARATION fails. So every way of writing such a stack is invalid (F002, whose evidence stack passes the schema layer). F002 also names design §13's point: an analysis with no decisions can never be "driven". That point is about composition, not `when`, and X34 settles it.

**The fix.**
- When RFC draft, a paragraph under modification 4, "Driving where decisions are switched off". A universe counts for a chain of declarations only if some decision reached through the chain (the declared analysis's, or one further along any chain through it) is active in its effective selection. The chain is driven by all of the universes that count, or by none. A universe that doesn't count takes that status, so a driven analysis is built there with nothing to set. If no universe counts, the chain is read as X34's no-decisions clause reads an analysis with no decisions: driven by every universe of the driver when its analysis has no universes of its own, and by none otherwise. Use F002's `cal`/`raw` stack as the example, and give `stage` a second output, `count`, with no `when`, which reads `root.table`. The root reads `stage.count` without a pin. Table: `cal` counts and drives `stage`. `raw` and `raw_derived` don't count, so they take "driven" and build `stage.count`. The stack is valid.
- §7 'Driving' (l.699-700) and the §9 `PARTIALLY_DRIVEN` row (l.1140) use X34's sentence plus the exemption: "A chain of declarations from the driver's file is driven by all of its universes or by none, counting only the universes in which a decision reached through the chain is active. Any other universe takes the chain's status." Then run `just gen-schema`.
- The no-decisions clause (option iii: an analysis with no decisions and no universes of its own is driven by every universe of its driver) is not part of this fix: it moves to X34's change 30, which defines "drives".

**Decided.** (A): a chain is judged only over the universes in which a decision reached through it is active, and the others take its status. Not (B), which forces a dummy decision to keep one active everywhere, nor (C), which lets a universe set a switched-off decision as a marker and weakens INACTIVE_DECISION.

**Why this route.** The skip is about decisions switched off by `when`. The draft's principle ("nothing can use, read or test an element where that element is switched off") and modification 4 ("the same within one analysis and across analyses") cover it, but they don't say what it means for driving. The tidy-up changes don't decide it.

**Done when.** The regenerated `rules.json` passes `just check-generated`. By hand: F002's stack with `stage.count` is valid. The u1/u2 `stage.inner` stack fails PARTIALLY_DRIVEN (u2 drives `stage` through `stage.inner.d`, u1 counts and doesn't drive it).

## Checker and runner phase

Semantics only checkers and runners need. Nothing in the schema-and-validator phase depends on them, so they can wait for that phase, as design §12 and §13 already defer such points.

### X53. Check phase: STALE_RESULT settles the option compared, a missing read result, and the working tree

- **Resolves:** F024, F040, F041 · **Size:** S · **Depends on:** X07, X33 · **Decision:** decided · **Done (now part):** `ab9310a`, `6d26cc1`

**The problem.** `STALE_RESULT` carries RFC 0005 §4 over word for word, edges and all. "The options of its decisions" has two readings, and the one that fits the spec's example doesn't say which of the option's fields count (F024). A result that reads a `missing` result has no defined status, though §7 asks the results an analysis from a commit was made from to be unchanged (F040). And nothing says what a checker compares the record's commit with for a local result: the working tree or HEAD (F041).

**The fix.**
- Now: design §13 gets an open point worded like "One result, two names": "**`STALE_RESULT`: which option counts, a read result that is missing, working tree or HEAD.** For the check and runner phase to settle", with decision (1)-(3)'s options and recommendations below. The spec, §9 and `rules.json` stay as they are.
- Check phase, through the RFC that amends RFC 0005 §4 (and RFC 0007 §4 for the scope):
  - §8 "When a result is unchanged", new opening sentence: "A checker judges a result of the analysis being checked, or of an analysis reached from it by path alone, against the working tree: the `astra.yaml` files, input files, results and records on disk, committed or not. A result of an analysis from a commit is judged at its declared commit (§7)." This is the frame X33 sets for the validator. Add that a runner can then read the records it has just written.
  - Second list item: "the option its universe's effective selection gives each of its decisions. Only the option's id counts, since only the id reaches the recipe (`{decisions.<entry>}`). Editing that option's other fields, or adding or editing other options, leaves the result unchanged."
  - Last item: "the results it reads. Each is compared by the `content_hash` of its current record with the hash in this record's `inputs`. A result that reads a result that isn't `unchanged` (stale or missing) is stale, and the checker says which. Once that one is rebuilt, this one is unchanged again if the rebuilt bytes are the same" (RFC 0005 §4, l.182).
  - §9 `STALE_RESULT` row: one sentence with the same scope and three points. Regenerate `rules.json`.

**Decision** (Tom, 2026-11-04: decided as recommended).
1. Which option counts. (a, recommended) The selected option's id: all the recipe sees. (b) The selected option's whole definition. It contradicts "editing … a description … leaves a result unchanged". (c) The selection plus the decision's whole `options` map. It is RFC 0005 §4's most literal reading ("an option of one of its decisions has changed") and fits both examples, but adding an option to explore later makes every result of the output stale.
2. A read result that is `missing`. (a, recommended) Stale, as in §7's commit case and `STALE_INPUT`. (b) Unchanged while the hashes match, which lets a broken upstream record pass.
3. What a local result is judged against. (a, recommended) The working tree: what an author checks after an edit, and a chain of builds works with no commit between steps. (b) HEAD: the same answer locally and in CI, but every result is `missing` right after a run. It must match X33's reading for the validator. X55 ("what it compares") and X54 ("what git holds at the path … now") rely on this answer.

**Why this route.** The spec follows RFC 0005 §4 (l.169-184) as written, so nothing has drifted and nothing needs restoring. No schema or validator rule reads `STALE_RESULT`, and design §12 and §13 defer check semantics to the check and runner phase.

**Done when.** Now: design §13 lists the three questions. The spec, §9 and `rules.json` are unchanged. Check phase: conformance fixtures show that changing the selection makes a result stale, that adding an option or describing the selected one doesn't, that a reader of a `missing` result is stale, that an uncommitted input edit makes a result stale, and that records just written are `unchanged`.

### X54. Check phase: a file's content is the bytes git stores, a commit's files are its tree, and a checker that can't get an input's content gives `unknown`

- **Resolves:** F044, F071, F073 · **Size:** M · **Depends on:** X02, X33, X53; X08 and X46 (done) · **Decision:** decided · **Done (now part):** `d83dac1`

**The problem.** The specification relies on "a commit's files" and on "the content" of an input or result, and defines neither. X08 (887946f) and X46 (9b06dcb) settled what stands in for content: §5 says "Something standing in for the content isn't the content", stand-ins are the tool's, and `STALE_RESULT` compares "the content of its inputs, never what stands in for it". They didn't say what the content is.
- *Which files* (F044). `NOT_AT_COMMIT` drives an analysis "on exactly its files at that commit". Nothing says whether that is the analysis's directory or the commit's tree, or whether submodules come with it. A driven recipe's `../src/x.py` runs under one reading and fails under the other.
- *Which bytes* (F073). A record's `content_hash` and `size` are "of the result's file" and "of the content the recipe read". Git's blob, a checkout under the commit's `.gitattributes`, and a checkout under the reader's `core.autocrlf` give different bytes for one file under `eol`, `ident`, `working-tree-encoding` or a filter. Two conforming checkers then disagree on `present` and on `unchanged` in a clean clone, and two runners record different hashes for one input at one commit.
- *Without the bytes* (F071). A checker that can't get an input's bytes, behind a stand-in that names no content hash (a WORM git-annex key), has no true status to give. Every planned result must still get `unchanged`, `stale` or `missing`, and "How a checker finds this out is the tooling's choice" lets each checker pick.

**The fix.**
- Now: design §12's check-phase items get "**Content and a commit's files** (X54)", the five decided points below in one line each. Nothing goes in §13: they are decided. The spec, the schema and `rules.json` don't change in this phase.
- Check and runner phase, through the RFC that amends RFC 0005 §3 and §4 and RFC 0007 §5 and §7 (no tidy-up change: item 15 keeps X54 out of the draft):
  - §8 'What a record holds', a paragraph before "Every hash names its algorithm": "**Content.** A file's content is the bytes git stores for it: at a commit, its blob; in the working tree, its bytes on disk. Git's checkout conversions (`text` and `eol`, `core.autocrlf`, `ident`, `working-tree-encoding`, a filter) never enter it: a checker neither applies nor undoes them, and reads no git configuration to do so. A stand-in the tool recognises gives the content it names (§5): the hash and size it names, or the bytes it leads to. Every `content_hash` and `size` is of content, and so is what a recipe reads ([`CONVERTED_CHECKOUT`](#runner))." This is the reading item 11 gives an `astra.yaml` (X33): the raw blob at a commit, the file on disk in the working tree.
  - §7 'Driving', before the `NOT_AT_COMMIT` sentence: "A commit's files are its tree, with each submodule at the commit its gitlink pins, got from the URL `.gitmodules` gives at that commit (X33, item 10). The driven recipes run in the declared analysis's directory within them, so `../src` and `..` reach what the commit holds there." §7 'What is read': "the bytes of each result or input file that a recipe reads" becomes "… reads, as git stores them (§8)".
  - §8 'What a runner does', after the `STALE_INPUT` sentence: "A recipe reads the content of each input and result: the bytes git stores, or what a stand-in the runner recognises leads to. Where a checkout would give other bytes, the runner refuses ([`CONVERTED_CHECKOUT`](#runner)). A runner that makes its own checkout to run in (X56) can make one with no conversion." Then plain advice, no keyword: "To let a runner run in any clone, set `-text` and no `ident`, `working-tree-encoding` or filter (other than a stand-in's) in the `.gitattributes` that apply to an analysis's inputs and `results/`." It is advice, not a `SHOULD`, so §1's count of recommendations stays two.
  - §9 runner table, a new row: `CONVERTED_CHECKOUT` | MUST | "Every input and result a recipe reads has, where the recipe reads it, the bytes git stores for it, or is got through a stand-in the runner recognises" | `0005 §3, 0007 §7, §8`.
  - §9 `NOT_AT_COMMIT`: "A runner drives an analysis from a commit on exactly that commit's files (§7)".
  - §8 'When a result is unchanged', the inputs item: "the content of its inputs (§8 'Content'), never what stands in for it". Then: "An input's content is unchanged when git holds the same blob at its path at the record's commit and now (both commits, §7); or when its bytes have the record's hash and size; or when a stand-in the checker recognises names the record's SHA-256 and size, or is the same, byte for byte, at the record's commit and now. A recognised stand-in naming another hash or size is a change. Anything at the path the checker doesn't recognise as a stand-in is read as the file (§5: two tools may judge one input differently). Otherwise the checker can't tell, and the result is `unknown`, never `unchanged`." In the working tree (X53's frame), "now" is the file on disk, compared by its bytes: a blob's identity counts only between two commits.
  - X53's last item, amended: "A result that reads a result that is `stale` or `missing` is stale. One that reads an `unknown` result, and that nothing else makes stale, is `unknown`."
  - "How a checker finds this out is the tooling's choice" ends "…, within §8 'Content'".
  - §8 'Statuses' and §9's check intro: the record status is "`unchanged`, `stale`, `unknown`, or `missing`". §9 Statuses table, a new row after `stale`: `unknown` | "The result has a valid record where its address says, and the checker can't tell whether `STALE_RESULT` holds: it can't get the content of an input, or of a result it reads, and no stand-in it recognises names its hash and size. The checker says which". The bytes status stays `present` or `absent` (X08, item 13): a stand-in at the default place is `absent`, whatever it names.
  - `STALE_EVIDENCE` passes `unknown` on through "the cited result's status" with no change. `STALE_INPUT` refuses to build from an `unknown` result: in the current row through "`unchanged`", in X02's row by adding it ("while that result is `missing`, `stale` or `unknown`").
  - provenance.yaml: `ProvenanceRecord.content_hash` and `size`, and `RecordedInput.content_hash` and `size`, say "of the content (spec §8)" after X08's wording. `Generation.commit`'s "pins every file of the code" links §7's commit's files.
  - Design §7's rows and §10's RFC 0007 row ("Bytes from what git holds at the commit" becomes "Bytes git stores at the commit") follow. Run `just gen-schema` and commit `rules.json`: the new row and the `NOT_AT_COMMIT` statement. A runner-layer code needs no fixture.

**Decided** (Tom, 2026-11-04).
1. *Which files.* (a) The commit's tree with its submodules, as X33 reaches them; their URLs come from `.gitmodules` at that commit (item 10). Not taken: (b) the analysis's directory only.
2. *Which bytes at a commit.* Changed to the bytes git stores, the raw blob (item 7). It needs no checkout, it is what git checks against the commit id (§7 "What is read"), and it is how item 11 reads an `astra.yaml`. The former recommendation, a checkout pinned to `core.autocrlf=false` and `core.eol=lf` under the commit's `.gitattributes`, goes: a checker would have to imitate git's conversions, which it never does.
3. *Which bytes in the working tree.* The bytes on disk, which are what git stores wherever no conversion applies. A runner refuses when a checkout would give other bytes (item 7), so every record's hashes are of stored bytes. In a clone whose checkout converts (an `autocrlf=true` clone with no `-text`), the checker reports what is on disk: the input changed and the result's bytes `absent`. At a commit it gives the blob's answer. Not taken: the blob git would stage from the file, which needs git's conversions.
4. *Who reports a breach.* Changed: the runner refuses, under a `MUST` row (`CONVERTED_CHECKOUT`), not a warning. The `.gitattributes` setting is advice with no keyword. Not taken: a check-layer row; a validator `SHOULD` reading `.gitattributes`.
5. *Without the content.* (a) `unknown`, a record status beside `unchanged`, `stale` and `missing`. Not taken: (b) the checker MUST fetch the bytes or fail; (c) `stale`, which contradicts its definition.

With X46, which stand-ins count is the tool's, so two checkers can give `unchanged` and `unknown`, or `unknown` and `stale`, for one input. Tom accepted this.

**Why this route.** RFC 0005 §4 leaves the method open ("How a checker finds this out is up to the tooling"), and RFC 0007 §7 says only "exactly those files". Defining content as the stored bytes adds no conversion to any checker, makes §7's "git checks everything read against the commit id" true of what a recipe reads, and turns the cases where git's bytes and a checkout's differ into a runner refusal rather than two answers. Every point binds only checkers and runners, and design §12 defers their semantics to the check and runner phase.

**Done when.** Now: design §12 lists the five decided points. Check and runner phase: `rules.json` has `CONVERTED_CHECKOUT` and the new `NOT_AT_COMMIT` statement, and conformance fixtures show:
- an `eol=crlf`, `ident` or `working-tree-encoding` input read at a commit gives the blob's status in every checker, whatever the reader's config;
- a runner refuses a recipe that reads such an input, or an input in an `autocrlf=true` clone with no `-text`;
- a `.gitattributes` change alone, same blob, leaves a result `unchanged` between two commits;
- a driven recipe reads `../src` from the commit's tree;
- a WORM key without its bytes gives `unknown`, a SHA-256 stand-in naming the recorded hash and size gives `unchanged`, and one naming another gives `stale`;
- a result reading an `unknown` result is `unknown`, evidence citing it gets `unknown`, and a runner refuses to build from it.

### X55. Which repository's commit a record names (now) and a rule that it stays in history (check phase)

- **Resolves:** F019, F043 · **Size:** M · **Depends on:** X02, X03, X33, X38, X53, X56 · **Decision:** decided · **Done (now part):** `6a48242`

**The problem.** RFC 0005 §2 defines a record's `commit` as "the commit the recipe ran from" and never says which repository it is in. So §8 and `Generation.commit` let a record of a submodule analysis's result name the superproject's commit. RFC 0007 §4 commits that record in the submodule's repository, which lacks that commit (F043). RFC 0005 §3 says the commit "has to stay in the history of the commits that hold the record", but no rule, code or status covers a breach. §8 states it as a fact, backed only by a SHOULD to merge rather than squash or rebase. A record whose commit has left the history is valid and in its place, so it isn't `missing`, and `STALE_RESULT` can't be judged without that commit, so it is neither `unchanged` nor `stale` (F019).

**The fix.**
- Now, just do:
  - §8 `commit` bullet and `Generation.commit` (`provenance.yaml`), one wording merged with X03: "The full id of the git commit the recipe ran from: a commit of the repository the record is committed in, the submodule's own commit for a result that belongs to an analysis in a submodule (the driver's commit for what a driver's universes build). It pins every file of the code and every `astra.yaml` in that repository: an analysis in a submodule of it through the commit the submodule is pinned at, and an analysis from a commit through its declaration's `commit`." The clause on submodules stays: a root's record of an output that reads `lib.base` names the superproject's commit, and X33's decision 3 rests on it (RFC 0005:119). This is the commit that the runner MUST record honestly in the agreed list of runner MUSTs (X02's runner rows).
  - §5, beside the recipe SHOULD that `UNDECLARED_READ` becomes (X02): "The commit pins only its own repository, so a recipe of an analysis in a submodule uses files outside the submodule's repository only through its output's inputs." This is the cost of the wording above. RFC 0004:63 allows a submodule that declares `..`, but such a submodule can no longer use the parent's code or `astra.yaml` as pinned by its commit.
  - §8 (:977-979): "stays in the history of every commit" becomes "has to stay in the history of the commits that hold the record", as RFC 0005 §3 says. The SHOULD to merge rather than squash or rebase stays.
  - Design §12, check phase: the rule, the status changes and the fixtures below, as decided. Nothing goes in §13.
  - `just gen-schema`: the provenance schemas' descriptions change; no keyword changes.
- Check phase, through the RFC that adds the row to RFC 0005's check table:
  - §9 check table, new row: `RECORD_COMMIT_NOT_ANCESTOR` | MUST | "The commit a planned result's record names is the commit the record is read at, or an ancestor of it, in the repository the record is committed in" | `missing` | 0005 §3. The commit it is read at follows X33 and X53: the commit that holds the record or, in the working tree, HEAD, or MERGE_HEAD during a merge (either passes). For a result of an analysis from a commit, it is the declared commit. A commit the repository no longer has breaks the rule too.
  - §8 "Statuses": "`missing` when it has no valid record where its address says, or its record names a commit outside the history of the commit it is read at". Statuses table: `unchanged` becomes "a valid record where its address says, `RECORD_COMMIT_NOT_ANCESTOR` holds, and `STALE_RESULT` holds"; `stale` becomes the same with "`STALE_RESULT` doesn't hold"; `missing` adds `RECORD_COMMIT_NOT_ANCESTOR` to its list of codes, merged with X37's and X59's edits to the same row.
  - `STALE_RESULT` isn't judged for a record that breaks the new rule: it follows from that error, so it isn't reported (X38).
  - §8, after the SHOULD-merge sentence: "A record whose commit has left the history is `missing` ([`RECORD_COMMIT_NOT_ANCESTOR`](#check))." §1's sentence stays as it is: how a branch was merged still can't be checked, only the outcome.
  - The checker checks the rule. `just gen-schema`, and commit `rules.json`.

**Decided.** Tom keeps the check rule as recommended: `RECORD_COMMIT_NOT_ANCESTOR`, MUST, with status `missing`, checked by the checker, and the "now" wording on which repository's commit a record names. A fourth record status (b) and advice only (c) are not taken. The second question, what a runner records when a tool re-creates commits, is dropped: how a runner keeps its files is outside the specification, and the rule judges the outcome as for any other record.

**Why this route.** Which commit a record names is not an open choice. Read together, RFC 0005 §3 ("has to stay in the history of the commits that hold the record") and RFC 0007 §4 (records committed in the analysis's repository; "`<commit A>` can be got wherever `<commit B>` can") require the record's own repository. Naming the root's commit would mean amending RFC 0005 §3. The 'Now' changes restore what the RFCs say together and change no keyword (`commit` stays `commit_id`), so they are just do and keep F043's contradictions out of v0.1. A `missing` status fits the breach: the record can't vouch for anything, the remedy is to rebuild, and the set of statuses doesn't change. The rule concerns only checkers, which design §12 leaves to the check phase.

**Done when.** Now: §8 and `Generation.commit` carry the merged wording, §5 carries the submodule sentence, §8 says "has to stay", design §12 lists the check-phase rule, and `just test` passes with the regenerated provenance schemas committed. Check phase: `rules.json` has `RECORD_COMMIT_NOT_ANCESTOR`. Conformance fixtures make a squash-merged record `missing` (with no `STALE_RESULT` reported), a submodule record naming the submodule's commit `unchanged`, a record just written at HEAD `unchanged`, and, during a merge, a record whose commit is an ancestor of MERGE_HEAD but not of HEAD pass. This depends on X53 (what `STALE_RESULT` compares) and X56 (a submodule's changes count as changes).

### X56. UNCOMMITTED_CHANGES is judged in the checkout a recipe runs in (now); what a change is (runner phase)

- **Resolves:** F045 · **Partly:** F053 · **Size:** S · **Depends on:** X02, X40, X53, X55 · **Decision:** decided · **Done (now part):** `91a808f`

**The problem.** `UNCOMMITTED_CHANGES` (RFC 0005 §3, spec §8 "What a runner does" and the §9 runner row) refuses a run "while the repository has changes", but nothing defines a change. Untracked files, ignored files, submodules, earlier runs' records and results, and tools' caches are all left open, so a runner using `git diff HEAD` and one using `git status` disagree on the same checkout (F045). The §9 row also drops two things: RFC 0005 §3's "itself" (F053, restored by X40), and the exemption RFC 0007 §2 and §7 grant to what a tool keeps of, or puts in place to drive, an analysis from a commit (F045). §8 states that exemption for what is read (spec §7, "Where a tool keeps what it read"), but the row doesn't. And "the repository" leaves out a common way to run: in a clean worktree at a commit, while the author's own working tree has edits in progress.

**The fix.**
- Now, just do. X40 and X56 edit the row together; X02 doesn't touch it.
  - §9 runner row: `UNCOMMITTED_CHANGES` | MUST | "A runner runs each recipe in a checkout of a commit whose only changes are the records and results it writes itself, each result's bytes the recorded ones, and what a tool keeps of, or puts in place to drive, an analysis from a commit" | `0005 §3, 0007 §2, 0007 §7`.
  - §8 "What a runner does", the sentence X02's list keeps as "It runs only committed code … ([`UNCOMMITTED_CHANGES`])": "It runs only committed code. It runs each recipe in a checkout of a commit, the repository's own working tree or one it makes for the run (a git worktree, say), and refuses to run while that checkout has changes other than the records and results it writes itself, each result's bytes the recorded ones, and what a tool keeps of, or puts in place to drive, an analysis from a commit ([`UNCOMMITTED_CHANGES`](#runner)). Changes outside that checkout don't stop it." X02's `WRONG_WORKING_DIRECTORY` then means the analysis's directory in that checkout, and X55's `commit` wording ("the commit the recipe ran from") is that checkout's commit. X02 links this sentence and doesn't restate it.
  - Spec §7's sentence on what a tool keeps of what it read (:841-844) stays: it is the same exemption, stated where it applies.
  - Design §7 runner table (:480): the same row. Design §10, RFC 0005 table (:644): Lands-in stays `UNCOMMITTED_CHANGES`; spec 8. Design §10, RFC 0007 table (:674): Lands-in stays.
  - Design §11, one new line, with no tidy-up RFC text (item 17): "`UNCOMMITTED_CHANGES` is judged in the checkout a recipe runs in, which can be a clean worktree at the recorded commit while the repository's own working tree has changes. RFC 0005 §3's rule is 'Only committed code is run' (l.161); its 'while the repository has changes' (l.149) is one way to keep it."
  - Design §12, runner phase: the definition and fixtures below, as decided. Nothing goes in §13.
  - `just gen-schema`, and commit `rules.json` with the new statement and Source.
- Runner phase, through the RFC that amends RFC 0005 §3:
  - Define a change, judged in the checkout the recipe runs in: what `git status` reports there, with files ignored by a committed `.gitignore` left out, less one exemption: every record, and every result whose bytes match the SHA-256 in its record, whichever run wrote them. A hand edit to a result is a change. What a tool keeps of, or puts in place to drive, an analysis from a commit is exempt only under an analysis's `.astra/` or in an ignored place.
  - It holds whatever each machine's git configuration says. A file ignored only by `.git/info/exclude` or `core.excludesFile` counts. Submodules are checked whatever `submodule.<name>.ignore` or `diff.ignoreSubmodules` say, since a committed `.gitmodules` `ignore = all` would hide a submodule off its pinned commit. Reference form, in the checkout and in each of its submodules, with the exemption applied in each (a runner's record in a submodule analysis shows in the parent as ` M sub`): `git status --porcelain=v2 --untracked-files=no --ignore-submodules=none` for tracked changes, and `git ls-files --others --exclude-per-directory=.gitignore` for untracked files.
  - Which bytes a checkout of a commit gives is X54's: a runner refuses when a checkout would give other bytes than git stores (item 7), under that rule, not this one.
  - Write it in §8 "What a runner does" and the §9 row. In §5, after X02's recipe sentence on `UNDECLARED_READ`: "A file ignored by a committed `.gitignore` isn't pinned by the commit, so a recipe uses one only if it is part of the environment or made only from the files at the commit (a bytecode cache, say)." Regenerate `rules.json`.

**Decided** (Tom, 2026-11-04).
- What a change is: what `git status` reports, ignored files left out, less exemption (e): every record, and any run's result whose bytes match its record's SHA-256. Not taken: (a) all of every analysis's `.astra/records/` and `results/`, which lets a hand edit pass; (b) tracked changes only, which runs untracked helper code unpinned; (c) ignored files counting, which makes any build cache refuse the run; (d) only what this run writes, which forces a commit between runs. (e) keeps "itself" (a runner, not a hand), reverses nothing, and needs no commit between runs. X40's text cites (a); the decision is (e).
- Which ignore rules count: (i) committed `.gitignore` files only, the same on every machine. Not taken: (ii) `.git/info/exclude` and `core.excludesFile` too, which vary by machine.
- Where it is judged (item 17): in the checkout the recipe runs in. A runner can run in its own clean worktree at commit C while the main checkout is dirty, and place the results and records, which name C, in the main checkout, to be committed later. The conditions: the worktree is a checkout of C whose only changes are what the runner writes and puts in place; a result a recipe reads is its recorded bytes; the records are committed in a commit that has C in its history (X55). No RFC text; one design §11 line.

**Why this route.** The row restores RFC 0005 §3's "itself" and RFC 0007 §2 ("none of it is a change … that stops a runner from running") and §7 ("aren't a change that stops a runner from running"): just do. The checkout reading keeps RFC 0005 §3's rule, "Only committed code is run" (l.161): a clean checkout of C runs only C's code, wherever the author's edits are. RFC 0005's "while the repository has changes" (l.149) is one way to keep that rule, so Tom takes the reading without RFC text. Nothing it lets through goes unseen. A record names C, the code that ran. X55's rule keeps C in the history of the commit that holds the record. A checker on the dirty main checkout judges the result against the working tree (X53), so a result whose inputs or declaration have uncommitted edits there shows as stale. Under (e), a result put in the worktree for a recipe to read passes only with its recorded bytes. Defining a change goes beyond RFC 0005 §3's words, and only runners read it; design §12 defers runner semantics, so that half waits for the runner phase.

**Done when.** Now: `rules.json`'s `UNCOMMITTED_CHANGES` has the statement above with Source `0005 §3, 0007 §2, 0007 §7`; §8 says a recipe runs in a checkout of a commit, the working tree or one the runner makes; design §7 has the same row, §11 the checkout line, and §12 the runner-phase definition; `just test` passes. Runner phase: RFC 0005 §3, §8 and the row agree, and conformance fixtures cover an untracked helper (refuse), a file ignored only by `.git/info/exclude` (refuse), an ignored `__pycache__/` (run), an earlier run's record and result (run), a hand-edited result (refuse), an untracked file in a submodule (refuse), a record in a submodule analysis's `.astra/records/` (run), and a run in a clean worktree at HEAD while the main checkout has an untracked helper (run; its records name HEAD, and a checker on the main checkout reports stale a result whose input has an uncommitted edit there).

### X57. Bytes status judged only against a valid record

- **Resolves:** F046 · **Size:** S · **Depends on:** X08 (done) · **Decision:** decided · **Done (now part):** `f65d67a`

**The problem.** §8 'Statuses' defines the record status against "a valid record where its address says", and the bytes status against "its record's hash and size". §9's `present` row says "the record's `content_hash`", and `ABSENT_RESULT` says "Every result's recorded bytes are at its default place". None says whether a file at the result's record place that breaks `INVALID_RECORD` or `MISSING_RECORD_INPUTS` still counts as "its record" for the bytes. So a `missing` result has no settled bytes status (F046). One checker reports `missing`/`present`. Another reports `missing`/`absent`, and `ABSENT_RESULT` too.

X08 took `modified` out of the statuses: bytes are `present` or `absent`, and other bytes at the default place are `absent`. That removed one status, not this split. The rows X08 rewrote still say "the record" without saying which.

**The fix.**
- Now: design §12's check-phase items get "Bytes are judged only against a valid record", with the text below, as decided. Nothing goes in §13 (as X55 does for its decided rule).
- At the check phase:
  - Spec §8 'Statuses': "… and one for its bytes, `present` when the bytes at its default place have its record's hash and size, `absent` otherwise." becomes "… and one for its bytes, judged only against a valid record where its address says: `present` when the bytes at its default place have that record's hash and size, `absent` otherwise. A result with no valid record has nothing to match, so its bytes are `absent`." The sentences after it (`absent` is not an error; a tool may report more) stay.
  - Spec §9, Statuses table. `present`: "The result has a valid record where its address says, and the bytes at its default place hash to that record's `content_hash` and have its `size`". `absent`: "Anything else: no valid record, or anything other than the recorded bytes at the default place, nothing, a stand-in or other bytes included".
  - Spec §9 `ABSENT_RESULT`: "Every result with a valid record has its recorded bytes at its default place". It is then never reported for a `missing` result. The §9 `rules.json` excerpt quotes this row, so its `statement` changes with it (`tests/test_specification.py` checks the excerpt against the committed file).
  - Spec §9 `MISSING_RECORD`, `INVALID_RECORD`, `MISPLACED_RECORD`, `MISSING_RECORD_INPUTS`: Status when broken becomes "`missing`; bytes `absent`". The preface ("the status a result, or evidence citing it, gets when the rule doesn't hold") then stays true. A sentence after the Statuses table would say the same but not reach `rules.json`, so the cells carry it.
  - Design §7's check table follows §9.
  - Run `just gen-schema` and commit the regenerated `rules.json`.
- With X37, a record that breaks a parse or schema rule fails `INVALID_RECORD`, so its result is `missing`/`absent`. With X59 (item 6), so is a result whose record names a `spec_version` with no record schema.

**Decided** (Tom, 2026-11-04, as recommended). Option A: judge the bytes only against a valid record, as above. Every result with no valid record gets `missing`/`absent` and no bytes code. RFC 0005:199 leans towards A: it writes "no record, or a file breaking a rule of section 1 or 2", setting such a file apart from a record, but never says what its bytes status is. RFC 0007:130 ("Their content hash and size are checked against the record") leans the same way. Option B, rejected: judge the bytes against whatever file sits at the result's record place, valid or not. A record that lacks only a new input would then report `present`. But B has to say which broken files still give a usable hash (an unparsable one, one naming another result), and it trusts one field of a file the check layer rejects.

**Why this route.** RFC 0005 leans but doesn't settle the point, so there is no source text to restore, and the tidy-up RFC draft has no change on statuses. Only checkers' reports depend on it: nothing in the schema or validator phase reads a bytes status. The check phase writes the rest of §9's check table (X52, X54, X55), so these cells land with it.

**Done when.** Design §12 lists the decided point. At the check phase, a conformance fixture passes: a result whose record lacks an `inputs` item, with matching bytes, reports `missing`/`absent` and no `ABSENT_RESULT`. `rules.json` has the scoped `ABSENT_RESULT` statement, also in §9's excerpt, and `status` "`missing`; bytes `absent`" for the four record codes.

### X58. Check layer: say what a level means there (check phase)

- **Resolves:** F097 · **Size:** S · **Depends on:** none (X08 and X12 are done) · **Decision:** decided · **Done (now part):** `444152f`

*Updated 2026-11-04 (item 13): `modified` is no longer a status, so the stand-in `MAY` half of this entry is gone. X08 (887946f) removed `modified` from §8 Statuses and §9's statuses table and added no `MODIFIED_RESULT`; X46 (9b06dcb) left nothing about it. X08 also resolves F018: design §11 item 18 records that RFC 0005 §1's MUST on what a result's default place holds is broken as `absent` (`ABSENT_RESULT`). X12 (1084fe5) resolves F149: a `MAY` row is defined, and allowed only in the runner table. X02's §1 edit ("the runner's recommendation and permission") no longer merges anything from X58.*

**The problem.** §9's consequence table says what a level means in every layer but check. Its check row says "Nothing fails. The code is reported and **the result gets a status** …" and never says what a broken `MUST` or a broken `SHOULD` leads to. §8 Statuses says only "Since the bytes can be kept elsewhere, `absent` is not an error", which implies that `stale` and `missing` are errors, but §9 never says so. So `MISSING_RECORD` (`MUST`) and `ABSENT_RESULT` (`SHOULD`) differ only in their status, and two conforming checkers can report a `stale` result one as an error and the other as a warning (F097). Design §7's consequence table has the same check row.

The `MAY` facet of F097 is resolved: the statuses table has no keyword left, and a keyword scan outside rule rows and code fences finds only what §1 lists (the two recommendations in §7 and §8, and the runner's two duties after §9's runner table).

**The fix.** In the check phase:
- §9 consequence table, check row: "Nothing fails. A broken `MUST` is reported as an **error** and a broken `SHOULD` as a **warning**, and **the result gets a status**, given in the check table's extra column, *Status when broken*."
- §9, paragraph after the table, one sentence: "Whether a check run that reports an error exits with a failure is the tool's."
- §8 Statuses: "`absent` is not an error" becomes "`absent` is not an error: [`ABSENT_RESULT`](#check) is a `SHOULD`, reported as a warning."
- Design §7's consequence table and the paragraph after it match §9.
- No row's level changes, and `rules.json` doesn't change: a check code's severity follows its level, which `rules.json` already has.

Now, with the other check-phase notes: one design §12 bullet, "Check layer: a broken `MUST` is an error and a broken `SHOULD` a warning, and nothing fails (X58)".

**Decided** (Tom, 2026-11-04: as recommended, A). A broken `MUST` is reported as an error and a broken `SHOULD` as a warning, and nothing fails. `stale` and `missing` are then errors and `absent` is a warning, as RFC 0005's "`absent` (not an error …)" implies. Evidence citing a `stale` or `missing` result is an error through `STALE_EVIDENCE` (`MUST`). Exit codes are left to the tools. Not chosen: (B) check codes have no severity and each tool picks one, so checkers differ; (C) a broken `MUST` fails the check run with a non-zero exit.

**Why this route.** A matches the other layers and RFC 0005, and needs no new field in `rules.json`. It is checker and runner phase: only checkers read the check row, no row's level changes, and design §12 defers check conformance. It needs no tidy-up RFC text: it restates RFC 0005's "not an error" and adds no rule (item 15).

**Done when.** Design §12 has the bullet above. At the check phase: §9's check row and design §7's say what both levels mean, §8 Statuses links `absent` to `ABSENT_RESULT` as a warning, and `just gen-schema` leaves `rules.json` unchanged.

### X59. Versions: one schema per minor version, and a record of a version that has no record schema

- **Resolves:** F098 · **Size:** S · **Depends on:** X38, X37, X30 · **Decision:** decided · **Done (now part):** `3c5cf91`

**The problem.** `INVALID_RECORD` (§9 check) picks a record's schema by comparing versions: "the open variant when that version is newer than the checker's". But the specification defines no order on versions and gives a checker no version (F098). A string comparison puts `0.10.0` before `0.9.0`. RFC 0005 §2 and tidy-up change 22 say only "a later version". §1 leaves "which versions a schema accepts" to the versioning policy, so nothing says whether a 0.1.0 schema reads a `0.1.3` file. And nothing says what a checker does with a record of a smaller version that has no record schema, such as `0.0.9`. For `astra.yaml`, `UNSUPPORTED_SPEC_VERSION` covers that case. X03 rewrote §8 and the `INVALID_RECORD` row around planned results, but kept "newer".

**The fix.**
- §1 Versions, after the sentence on the form (the order and the minor version are X59's; X38 edits the same paragraph, and both land together): "Versions are ordered by MAJOR, then MINOR, then PATCH, each compared as an integer, so `0.10.0` comes after `0.9.0`. A version's minor version is its MAJOR and MINOR: `0.1` for `0.1.3`. Patch releases never change what is valid. Each minor version has one analysis schema and one record schema, and every `0.1.x` document is read by 0.1's." The last sentence of the paragraph becomes "What one minor version promises about another is left to ASTRA's versioning policy."
- X38's §1 validator sentence and its `UNSUPPORTED_SPEC_VERSION` row say "the schema of its minor version", not "that version's schema". A 0.1 validator then accepts every `0.1.x`.
- §2 checker bullet gains: "A checker implements one version, its own. It has the record schema of every minor version from 0.1 up to its own." Records begin at 0.1.0 (RFC 0005, tidy-up change 10), and no earlier version has a record schema. In v0.1 that is one schema, 0.1's. A checker gets an earlier minor version's schema from its published URL (X30's versioned `$id`s keep them apart). astra-spec ships only its own, with no `releases/` copies.
- §9 check, `INVALID_RECORD`, on X37's wording: "The record at each planned result's place breaks no parse rule, states a `spec_version` whose minor version has a record schema or is greater than the checker's, and breaks no schema rule against that minor version's record schema, or against the open variant when that minor version is greater than the checker's". Status and Source stay (`missing`; `0005 §2, tidy-up, §8`). No new code: a `0.0.9` record, or one of a smaller minor version never released, breaks `INVALID_RECORD`, so its result is `missing`.
- §8 "What a record holds", replacing the sentences from "A record holds only the fields" to "(§10)": "A record holds only the fields its `spec_version` defines, and is read by its minor version's record schema. A checker reading a record of a greater minor version than its own validates it against the open variant of the record schema, which ignores the fields the checker doesn't know, rather than calling the record `missing`. A record whose minor version is smaller and has no record schema, such as `0.0.9`, is `missing`. A record whose `spec_version` is missing or malformed is validated against the checker's own record schema, so every error is a reason ([`INVALID_RECORD`](#check), §10)."
- §10 "The open variant": "a record of a newer version than its own" becomes "a record of a greater minor version than its own".
- Design: D10 ("checkers reading newer records") and the §7 `INVALID_RECORD` row follow the spec. §8's Conventions line and §12's versioning-policy item say that patch releases never change what is valid, and that a checker has the record schema of every minor version from 0.1. §12 gains a later item: a release check that a patch release's schemas accept what its X.Y.0 accepts. §11 gains a line for the version rule.
- CLAUDE.md's release flow gains: "A patch release never changes what the schemas accept. A change to what is valid waits for the next minor version."
- The plan's For astra-tools bullet on `spec_version`s reads: a 0.1 validator accepts every `0.1.x`, and a checker keeps the record schema of every minor version from 0.1.
- No tidy-up text (item 15's scope). The spec's order settles change 22's "later".
- Run `just gen-schema` and commit `rules.json` with the new `INVALID_RECORD` statement.

**Decided.** (1b) No new code: a record whose `spec_version` has no record schema (below 0.1.0, or a minor version never released) breaks `INVALID_RECORD`, and its result is `missing`. `UNSUPPORTED_RECORD_VERSION` (1a) is dropped. A record of a greater minor version is read through the open variant, as now. (2i) is kept per minor version: one analysis schema and one record schema per minor version, since patch releases never change what is valid. A checker has the record schema of every minor version from 0.1 up to its own. astra-spec ships no per-release copies.

**Why this route.** Only checkers read records, and no validator rule does. One schema per minor version means a checker never needs a patch release's copy, and a version with no record schema is a broken record like any other. The minor-version rule also answers astra-tools' question of which `spec_version`s a validator accepts, so §1 lands with X38 now. The rest is text and lands with it. Nothing writes records yet, so nothing breaks.

**Done when.**
- §1 states the order, the minor version and that patch releases never change what is valid. §2 gives a checker its version and its schemas. `grep -n newer docs/specification.md` finds no comparison of versions.
- `just gen-schema` passes, and `rules.json` carries the new `INVALID_RECORD` statement.
- The design's D10, §7 row, §8, §11 and §12 match the spec, and CLAUDE.md states the patch-release rule.
- Check-phase conformance cases (design §12) cover: a `0.10.0` record read by a `0.9.x` checker (open variant); a `0.1.7` record read by a 0.1.0 checker (0.1's closed schema); a 0.1.0 record read by a later minor version's checker (0.1's closed schema); a `0.0.9` record (`INVALID_RECORD`, `missing`); and a record with no `spec_version` (`INVALID_RECORD`, with `MISSING_FIELD` as its reason).

## Not fully resolved

No fix in the plan fully resolves these findings:

- **F061** (Important): `Environment.iri`'s pattern accepts strings that aren't IRIs. Partly by X16, X47.
- **F140** (Minor): §3 never says how YAML integers and floats become JSON values, so `version: 2.0` reads as a type error but passes. Partly by X19. (X19: resolved here — integers and floats are JSON numbers typed as JSON Schema types them, so 2.0 is an integer. Left for X31: .inf, -.inf, .nan (no JSON value).)
